“I am so awfully sorry,” he began. “I thought I should enjoy having you here, but my nerves are all anyhow. The very sound of your voices. I can’t write a line. My brain reels. I wonder whether you’d be good enough to do a little thing for me? Would you mind escaping?”

“But won’t you get into trouble?”

“Nothing could be worse than this,” said the gaoler, with feeling. “I had no idea that children’s voices were so penetrating. Go, go. I implore you to escape. Only don’t tell the judge. I am sure he would never forgive me.”

===Chinese===
菲利普觉得这话可能是真的。真恼人，跟让人凭荣誉担保一样糟糕。

「烦死了！」他就说了这么一句。

接着狱卒进来了。他脸色发白，愁眉不展。

「实在对不住得很。」他开口道，「我本以为有你们在这儿，我会很快活，可我的神经全乱了套。光是你们说话的声音。我一个字也写不出来。我的脑子发晕。不知两位能不能赏脸帮我一个小忙？你们愿意越狱吗？」

「可您不会惹麻烦吗？」

「没什么比眼下更糟的了。」狱卒感慨地说。「我万万没想到，小孩子的声音这么扎耳朵。走吧，走吧。我求你们快逃。只是千万别告诉法官。我肯定他永远不会原谅我的。」

===Original===
After that, what prisoner would not immediately have escaped?

The two children only waited till the sound of the gaoler’s keys had died away on the stairs, to open their door, run down the many steps and slip out of the prison gate. They walked a little way in silence. There were plenty of people about, but no one seemed to notice them.

“Which way shall we go?” Lucy asked. “I wish we’d asked him where the Charitables live.”

“I think,” Philip began; but Lucy was not destined to know what he thought.

There was a sudden shout, a clattering of horses’ hoofs, and all the faces in the square turned their way.

“They’ve seen us,” cried Philip. “Run, run, run!”

===Chinese===
听了这话，哪个囚犯会不立马越狱呢？

两个孩子只等狱卒的钥匙声在楼梯上消逝，就打开房门，跑下许多级台阶，溜出了监狱大门。他们默默走了一小段路。到处是人，可谁也没留意他们。

「我们往哪边走？」露西问。「早知道问问他们，慈善会众人（the Charitables）住在哪儿就好了。」

「我想——」菲利普刚一开口；可露西注定听不到他在想什么了。

广场上忽然爆出一声呐喊，一阵马蹄的嘚嘚声，广场上所有的脸都朝他们转了过来。

「他们看见我们了！」菲利普喊道。「跑，跑，跑！」

===Original===
He himself ran, and he ran toward the gatehouse that stood at the top of the ladder stairs by which they had come up, and behind him came the shouting and clatter of hot pursuit. The captain stood in the gateway alone, and just as Philip reached the gate the captain turned into the guardroom and pretended not to see anything. Philip had never run so far or so fast. His breath came in deep sobs; but he reached the ladder and began quickly to go down. It was easier than going up.

He was nearly at the bottom when the whole ladder bridge leapt wildly into the air, and he fell from it and rolled in the thick grass of that illimitable prairie.

All about him the air was filled with great sounds, like the noise of the earthquakes that destroy beautiful big palaces, and factories which are big but not beautiful. It was deafening, it was endless, it was unbearable.

Yet he had to bear that, and more. And now he felt a curious swelling sensation in his hands, then in his head﻿—then all over. It was extremely painful. He rolled over in his agony, and saw the foot of an enormous giant quite close to him. The foot had a large, flat, ugly shoe, and seemed to come out of grey, low-hanging, swaying curtains. There was a gigantic column too, black against the grey. The ladder bridge, cast down, lay on the ground not far from him.

Pain and fear overcame Philip, and he ceased to hear or feel or know anything.

When he recovered consciousness he found himself under the table in the drawing-room. The swelling feeling was over, and he did not seem to be more than his proper size.

===Chinese===
他自己撒腿就跑，朝着梯桥顶上那座门房跑——他们就是打那儿上来的——身后跟着追捕的呐喊和热辣辣的嘚嘚声。卫队长独自站在门洞里，菲利普刚冲到门口，他就转身进了卫兵室，装作什么也没看见。菲利普从来没有跑得这么远、这么快过。他喘得胸部一起一伏；可他够到了梯子，飞快地往下爬。下比上容易。

他快要爬到底的时候，整座梯桥猛地腾空跃起，他从上面摔了下来，滚进那片一望无际的大草原的深草里。

他四周的空气里充满了巨大的声响，像地震摧毁又大又美的宫殿、又大却不美的工厂时的那种轰闹。震耳欲聋，没完没了，叫人没法忍受。

然而他必须受着，还得受更多。这时他感到手上有一种奇怪的胀大的感觉，接着是头上——然后是全身。疼极了。他在苦痛里翻滚，看见一只硕大无朋的巨人的脚，就在自己近旁。那只脚穿着一只又大又扁又难看的鞋，似乎从灰色低垂、摇摇晃晃的帷幕里伸出来。还有一根巨大的柱子，衬着灰色，黑黢黢的。梯桥被掀翻在地，躺在离他不远的地方。

疼痛和恐惧压倒了菲利普，他什么也听不见、感觉不到、不知道了。

等他恢复知觉，他发现自己正待在客厅的桌子底下。胀大的感觉没有了，他似乎也恢复了自己本来的尺寸。

===Original===
He could see the flat feet of the nurse and the lower part of her grey skirt, and a rattling and rumbling on the table above told him that she was doing as she had said she would, and destroying his city. He saw also a black column which was the leg of the table. Every now and then the nurse walked away to put back into its proper place something he had used in the building. And once she stood on a chair, and he heard the tinkling of the lustre-drops as she hooked them into their places on the chandelier.

“If I lie very still,” said he, “perhaps she won’t see me. But I do wonder how I got here. And what a dream to tell Helen about!”

He lay very still. The nurse did not see him. And when she had gone to her breakfast Philip crawled out.

Yes, the city was gone. Not a trace of it. The very tables were back in their proper places.

===Chinese===
他看得见保姆那双扁平的脚，和她灰裙子的下半截；头顶桌子上咔啦咔啦、轰隆轰隆的动静告诉他，她正在照她自己说过的那样，毁掉他的城。他还看见一根黑色的柱子，那是桌腿。保姆时不时走开，把一样他建城用过的东西放回原处。有一回她站上椅子，他听见吊灯水晶坠的叮当声，她正把它们一个个挂回吊灯上。

「我要是躺着一动不动，」他说，「说不定她看不见我。可我真是纳闷，我是怎么到这儿来的。这场梦，够讲给海伦听的了！」

他一动不动地躺着。保姆没有看见他。等她去吃早饭了，菲利普爬了出来。

是的，城没了。一点痕迹也没有。连桌子都放回了原位。

===Original===
Philip went back to his proper place, which, of course, was bed.

“What a splendid dream,” he said, as he cuddled down between the sheets, “and now it’s all over!”

Of course he was quite wrong.

===Chinese===
菲利普回到了他自己该在的地方——那当然是床上。

「多精彩的梦啊。」他蜷进被窝里说，「现在它全结束啦！」

当然，他完全想错了。

## III Lost / 迷失

===Original===
Philip went to sleep, and dreamed that he was at home again and that Helen had come to his bedside to call him, leading a white pony that was to be his very own. It was a pony that looked clever enough for anything, and he was not surprised when it shook hands with him; but when it said, “Well, we must be moving,” and began to try to put on Philip’s shoes and stockings, Philip called out, “Here, I say, stop that,” and awoke to a room full of sunshine, but empty of ponies.

“Oh, well,” said Philip, “I suppose I’d better get up.” He looked at his new silver watch, one of Helen’s parting presents, and saw that it marked ten o’clock.

“I say, you know,” said he to the watch, “you can’t be right.” And he shook it to encourage it to think over the matter. But the watch still said “ten” quite plainly and unmistakably.

===Chinese===
菲利普睡着了，梦见自己又回到了家，海伦来到他床边叫他起床，手里还牵着一匹要完全归他所有的白色小马。那小马看着聪明得干什么都行，它跟菲利普握手时，他一点也不惊讶；可当它开口说「好了，我们该动身了」，还开始试着替菲利普穿鞋穿袜时，菲利普喊了起来：「喂，我说，别闹了」，随即醒来，满屋阳光，却没有小马。

「哦，好吧，」菲利普说，「我看我最好起来。」他看了看那块新的银表——海伦临别送的礼物之一——表上指着十点。

「我说，你可知道，」他对表说，「你不可能是对的。」他晃了晃它，鼓励它把这事再好好想想。可表还是清清楚楚、明明白白地说「十点」。

===Original===
Now the Grange breakfast time was at eight. And Philip was certain he had not been called.

“This is jolly rum,” he remarked. “It must be the watch. Perhaps it’s stopped.”

But it hadn’t stopped. Therefore it must be two hours past breakfast time. The moment he had thought this he became extremely hungry. He got out of bed as soon as he knew exactly how hungry he was.

There was no one about, so he made his way to the bathroom and spent a happy hour with the hot water and the cold water, and the brown Windsor soap and the shaving soap and the nail brush and the flesh brush and the loofahs and the shower bath and the three sponges. He had not, so far, been able thoroughly to investigate and enjoy all these things. But now there was no one to interfere, and he enjoyed himself to that degree that he quite forgot to wonder why he hadn’t been called. He thought of a piece of poetry that Helen had made for him, about the bath; and when he had done playing he lay on his back in water that was very hot indeed, trying to remember the poetry. The water was very nearly cold by the time he had remembered the poetry. It was called Dreams of a Giant Life, and this was it.

===Chinese===
要知道，格兰奇庄园的早饭时间是八点。而菲利普确定，没有人来叫过他。

「这事可真够邪门的。」他评论道。「准是表的问题。说不定它停了。」

可它没停。那么，现在必定已经过了早饭时间整整两个钟头。他一想到这一点，立刻饿得不行。等他弄清了自己到底有多饿，他马上就下了床。

四下没有一个人，于是他自己摸到浴室，跟热水和冷水、棕色温莎皂和剃须皂、指甲刷和身刷、丝瓜络、淋浴和三块海绵，痛痛快快地消磨了一个钟头。到今天为止，他一直没能把这些东西痛痛快快地研究个遍、享受个够。可现在没有人来管，他玩得那么尽兴，把「为什么没人叫我」这回事忘了个一干二净。他想起海伦给他编的一首诗，是关于洗澡的；玩够了以后，他躺在确实非常热的水里，仰面朝天，试着把那首诗回忆起来。等他把诗全想起来，水已经快凉透了。那首诗叫《巨人生活之梦》（Dreams of a Giant Life），是这么写的：

===Original===
> What was I once-in ages long ago?
 

 I look back, and I see myself. We grow
 

 So changed through changing years, I hardly see
 

 How that which I look back on could be me?1
> Glorious and splendid, giant-like I stood
 

 On a white cliff, topped by a darkling wood.
 

 Below me, placid, bright and sparkling, lay
 

 The equal waters of a lovely bay.
 

 White cliffs surrounded it﻿—and calm and fair
 

 It lay asleep, in warm and silent air.

===Chinese===
> 许久许久以前，我究竟是什么？

 



 我回望，我看见我自己。岁月蹉跎，

 



 我们变到面目全非，我简直看不出，

 



 我回头望见的那个，怎么会是我？1

> 辉煌壮丽，巨人一般，我伫立

 



 在一座白色的悬崖上，崖顶幽林蒙翳。

 



 我的脚下，安详、明净、波光闪闪，

 



 躺着一湾可爱海水平平的港湾。

 



 白色的悬崖四面环抱——安谧、明妍，

 



 它酣睡着，在温暖无声的空气里边。

===Original===
> I stood alone-naked and strong, upright
 

 My limbs gleamed in the clear pure golden light.
 

 I saw below me all the water lie
 

 Expecting something, and that thing was I.2
> I leaned, I plunged, the waves splashed over me.
 

 I lay, a giant in a little sea.

===Chinese===
> 我独自伫立——赤裸、强壮、挺直，

 



 我的四肢在纯净的金光里闪熠。

 



 我看见脚下的海水静静躺卧，

 



 盼望着什么，而那什么正是我。2

> 我俯身，我跃入，浪花溅过我的身。

 



 我躺下了，一个小海洋里的巨人。

===Original===
> White cliffs all round, wood-crowned, and as I lay
 

 I saw the glories of the dying day;
 

 No wind disturbed my sea; the sunlight was
 

 As though it came through windows of gold glass.
 

 The white clifis rose above me, and around
 

 The clear sea lay, pure, perfect and profound;
 

 And I was master of the cliffs, the sea,
 

 And the gold light that brightened over me.

===Chinese===
> 白崖四面，林影罩顶，我躺卧着

 



 看垂死白日的种种壮丽颜色；

 



 没有风搅扰我的海；那片阳光

 



 仿佛透过黄金的玻璃窗。

 



 白色的悬崖在我头顶高耸，四周

 



 清澈的海躺着，纯净、完美、深幽；

 



 而我是悬崖的主人，海的主人，

 



 也是那照亮我的金光的主人。
===Original===
> Far miles away my giant feet showed plain,
 

 Rising, like rocks out of the quiet main.
 

 On them a lighthouse could be built, to show
 

 Wayfaring ships the way they must not go.
> I was the master of that cliff-girt sea.
 

 I splashed my hands, the waves went over me,
 

 And in the dimples of my body lay
 

 Little rock-pools, where small sea-beasts might play.

===Chinese===
> 远处好几里外，我巨人的双脚露出水面，

 



 像礁石一般，从平静的大海里浮现。

 



 大可以在上面造一座灯塔，好告诉

 



 过路的船只哪条航路万万走不得。

> 我是那悬崖环抱的海的主人。

 



 我拍打双手，波浪漫过我的周身，

 



 在我身上的酒窝里，一汪又一汪，

 



 是小小的石潮池，海中小兽可在池里游荡。

===Original===
> I found a boat, its deck was perforate;
 

 I launched it, and it dared the storms of fate.
 

 Its woollen sail stood out against the sky;
 

 Supported by a mast of ivory.
> Another boat rode proudly to my hand,
 

 Upon its deck a thousand spears did stand;
 

 I launched it, and it sped full fierce and fast
 

 Against the boat that had the ivory mast
 

 And woollen sail and perforated deck.
 

 The two went down in one stupendous wreck!

===Chinese===
> 我找到一条船，它的甲板满是窟窿；

 



 我放它下水，它就敢闯命运的暴风。

 



 它的绒线帆迎着天空鼓胀；

 



 支撑它的是一根象牙的桅樯。

> 又一条船神气地驶到我手边，

 



 它的甲板上立着一千杆长矛尖；

 



 我放它下水，它又凶又猛地飞驰，

 



 直扑那条有着象牙桅樯、

 



 绒线帆、漏了底的甲板的船。

 



 两条船一起沉没，一场了不得的海难！

===Original===
> Beneath the waves I chased with joyous hand U
 

 pon the bed of an imagined sand
 

 The slippery brown sea mouse, that still escaped,
 

 Where the deep cave beneath my knee was shaped.
 

 Caught it at last and caged it into rest
 

 Upon the shallows of my submerged breast.
> Then, as I lay, wrapped as in some kind arm
 

 By the sweet world of waters soft and warm,
 

 A great voice cried, from some far unseen shore,
 

 And I was not a giant any more.

===Chinese===
> 波浪底下，我挥着欢快的手，追猎

 



 那滑溜溜的棕色海鼠，在想象的海沙床上，

 



 它一次次逃脱，逃向

 



 我膝盖底下那个深洞的暗巢。

 



 终于捉住了它，把它圈起来安顿

 



 在我没入水中的胸脯的浅滩上。

> 后来，我躺着，仿佛被一条温柔的臂膀

 



 搂着，被那温软的甜蜜水乡，

 



 一个洪亮的声音喊起来，从远处看不见的岸——

 



 我就再也不是巨人了。

===Original===
> “Come out, come out,” cried out the voice of power,
 

 “You’ve been in for a quarter of an hour.
 

 The water’s cold﻿—come, Master Pip﻿—your head
 

 ’S all wet, and it is time you were in bed.”
> I rose all dripping from the magic sea
 

 And left the ships that had been slaves to me﻿—
 

 The soap-dish, with its perforated deck,
 

 The nail-brush, that had rushed to loss and wreck,
 

 The flannel sail, the tooth-brush that was mast,
 

 The sleek soap-mouse﻿—I left them all at last.
> I went out of that magic sea and cried
 

 Because the time came when I must be dried
 

 And leave the splendour of a giant’s joy
 

 And go to bed﻿—a little well-washed boy.

===Chinese===
> 「出来吧，出来吧，」那威严的声音喊道，

 



 「你泡在里面已经一刻钟了。

 



 水凉了——来吧，皮普少爷——你的头

 



 全湿了，你该上床睡觉了。」

> 我从那魔法之海里滴着水站起，

 



 撇下了那些曾听命于我的船只——

 



 肥皂盒，顶着满是窟窿的甲板，

 



 指甲刷，冲向了损失和沉翻，

 



 法兰绒的帆，充作桅杆的牙刷，

 



 油光水滑的肥皂鼠——我到底撇下了它们。

> 我走出那魔法之海，哭了一场，

 



 因为到时候了，我得让人擦干，

 



 丢下巨人欢畅的荣光，

 



 上床去——做一个洗得干干净净的小男孩。

===Original===
When he had quite remembered the poetry he had another shower-bath, and then when he had enjoyed the hot rough towels out of the hot cupboard he went back to his room to dress. He now felt how deeply he wanted his breakfast, so he dressed himself with all possible speed, even forgetting to fasten his bootlaces properly. He was in such a hurry that he dropped his collar-stud, and it was as he stooped to pick it up that he remembered his dream. Do you know that was really the first time he had thought of it. The dream﻿—that indeed would be something to think about.

Breakfast was the really important thing. He went down very hungry indeed. “I shall ask for my breakfast directly I get down,” he said. “I shall ask the first person I meet.” And he met no one.

There was no one on the stairs, or in the hall, or in the dining-room, or in the drawing-room. The library and billiard-room were empty of living people, and the door of the nursery was locked. So then Philip made his way into the regions beyond the baize door, where the servants’ quarters were. And there was no one in the kitchen, or in the servants’ hall, or in the butler’s pantry, or in the scullery, or the washhouse, or the larder. In all that big house, and it was much bigger than it looked from the front because of the long wings that ran out on each side of its back﻿—in all that big house there was no one but Philip. He felt certain of this before he ran upstairs and looked in all the bedrooms and in the little picture gallery and the music-room, and then in the servants’ bedrooms and the very attics. There were interesting things in those attics, but Philip only remembered that afterwards. Now he tore down the stairs three at a time. All the room doors were open as he had left them, and somehow those open doors frightened him more than anything else. He ran along the corridors, down more stairs, past more open doors and out through the back kitchen, along the moss-grown walk by the brick wall and so round by the three yew trees and the mounting block to the stable-yard. And there was no one there. Neither coachman nor groom nor stable-boys. And there was no one in the stables, or the coach-house, or the harness-room, or the loft.

===Chinese===
他把诗完全想起来之后，又冲了个淋浴，然后从热烘柜里取出热乎乎的粗毛巾享受了一番，回房去穿衣裳。这时他才感到自己有多想吃早饭，于是以最快的速度穿戴起来，连靴带都没顾上系好。他慌慌张张的，领扣掉了，就在他弯腰去捡的时候，他想起了自己的梦。你可知道，那真是他头一回想起它。那个梦——那可真是一件值得好好想想的事。

早饭才是真正要紧的事。他下楼的时候实在饿得很。「我一下楼就要我的早饭。」他说。「我要向碰见的第一个人要。」结果他一个人也没碰见。

楼梯上没有人，大厅里没有人，饭厅里没有人，客厅里也没有人。图书室和台球房里空无一人，育儿室的门锁着。于是菲利普穿过绿呢门，摸进了仆人住的那一带。厨房里没有人，仆人厅里没有人，管家配膳室里没有人，洗碗室、洗衣房、食品间，全都没有人。那么大一座宅子——因为背后两翼各伸出长长的侧楼，它比从正面看起来要大得多——那么大一座宅子里，除了菲利普再没有一个人。还没等他跑上楼，把所有卧室、小画廊和音乐室挨个看遍，再看仆人卧室和顶楼的阁楼间，他就已经确信这一点了。那些阁楼里有些很有意思的东西，不过菲利普是后来才想起来的。此刻他三级一跨地冲下楼梯。所有房门都像他离开时那样敞着，不知怎么，那些敞开的门比什么都吓人。他沿着走廊跑，又下了几道楼梯，经过更多敞开的门，穿过后厨房，沿砖墙边长满青苔的小径，绕过三棵紫杉和上马石，跑到马厩院子。那里也没有人。车夫没有，马夫没有，马童也没有。马厩里、车房里、马具房里、草料阁上，都没有人。

===Original===
Philip felt that he could not go back into the house. Something terrible must have happened. Was it possible that anyone could want the Grange servants enough to kidnap them? Philip thought of the nurse and felt that, at least as far as she was concerned, it was *not* possible. Or perhaps it was magic! A sort of Sleeping-Beauty happening! Only everyone had vanished instead of just being put to sleep for a hundred years.

He was alone in the middle of the stable-yard when the thought came to him.

“Perhaps they’re only made invisible. Perhaps they’re all here and watching me and making fun of me.”

He stood still to think this. It was not a pleasant thought.

===Chinese===
菲利普觉得自己回不进那座房子了。一定出了什么可怕的事。难道会有人那么想要格兰奇庄园的仆人，把他们统统绑了票？菲利普想起保姆，觉得至少就她而言，这*不*可能。要么，这是魔法！一场睡美人式的事件！只不过不是人人睡上一百年，而是人人凭空消失了。

他独自站在马厩院子中央，这时一个念头冒了出来。

「说不定他们只是被变隐形了。说不定他们全都在这儿，看着我，拿我开心呢。」

他站着不动，琢磨这个念头。这念头可不怎么痛快。
===Original===
Suddenly he straightened his little back, and threw back his head.

“They shan’t see I’m frightened anyway,” he told himself. And then he remembered the larder.

“I haven’t had any breakfast,” he explained aloud, so as to be plainly heard by any invisible people who might be about. “I ought to have my breakfast. If nobody gives it to me I shall take my breakfast.”

He waited for an answer. But none came. It was very quiet in the stable-yard. Only the rattle of a halter ring against a manger, the sound of a hoof on stable stones, the cooing of pigeons and the rustle of straw in the loose-box broke the silence.

“Very well,” said Philip. “I don’t know what *you* think I ought to have for breakfast, so I shall take what *I* think.”

===Chinese===
忽然，他挺直了小小的背，昂起头来。

「反正，他们休想看出我害怕。」他对自己说。接着，他想起了食品间。

「我还没吃早饭呢。」他大声解释，好让附近可能潜伏的隐形人听得清清楚楚。「我应该吃早饭。要是没人给我，我就自己拿了。」

他等着回答。可没有回答。马厩院里静得很。只有缰绳环磕在马槽上的咔啦声、蹄子踏在马厩石板上的声音、鸽子咕咕的叫和软棚里干草的窸窣，打破了寂静。

「很好。」菲利普说。「既然我不知道*你们*认为我早饭该吃什么，那我就拿*我认为*该吃的。」

===Original===
He drew a long breath, trying to draw courage in with it, threw back his shoulders more soldierly than ever, and marched in through the back door and straight to the larder. Then he took what he thought he ought to have for breakfast. This is what he thought:

- 1 cherry pie,

- 2 custards in cups,

- 1 cold sausage,

- 2 pieces of cold toast,

- 1 piece of cheese,

- 2 lemon cheesecakes,

- 1 small jam tart (there was only one left),

- Butter, 1 pat.

“What jolly things the servants have to eat,” he said. “I never knew. I thought that nothing but mutton and rice grew here.”

He put all the food on a silver tray and carried it out on to the terrace, which lies between the two wings at the back of the house. Then he went back for milk, but there was none to be seen so he got a white jug full of water. The spoons he couldn’t find, but he found a carving-fork and a fish-slice. Did you ever try to eat cherry pie with a fish-slice?

===Chinese===
他深深吸了口气，想连勇气一起吸进来，肩膀比先前更像个军人地向后一甩，从后门 march 进去，径直奔向食品间。然后他拿了自己认为早饭该吃的东西。他是这么认为的：

- 1 个樱桃派，
- 2 杯杯装蛋奶冻，
- 1 根冷香肠，
- 2 片冷吐司，
- 1 块奶酪，
- 2 个柠檬奶酪蛋糕，
- 1 个小果酱挞（只剩一个了），
- 黄油，1 小块。

「仆人们吃的东西真够丰盛的。」他说。「我以前都不知道。我还以为这儿只长羊肉和大米呢。」

他把所有吃的摆上一只银托盘，端到外头的平台上——平台在宅子背后两翼之间。然后他回去拿牛奶，可是一滴也找不见，于是他灌了满满一白壶水。勺子找不着，但他找到一把切肉叉和一把鱼刀。你试过用鱼刀吃樱桃派吗？

===Original===
“Whatever’s happened,” said Philip to himself, through the cherry pie, “and whatever happens it’s as well to have had your breakfast.” And he bit a generous inch off the cold sausage which he had speared with the carving-fork.

And now, sitting out in the good sunshine, and growing less and less hungry as he plied fish-slice and carving-fork, his mind went back to his dream, which began to seem more and more real. Suppose it really *had* happened? It might have; magic things did happen, it seemed. Look how all the people had vanished out of the house﻿—out of the world too, perhaps.

“Suppose everyone’s vanished,” said Philip. “Suppose I’m the only person left in the world who hasn’t vanished. Then everything in the world would belong to me. Then I could have everything that’s in all the toy shops.” And his mind for a moment dwelt fondly on this beautiful idea.

Then he went on. “But suppose I vanished too? Perhaps if I were to vanish I could see the other people who have. I wonder how it’s done.”

He held his breath and tried hard to vanish. Have you ever tried this? It is not at all easy to do. Philip could not do it at all. He held his breath and he tried and he tried, but he only felt fatter and fatter and more and more as though in one more moment he should burst. So he let his breath go.

===Chinese===
「不管出了什么事，」菲利普一边嚼着樱桃派一边对自己说，「也不管往后还要出什么事，先吃好早饭总是没错的。」他从叉在切肉叉上的冷香肠上，咬下了足足一英寸的一大口。

这时，他坐在好好的太阳地里，鱼刀和切肉叉越使越起劲，肚子越来越不饿，心思却回到那个梦里去了，那梦显得越来越真。要是它真的*发生过*呢？说不定真发生过；魔法事儿看来是会发生的。瞧瞧，全家的人怎么从宅子里消失了——说不定也从这世界上消失了。

「假定大家都消失了，」菲利普说，「假定我是世界上唯一没消失的人。那世界上的一切就都归我了。那我就能把所有玩具店里的东西统统拿到手。」他的心思在这美妙的念头上温柔地流连了一会儿。

接着他接着往下想。「可要是我也消失了呢？说不定我一消失，就能看见那些已经消失的人了。我倒想知道这是怎么弄的。」

他屏住呼吸，拼命想让自己消失。你试过这个吗？一点也不容易。菲利普压根儿办不到。他屏住气，试了又试，可只觉得自己越来越鼓、越来越胀，仿佛再过一刻就要炸开了。于是他把气放了。

===Original===
“No,” he said, looking at his hands; “I’m not any more invisible than I was before. Not so much I think,” he added thoughtfully, looking at what was left of the cherry pie. “But that dream﻿—”

He plunged deep in the remembrance of it that was, to him, like swimming in the waters of a fairy lake.

He was hooked out of his lake suddenly by voices. It was like waking up. There, away across the green park beyond the sunk fence, were people coming.

“So everyone hasn’t vanished,” he said, caught up the tray and took it in. He hid it under the pantry shelf. He didn’t know who the people were who were coming and you can’t be too careful. Then he went out and made himself small in the shadow of a red buttress, heard their voices coming nearer and nearer. They were all talking at once, in that quick interested way that makes you certain something unusual has happened.

He could not hear exactly what they were saying, but he caught the words: “No.”

===Chinese===
「不行。」他看着自己的手说；「我不比刚才更隐形。我看反倒不如刚才了。」他又若有所思地补了一句，眼睛瞧着樱桃派剩下的部分。「可是那个梦——」

他一头扎进对它的追忆里，那对他就像在仙湖的水里游泳。

突然，说话声把他从湖里钩了出来，像猛地惊醒一样。瞧，隐蔽矮栅那边远远的绿园子里，有人来了。

「这么说，不是所有人都消失了。」他说着，抓起托盘端了进去，把它藏到配膳室的架子底下。他不知道来的是什么人，小心总没大错。然后他出去，把自己缩在红砖扶壁的影子里，听见他们的声音越来越近。他们七嘴八舌一齐说个不停，那副急切起劲的样子，让你断定出了什么不寻常的事。

===Original===
“Of course I’ve asked.”

“Police.”

“Telegram.”

“Yes, of course.”

“Better make quite sure.”

Then everyone began speaking all at once, and you could not hear anything that anybody said. Philip was too busy keeping behind the buttress to see who they were who were talking. He was glad *something* had happened.

===Chinese===
他们说的确切听不清，但他捕到了这几个词：「不行。」

「我当然问过了。」

「警察。」

「电报。」

「是的，当然。」

「最好弄确实了。」接着大家又同时说起话来，谁说了什么一句也听不清。菲利普忙着躲在扶壁后面，没看清说话的都是谁。他很高兴，总算*出了点事*。

===Original===
“Now I shall have something to think about besides the nurse and my beautiful city that she has pulled down.”

But what was it that had happened? He hoped nobody was hurt﻿—or had done anything wrong. The word police had always made him uncomfortable ever since he had seen a boy no bigger than himself pulled along the road by a very large policeman. The boy had stolen a loaf, Philip was told. Philip could never forget that boy’s face; he always thought of it in church when it said “prisoners and captives,” and still more when it said “desolate and oppressed.”

“I do hope it’s not *that*,” he said.

And slowly he got himself to leave the shelter of the redbrick buttress and to follow to the house those voices and those footsteps that had gone by him.

He followed the sound of them to the kitchen. The cook was there in tears and a Windsor armchair. The kitchenmaid, her cap all on one side, was crying down most dirty cheeks. The coachman was there, very red in the face, and the groom, without his gaiters. The nurse was there, neat as ever she seemed at first, but Philip was delighted when a more careful inspection showed him that there was mud on her large shoes and on the bottom of her skirt, and that her dress had a large three-cornered tear in it.

===Chinese===
「这下好了，除了保姆和她拆掉的我那座美丽的城，我总算有别的事可想了。」

可出的是什么事呢？他希望没人受伤——也没人干坏事。「警察」这个词，自从他见过一个不比他大的男孩，被一个块头很大的警察拖着在大路上走，就总让他浑身不自在。人家告诉他，那男孩偷了一个面包。菲利普永远忘不掉那男孩的脸；教堂里念到「囚徒和俘虏」时他总会想起它，念到「孤苦和受压迫的人」时想得更多。

「我千万希望不是*那种事*。」他说。

他磨磨蹭蹭，终于让自己离开红砖扶壁的庇护，跟着那些打他身边经过的声音和脚步，朝宅子走去。

他跟着声音来到厨房。厨娘正在那里，一把眼泪一把鼻涕地坐在一把温莎扶手椅里。厨房女仆的帽子歪在一边，正顺着脏兮兮的脸颊往下哭。车夫在那儿，脸红得厉害；马夫也在，连绑腿都没打。保姆在那儿，乍看还是那么整洁，可菲利普仔细一瞧，高兴地发现：她那双大鞋上、裙子下摆上全是泥，衣裳上还撕开了一个三角的大口子。

===Original===
“I wouldn’t have had it happen for a twenty-pun note,” the coachman was saying.

“George,” said the nurse to the groom, “you go and get a horse ready. I’ll write the telegram.”

“You’d best take Peppermint,” said the coachman. “She’s the fastest.”

The groom went out, saying under his breath, “Teach your grandmother,” which Philip thought rude and unmeaning.

Philip was standing unnoticed by the door. He felt that thrill﻿—if it isn’t pleasure it is more like it than anything else﻿—which we all feel when something real has happened.

===Chinese===
「给我二十镑的票子，我也不乐意出这种事。」车夫正说着。

「乔治，」保姆对马夫说，「你去备一匹马。我来写电报。」

「你顶好骑薄荷糖（Peppermint），」车夫说，「她跑得最快。」

马夫往外走，压着嗓子嘀咕了一句「用得着教你的奶奶」，菲利普觉得这话又没礼貌，又不知所谓。

菲利普站在门边，没人留意他。他感到那种激动——就算算不上快活，也比什么都更像快活——出了真正的大事时，我们人人都会有的那种激动。

===Original===
But what *had* happened. What?

“I wish I’d never come back,” said the nurse. “Then nobody could pretend it was *my* fault.”

“It don’t matter what they pretend,” the cook stopped crying to say. “The thing is what’s happened. Oh, my goodness. I’d rather have been turned away without a character than have had this happen.”

“And I’d rather *any*thing,” said the nurse. “Oh, my goodness me. I wish I’d never been born.”

And then and there, before the astonished eyes of Philip, she began to behave as any nice person might﻿—she began to cry.

===Chinese===
可到底出了*什么*事？什么？

「我要是没回来就好了。」保姆说。「那就谁也没法装作是*我的*错了。」

「他们装作什么不要紧，」厨娘停了哭，说，「要紧的是出了什么事。哦，我的老天。宁可让我两手空空没张操行证明就卷铺盖，也别出这种事。」

「我宁肯*怎么都行*。」保姆说。「哦，我的老天爷。我情愿没生下来过。」

就在当时当地，当着菲利普吃惊的眼睛，她做出了任何一个体面人都可能做的事——她哭起来了。

===Original===
“It wouldn’t have happened,” said the cook, “if the master hadn’t been away. He’s a Justice of the Peace, he is, and a terror to gipsies. It wouldn’t never have happened if﻿—”

Philip could not bear it any longer.

“*What* wouldn’t have happened if?” he asked, startling everybody to a quick jump of surprise.

The nurse stopped crying and turned to look at him.

“Oh, *you*!” she said slowly. “I forgot *you*. You want your breakfast, I suppose, no matter what’s happened?”

“No, I don’t,” said Philip, with extreme truth. “I want to know what *has* happened?”

===Chinese===
「要是主人没出门，就不会出这事。」厨娘说，「他是个治安官，他这个人，吉卜赛人见了他都怕。要不是——」

菲利普再也受不了了。

「*什么*不会出事，要是怎么？」他问，把大家吓得齐齐一跳。

保姆停了哭，转过来看他。

「哦，是*你*！」她慢慢地说。「我把你给忘了。我猜，不管出了什么事，你总归是要你的早饭的吧？」

「不，我不要。」菲利普说得千真万确。「我想知道，到底*出了*什么事？」

===Original===
“Miss Lucy’s lost,” said the cook heavily, “that’s what’s happened. So now you know. You run along and play, like a good little boy, and don’t make extry trouble for us in the trouble we’re in.”

“Lost?” repeated Philip.

“Yes, lost. I expect you’re glad,” said the nurse, “the way you treated her. You hold your tongue and don’t let me so much as hear you breathe the next twenty-four hours. I’ll go and write that telegram.”

Philip thought it best not to let anyone hear him breathe. By this means he heard the telegram when nurse read it aloud to the cook.

===Chinese===
「露西小姐丢了，」厨娘沉沉地说，「就是这么回事。这下你知道了。乖乖跑去玩吧，好孩子，别在我们正烦的时候再给我们添乱了。」

「丢了？」菲利普重复道。

「对，丢了。我料想你巴不得呢，」保姆说，「瞧你待她的那副样子。你给我闭嘴，往后二十四个钟头，别让我听见你出一点声儿。我去写电报。」

菲利普觉得，最好别让任何人听见他出气。这么一来，保姆把电报念给厨娘听的时候，他倒听了个正着。
===Original===
> “Miss Lucy lost. Please come home immediately.
> “Philkins.

“I don’t see why you sign it Philkins. You’re only the nurse﻿—I’m the head of the house when the family’s away, and my name’s Bobson,” the cook said.

===Chinese===
> 「露西小姐失踪。请即刻回家。

> 「菲尔金斯。

「我不明白你干吗署名菲尔金斯。你不过是个保姆——主人一家不在时，宅子里当家的可是我，我姓博布森。」厨娘说。

===Original===
There was a sound of torn paper.

“There﻿—the paper’s tore. I’d just as soon your name went to it,” said the nurse. “I don’t want to be the one to tell such news.”

“Oh, my good gracious, what a thing to happen,” sighed the cook. “Poor little darling!”

Then somebody wrote the telegram again, and the nurse took it out to the stable-yard, where Peppermint was already saddled.

===Chinese===
传来撕纸的声音。

「喏——纸撕破了。署你的名我也不嫌。」保姆说。「这种消息，我才不乐意由我来讲呢。」

「哦，我的老天爷，怎么偏出这种事。」厨娘叹道。「可怜的小宝贝！」

然后有人把电报重写了一遍，保姆把它送到马厩院去，薄荷糖已经备好了鞍。

===Original===
“I thought,” said Philip, bold in the nurse’s absence, “I thought Lucy was with her aunt.”

“She came back yesterday,” said the cook. “Yes, after you’d gone to bed. And this morning that nurse went into the night nursery and she wasn’t there. Her bed all empty and cold, and her clothes gone. Though how the gipsies could have got in without waking that nurse is a mystery to me and ever will be. She must sleep like a pig.”

“Or the seven sleepers,” said the coachman.

“But what would gipsies want her *for*?” Philip asked.

“What do they ever want anybody for?” retorted the cook. “Look at the heirs that’s been stolen. I don’t suppose there’s a titled family in England but what’s had its heir stolen, one time and another.”

===Chinese===
「我还以为，」菲利普趁保姆不在，胆子大了些，「我还以为露西在她姑妈家呢。」

「她昨天回来了。」厨娘说。「对，你上床睡了以后回来的。今天早上，那位保姆进了夜间育儿室，人不见了。床空着，凉冰冰的，衣裳也不见了。只是，吉卜赛人怎么进得来还不吵醒那位保姆，这对我是个谜，永远是个谜。她准是睡得像头猪。」

「要么像那七个睡人。」车夫说。

「可吉卜赛人要她*干什么*？」菲利普问。

「他们要什么人不是老一套？」厨娘回敬道。「想想那些被偷走的继承人吧。依我看，英格兰但凡有个头衔的人家，就没有哪家没丢过继承人，不是这回就是那回。」

===Original===
“I suppose you’ve looked all over the house,” said Philip.

“I suppose we ain’t deaf and dumb and blind and silly,” said the cook. “Here’s that nurse. You be off, Mr. Philip, without you want a flea in your ear.”

And Philip, at the word, *was* off. He went into the long drawing-room, and shut the door. Then he got the ivory chessmen out of the Buhl cabinet, and set them out on that delightful chess-table whose chequers are of mother-of-pearl and ivory, and tried to play a game, right hand against left. But right hand, who was white, and so moved first, always won. He gave up after awhile, and put the chessmen away in their proper places. Then he got out the big book of photographs of pictures, but they did not seem interesting, so he tried the ivory spellicans. But his hand shook, and you know spellicans is a game you can’t play when your hand shakes. And all the time, behind the chess and the pictures and the spellicans, he was trying not to think about his dream, about how he had climbed that ladder stair, which was really the yardstick, and gone into the cities that he had built on the tables. Somehow he did not want to remember it. The very idea of remembering made him feel guilty and wretched.

===Chinese===
「我猜，你们把整座宅子都找遍了。」菲利普说。

「我猜，我们不聋、不哑、不瞎、也不傻。」厨娘说。「那位保姆来了。你快走，菲利普少爷，省得讨一顿剋。」

菲利普一听这话，*当真*走了。他进了长客厅，关上门。然后他从布尔柜里取出象牙棋子，摆在那张可爱的棋桌上——棋盘的格子是珍珠母和象牙的——试着下了一盘，右手对左手。可右手执白先行，回回都赢。下了一会儿他就不下了，把棋子一一放回原位。然后他拿出那本大幅照片画册，可看着没意思，于是他玩象牙挑签。可他的手直发抖，你知道的，挑签这游戏，手一抖就没法玩。而在下棋、看画册、玩挑签的整个工夫，他一直拼命不去想那个梦，不去想自己怎么爬上那道梯桥——其实就是那把码尺——走进了他搭在桌子上的那些城里。不知怎么，他不愿想起它。光是「想起来」这个念头，就让他觉得又心虚又难受。

===Original===
He went and looked out of the window, and as he stood there his wish not to remember the dream made his boots restless, and in their shuffling his right boot kicked against something hard that lay in the folds of the blue brocade curtain.

He looked down, stooped, and picked up little Mr. Noah. The nurse must have dropped it there when she cleared away the city.

And as he looked upon those wooden features it suddenly became impossible not to think of the dream. He let the remembrance of it come, and it came in a flood. And with it the remembrance of what he had done. He had promised to be Lucy’s noble friend, and they had run together to escape from the galloping soldiers. And he had run faster than she. And at the top of the ladder﻿—the ladder of safety﻿—*he had not waited for her*.

“Any old hero would have waited for her, and let her go first,” he told himself. “Any gentleman would﻿—even any *man*﻿—let alone a hero. And I just bunked down the ladder and forgot her. I *left* her there.”

===Chinese===
他走到窗前望出去。站在那里，那个不愿想起梦的念头让他的靴子不安分起来，蹭来蹭去，右靴一下子踢到了什么硬东西，就在蓝锦缎窗帘的褶子里。

他低头一看，弯腰捡起了小小的诺亚先生。准是保姆收走那座城的时候，把他掉在那儿的。

他望着那张木头脸，忽然间，再不去想那个梦已经不可能了。他由着回忆涌来，它果然洪水似的涌来。连同它一起涌来的，还有对他自己所作所为的记忆。他保证过要做露西高贵的朋友，他们还一起跑着躲避纵马而来的士兵。可他跑得比她快。在梯子顶上——那道通向安全的梯子顶上——*他没有等她*。

「随便哪个老牌英雄都会等她，让她先走。」他对自己说。「随便哪位绅士都会——随便哪个*男子汉*都会——更别提英雄了。可我一溜烟下了梯子，把她忘了。我把她*丢*在那儿了。」

===Original===
Remorse stirred his boots more ungently than before.

“But it was only a dream,” he said. And then remorse said, as he had felt all along that it would if he only gave it a chance:

“But suppose it wasn’t a dream﻿—suppose it was real. Suppose you *did* leave her there, my noble friend, and that’s why she’s lost.”

Suddenly Philip felt very small, very forlorn, very much alone in the world. But Helen would come back. That telegram would bring her.

===Chinese===
悔恨把他的靴子催得比先前更不安分。

「可那不过是个梦啊。」他说。接着悔恨开口了，说的正是他一直觉得、只要给它个机会它就会说的话：

「可要是那不是梦呢——要是它是真的呢。假如你*确实*把她丢在了那儿，我高贵的朋友，她失踪就是这个缘故。」

菲利普忽然觉得自己非常渺小、非常孤苦、在这世上孤零零一个人。可海伦会回来的。那封电报会把她带回来。

===Original===
Yes. And he would have to tell her that perhaps it was his fault.

It was in vain that Philip told himself that Helen would never believe about the city. He felt that she would. Why shouldn’t she? She knew about the fairy tales and the Arabian Nights. And she would know that these things *did* happen.

“Oh, what shall I do? What shall I do?” he said, quite loud. And there was no one but himself to give the answer.

“If I could only get back into the city,” he said. “But that hateful nurse has pulled it all down and locked up the nursery. So I can’t even build it again. Oh, what *shall* I do?”

===Chinese===
是的。而他将不得不告诉她，那也许是他的错。

菲利普白白地对自己说，海伦绝不会相信那座城的事。他觉得她会的。她为什么不会？童话和《一千零一夜》的事她都知道。她会明白这种事*确实*会发生。

「哦，我怎么办呢？我怎么办呢？」他说，声音很响。可除了他自己，没有谁来回答。

「要是能回到那座城里就好了。」他说。「可那个可恨的保姆把它全拆了，还把育儿室锁了。我连重新搭一遍都不行。哦，我到底*怎么办*呢？」

===Original===
And with that he began to cry. For now he felt quite sure that the dream wasn’t a dream﻿—that he really *had* got into the magic city, had promised to stand by Lucy, and had been false to his promise and to her.

He rubbed his eyes with his knuckles and also﻿—rather painfully﻿—with Mr. Noah, whom he still held. “What shall I do?” he sobbed.

And a very very teeny tiny voice said:

“Put me down.”

===Chinese===
说着说着，他哭了起来。因为现在他完全确定了：那个梦不是梦——他真的进过那座魔法之城，真的保证过要与露西相依相伴，却背弃了自己的誓言，也背弃了她。

他用指节揉眼睛，还用一直攥在手里的诺亚先生揉了揉——揉得相当疼。「我怎么办哪？」他抽抽搭搭地说。

一个很小很小的、细得不能再细的声音说：

「把我放下来。」

===Original===
“Eh?” said Philip.

“Put me down,” said the voice again. It was such a teeny tiny voice that he could only just hear it. It was unlikely, of course, that the voice could have been Mr. Noah’s; but then whose else could it be? On the bare chance that it *might* have been Mr. Noah who spoke﻿—more unlikely things had happened before, as you know﻿—Philip set the little wooden figure down on the chess-table. It stood there, wooden as ever.

“Put *who* down?” Philip asked. And then, before his eyes, the little wooden figure grew alive, stooped to pick up the yellow disc of wood on which Noah’s Ark people stand, rolled it up like a mat, put it under his arm and began to walk towards the side of the table where Philip stood.

He knelt down to bring his ears nearer the little live moving thing.

===Chinese===
「嗯？」菲利普说。

「把我放下来。」那声音又说。它细小得他勉强才听得见。当然，那声音不可能是诺亚先生的；可除此之外，又能是谁的呢？看在那万一的份上——万一说话的*真是*诺亚先生，要知道，更邪门的事从前也是有过的——菲利普把那个小小的木头人放到了棋桌上。它站在那儿，还跟先前一样是木头。

「把*谁*放下来？」菲利普问。紧接着，就在他眼前，那个小木头人活了过来，弯腰捡起诺亚方舟小人脚下踩的那块黄色圆木片，像卷垫子一样卷起来，往胳膊底下一夹，朝着菲利普站的那一侧桌边走来。

他跪了下来，好让耳朵离那小小的活物更近些。

===Original===
“*What* did you say?” he asked, for he fancied that Mr. Noah had again spoken.

“I said, what’s the matter?” said the little voice.

“It’s Lucy. She’s lost and it’s my fault. And I can only just hear you. It hurts my ears hearing you,” complained Philip.

“There’s an ear-trumpet in a box on the middle of the cabinet,” he could just hear the teeny tiny voice say; “it belonged to a great-aunt. Get it out and listen through it.”

Philip got it out. It was an odd curly thing, and at first he could not be sure which end he ought to put to his ear. But he tried both ends, and on the second trial he heard quite a loud, strong, big voice say:

===Chinese===
「你*方才*说什么？」他问，因为他恍惚觉得诺亚先生又开了口。

「我说，出什么事了？」那细细的声音说。

「是露西。她丢了，是我的错。还有，你的声音我勉强才听得见。听你说话，我耳朵都疼。」菲利普抱怨道。

「柜子当中一个盒子里有一只助听筒，」他勉强听见那细细的声音在说；「它是一位姨祖母的遗物。拿出来，凑着它听。」

菲利普把它拿了出来。那是个弯弯曲曲的怪东西，起初他拿不准该把哪一头贴在耳朵上。可他把两头都试了试，到第二回，就听见一个相当洪亮、结实、粗大的声音说：
===Original===
“That’s better.”

“Then it wasn’t a dream last night,” said Philip.

“Of course it wasn’t,” said Mr. Noah.

“Then where is Lucy?”

“In the city, of course. Where you left her.”

===Chinese===
「这就好多了。」

「那么，昨晚不是梦了。」菲利普说。

「当然不是。」诺亚先生说。

「那露西在哪儿？」

「当然在城里。在你丢下她的地方。」

===Original===
“But she *can’t* be,” said Philip desperately. “The city’s all pulled down and gone forever.”

“The city you built in this room is pulled down,” said Mr. Noah, “but the city you went to wasn’t in this room. Now I put it to you﻿—how could it be?”

“But it *was*,” said Philip, “or else how could I have got into it.”

“It’s a little difficult, I own,” said Mr. Noah. “But, you see, you built those cities in two worlds. It’s pulled down in *this* world. But in the other world it’s going on.”

===Chinese===
「可她*不可能*在啊，」菲利普绝望地说，「那座城全拆了，永远没了。」

「你在这间屋里搭的城是拆了，」诺亚先生说，「可你去过的那座城，不在这间屋里。你自己想想——它怎么可能在呢？」

「可它就在啊，」菲利普说，「不然我怎么进得去。」

「是有点绕，我承认。」诺亚先生说。「不过你瞧，你那两座城是在两个世界里搭的。在*这个*世界里，它拆了。可在另一个世界里，它还照样过着日子呢。」

===Original===
“I don’t understand,” said Philip.

“I thought you wouldn’t,” said Mr. Noah; “but it’s true, for all that. Everything people make in that world goes on forever.”

“But how was it that I got in?”

“Because you belong to both worlds. And you built the cities. So they were yours.”

===Chinese===
「我不明白。」菲利普说。

「我就知道你不明白，」诺亚先生说；「不过它照样是真的。人在那个世界里做的一切，都永远长存。」

「可我怎么就进去了呢？」

「因为你属于两个世界。城又是你搭的。所以它们是你的。」

===Original===
“But Lucy got in.”

“She built up a corner of your city that the nurse had knocked down.”

“But *you*,” said Philip, more and more bewildered. “You’re here. So you can’t be there.”

“But I *am* there,” said Mr. Noah.

“But you’re here. And you’re alive here. What made you come alive?”

===Chinese===
「可露西也进去了。」

「她把你城里被保姆碰塌的一个角，重新搭了起来。」

「可是*您*，」菲利普说，越来越糊涂，「您在这儿。那您就不可能在那儿。」

「可我*就在*那儿。」诺亚先生说。

「可您在这儿呀。您在这儿是活的。您怎么活过来的？」

===Original===
“Your tears,” said Mr. Noah. “Tears are very strong magic. No, don’t begin to cry again. What’s the matter?”

“I want to get back into the city.”

“It’s dangerous.”

“I don’t care.”

“You were glad enough to get away,” said Mr. Noah.

===Chinese===
「靠你的眼泪。」诺亚先生说。「眼泪是很强的魔法。不，别又哭起来。到底怎么回事？」

「我想回到那座城里去。」

「那可危险。」

「我不怕。」

「你当时可是巴不得逃出来。」诺亚先生说。

===Original===
“I know: that’s the worst of it,” said Philip. “Oh, isn’t there any way to get back? If I climbed in at the nursery windows and got the bricks and built it all up and﻿—”

“Quite unnecessary, I assure you. There are a thousand doors to that city.”

“I wish I could find *one*,” said Philip; “but, I say, I thought time was all different there. How is it Lucy is lost all this time if time doesn’t count?”

“It does count, now,” said Mr. Noah; “you made it count when you ran away and left Lucy. That set the clocks of the city to the time of this world.”

===Chinese===
「我知道：这正是最糟的地方。」菲利普说。「哦，难道就没有办法回去吗？要是我从育儿室的窗户爬进去，把积木拿出来，把城全搭起来，然后——」

「完全没必要，我向你保证。那座城有一千扇门。」

「但愿我找得着*一扇*，」菲利普说；「不过，我说，我还以为那边的时间全然是另一回事呢。要是时间不算数，露西怎么会丢了这么久？」

「现在算了。」诺亚先生说；「你逃跑丢下露西，时间就开始算了。那一来，城里的钟就对上了这个世界的时间。」

===Original===
“I don’t understand,” said Philip; “but it doesn’t matter. Show me the door and I’ll go back and find Lucy.”

“Build something and go through it,” said Mr. Noah. “That’s all. Your tears are dry on me now. Goodbye.” And he laid down his yellow mat, stepped on to it and was just a little wooden figure again.

Philip dropped the ear-trumpet and looked at Mr. Noah.

“I *don’t* understand,” he said. But this at least he understood. That Helen would come back when she got that telegram, and that before she came he must go into the other world and find the lost Lucy.

===Chinese===
「我不明白。」菲利普说；「不过不要紧。指给我一扇门，我就回去找露西。」

「搭个什么东西，从它中间穿过去。」诺亚先生说。「就这么简单。你的眼泪在我身上干了。再见。」他放下黄垫子，踩上去，又成了一具小小的木头人。

菲利普丢下助听筒，看着诺亚先生。

「我*是*不明白。」他说。可至少这一点他明白了：那封电报一到，海伦就会回来；而在她回来之前，他必须进到另一个世界里去，找到失踪的露西。

===Original===
“But oh,” he said, “suppose I *don’t* find her. I wish I hadn’t built those cities so big! And time will go on. And, perhaps, when Helen comes back she’ll find *me* lost *too*﻿—as well as Lucy.”

But he dried his eyes and told himself that this was not how heroes behaved. He must build again. Whichever way you looked at it there was no time to be lost. And besides the nurse might occur at any moment.

He looked round for building materials. There was the chess-table. It had long narrow legs set round it, rather like arches. Something might be done with it, with books and candlesticks and Japanese vases.

Something *was* done. Philip built with earnest care, but also with considerable speed. If the nurse should come in before he had made a door and got through it﻿—come in and find him building again﻿—she was quite capable of putting him to bed, where, of course, building is impossible. In a very little time there was a building. But how to get in. He was, alas, the wrong size. He stood helpless, and once more tears pricked and swelled behind his eyelids. One tear fell on his hand.

===Chinese===
「可是哦，」他说，「万一我*找不着*她呢。真后悔把那些城搭得那么大！时间还要往前走。说不定海伦回来的时候，会发现*我*也丢了——跟露西一起。」

可他擦干眼睛，告诉自己，英雄可不是这副做派。他必须重新搭。不管怎么看，耽误不得。再说，保姆随时可能冒出来。

他环顾四周找建材。有那张棋桌。桌子四围着细长的桌腿，颇像一道道拱门。用它，再配上书、烛台和日本花瓶，兴许能成点事。

真成了点事。菲利普搭得又认真又相当快。万一他还没做出一扇门、还没穿过去，保姆就进来了——进来撞见他又在搭东西——她完全有可能把他打发上床，而床上自然是没法搭城的。不多时，一座建筑立起来了。可是，怎么进去呢。唉，他的个头不对。他束手无策地站着，眼泪又一次在眼皮后面刺胀起来。一滴泪落在他的手上。

===Original===
“Tears are a strong magic,” Mr. Noah had said. And at the thought the tears stopped. Still there *was* a tear, the one on his hand. He rubbed it on the pillar of the porch.

And instantly a queer tight thin feeling swept through him. He felt giddy and shut his eyes. His boots, ever sympathetic, shuffled on the carpet. Or was it the carpet? It was very thick and﻿—He opened his eyes. His feet were once more on the long grass of the illimitable prairie. And in front of him towered the gigantic porch of a vast building and a domino path leading up to it.

“Oh, I am so glad,” cried Philip among the grass. “I couldn’t have borne it if she’d been lost forever, and all my fault.”

===Chinese===
「眼泪是很强的魔法。」诺亚先生说过。这念头一起，眼泪止住了。可手上*还有*一滴，落下来的那一滴。他把它抹在门廊的柱子上。

霎时间，一种古怪、紧窄、细瘦的感觉掠过他全身。他觉得发晕，闭上了眼睛。他的靴子——它们向来贴心——在地毯上蹭了蹭。还是地毯吗？它那么厚，而且——他睁开了眼睛。他的双脚，又站在一望无际的大草原的长草上了。面前高耸着一座巨大建筑庞大的门廊，一条多米诺骨牌铺的小径通向它。

「哦，我太高兴了。」菲利普在草丛中喊道。「要是她永远丢了，还全怪我，我可受不了。」

===Original===
The gigantic porch lowered frowningly above him. What would he find on the other side of it?

“I don’t care. I’ve simply got to go,” he said, and stepped out bravely. “If I can’t *be* a hero I’ll try to behave like one.”

And with that he stepped out, stumbling a little in the thick grass, and the dark shadow of the porch received him.

===Chinese===
庞大的门廊阴沉沉地压在他头顶。门廊的那一边，他会找到什么呢？

「我不管。我非去不可。」他说，勇敢地迈步。「就算我当不成英雄，我也要照英雄的样子行事。」

说罢他迈了出去，在深草里微微磕绊，门廊的黑影把他接了进去。
===Original===
---

“Bother the child,” said the nurse, coming into the drawing-room a little later; “if he hasn’t been at his precious building game again! I shall have to give him a lesson over this﻿—I can see that. And I will too﻿—a lesson he won’t forget in a hurry.”

She went through the house, looking for the too bold builder that she might give him that lesson. Then she went through the garden, still on the same errand.

Half an hour later she burst into the servants’ hall and threw herself into a chair.

===Chinese===
---

「这孩子真讨厌，」过了一会儿，保姆走进客厅说；「他又玩上他那宝贝搭城游戏了！这回我得给他个教训——我看得出来。我说到做到——一个他一时半会儿忘不掉的教训。」

她走遍全宅，找这个胆子太大的建筑师，好给他那个教训。接着她又走遍花园，还是为着同一桩差事。

半个钟头后，她冲进仆人厅，一屁股坐进椅子里。

===Original===
“I don’t care what happens now,” she said. “The house is bewitched, I think. I shall go the very minute I’ve had my dinner.”

“What’s up now?” the cook came to the door to say.

“Up?” said the nurse. “Oh, nothing’s *up*. What should there be? Everything’s all right and beautiful, and just as it should be, of course.”

“Miss Lucy’s not found yet, of course, but that’s all, isn’t it?”

“All? And enough too, I should have thought,” said the nurse. “But as it happens it’s *not* all. The boy’s lost now. Oh, I’m not joking. He’s lost I tell you, the same as the other one﻿—and I’m off out of this by the two thirty-seven train, and I don’t care who knows it.”

===Chinese===
「现在随它出什么事，我都不管了。」她说。「这宅子闹鬼了，我看是。我一吃完午饭就走，一分钟也不多待。」

「这又是怎么了？」厨娘到门口来问。

「怎么了？」保姆说。「哦，没*怎么*。还能怎么？一切都好好的、美美的，自然就该那个样子。」

「露西小姐还没找着，当然，不过就这么点事，不是吗？」

「就这么点事？这还不够呛吗，我倒要问问。」保姆说。「可偏偏不光是这个。现在那男孩也丢了。哦，我不是说笑。我告诉你，他丢了，跟那一个一样——我要搭两点三十七分的火车离开这儿，谁爱知道谁知道去。」

===Original===
“Lor!” said the cook.

---

Before starting for the two thirty-seven train the nurse went back to the drawing-room to destroy Philip’s new building, to restore to their proper places its books, candlesticks, vases, and chessmen.

There we will leave her.

===Chinese===
「哎哟！」厨娘说。

---

赶两点三十七分的火车之前，保姆又回了一趟客厅，去毁掉菲利普新搭的建筑，把那些书、烛台、花瓶和棋子放回原位。

她嘛，我们就把她撂在这儿吧。

## IV The Dragon-Slayer / 屠龙者
===Original===
When Philip walked up the domino path and under the vast arch into the darkness beyond, his heart felt strong with high resolve. His legs, however, felt weak; strangely weak, especially about the knees. The doorway was so enormous, that which lay beyond was so dark, and he himself so very very small. As he passed under the little gateway which he had built of three dominoes with the little silver knight in armour on the top, he noticed that he was only as high as a domino, and you know how very little that is.

Philip went along the domino path. He had to walk carefully, for to him the spots on the dominoes were quite deep hollows. But as they were black they were easy to see. He had made three arches, one beyond another, of two pairs of silver candlesticks with silver inkstands on the top of them. The third pair of silver candlesticks had a book on the top of them because there were no more inkstands. And when he had passed through the three silver arches, he stopped.

Beyond lay a sort of velvety darkness with white gleams in it. And as his eyes became accustomed to the darkness, he saw that he was in a great hall of silver pillars, gigantic silver candlesticks they seemed to be, and they went in long vistas this way and that way and every way, like the hop-poles in a hop-field, so that whichever way you turned, a long pillared corridor lay in front of you.

Philip had no idea which way he ought to go. It seemed most unlikely that he would find Lucy in a dark hall with silver pillars.

===Chinese===
菲利普沿着多米诺骨牌铺成的小路走向那座巨大的拱门，走进门外的黑暗里。他的心因为满腔豪情而坚强，两条腿却发软；软得出奇，尤其是膝盖那儿。门洞那么高大，门外的世界那么黑暗，而他自己是那么那么渺小。当他从自己用三块多米诺骨牌搭成的小门楼下走过时——门楼顶上还立着那个披甲戴盔的小银骑士——他发觉自己如今只有一块骨牌那么高，你知道那有多矮。

菲利普沿着骨牌小路往前走。他得走得很小心，因为在他眼里，骨牌上的圆点都是相当深的坑。好在圆点是黑的，一眼就能看清。他先前搭过三道拱门，一道挨着一道，用两对银烛架支起来，顶上搁着银墨水台。第三对银烛架顶上搁的是一本书，因为墨水台不够用了。走过三道银拱门之后，他停住了脚步。

前面是一片天鹅绒似的黑暗，其中浮动着点点白光。等眼睛渐渐适应了黑暗，他看清自己正置身于一座满是银柱的大厅——那些银柱看上去是一支支巨大的银烛台，朝着四面八方纵横延伸，就像啤酒花田里的花架杆，无论你转向哪边，眼前都是一条长长的柱廊。

菲利普完全不知道该往哪边走。要在一座黑漆漆的银柱大厅里找到露西，看来是没什么指望了。

===Original===
“All the same,” he said, “it’s not so dark as it was, by long chalks.”

It was not. The silver pillars had begun to give out a faint soft glow like the silver phosphorescence that lies in sea pools in summer time.

“It’s lucky too,” he said, “because of the holes in the floor.”

The holes were the spots on the dominoes with which the pillared hall was paved.

===Chinese===
「不过，」他说，「现在可比刚才亮多了，亮得不是一星半点。」

确实亮了。银柱开始发出一种幽幽的柔光，就像夏天海边岩石水潭里泛起的银色磷光。

「这也算走运，」他说，「多亏地板上有那些窟窿。」

所谓窟窿，就是铺满柱厅地面的多米诺骨牌上的圆点。

===Original===
“I wonder what part of the city where Lucy is I shall come out at?” Philip asked himself. But he need not have troubled. He did not come out at all. He walked on and on and on and on and on. He thought he was walking straight, but really he was turning first this way and then that, and then the other way among the avenues of silver pillars which all looked just alike.

He was getting very tired, and he had been walking a long time, before he came to anything that was not silver pillars and velvet black under invisible roofs, and floor paved with dominoes laid very close together.

“Oh, I am glad!” he said at last, when he saw the pavement narrow to a single line of dominoes just like the path he had come in by. There was an arch too, like the arch by which he had come in. And then he perceived in a shock of miserable surprise that it was, in fact, the same arch and the same domino path. He had come back, after all that walking, to the point from which he had started. It was most mortifying. So silly! Philip sat down on the edge of the domino path to rest and think.

===Chinese===
「不知道我会从这座城市的哪个地界走出去，走到露西待的那一头？」菲利普心里嘀咕。其实他犯不着操心。他压根儿就没走出去。他走啊走，走啊走，走啊走。他以为自己走的是直线，其实他在那些长得一模一样的银柱大道之间，一会儿拐向这边，一会儿拐向那边，一会儿又拐向另一边。

他走得越来越累，也走了好久好久，才终于见到点别的东西——在那之前，入眼的除了银柱、看不见的屋顶下天鹅绒般的黑暗，和铺得密密实实的多米诺骨牌地面，什么也没有。

「哦，太好了！」他终于喊了出来——他看见那条骨牌路窄成了一列，正和他进来时走的那条一模一样。还有一道拱门，也和他进来时经过的那道一个样。紧接着，他猛地一惊，又是懊恼又是意外地发现：那其实就是同一道拱门、同一条骨牌路。走了这么半天，他兜了一大圈，又回到了出发的地方。真是窝囊透了。多傻呀！菲利普在骨牌路边坐下来，一边歇脚，一边想办法。

===Original===
“Suppose I just walk out and don’t believe in magic any more?” he said to himself. “Helen says magic can only happen to people who believe in magic. So if I just walked out and didn’t believe as hard as ever I could, I should be my own right size again, and Lucy would be back, and there wouldn’t *be* any magic.”

“Yes, but,” said that voice that always would come and join in whenever Philip was talking to himself, “suppose Lucy *does* believe it? Then it’ll all go on for her, whatever *you* believe, and she *won’t* be back. Besides, you know you’ve *got* to believe it, because it’s true.”

“Oh, bother!” said Philip; “I’m tired. I don’t want to go on.”

“You shouldn’t have deserted Lucy,” said the tiresome voice, “then you wouldn’t have had to go back to look for her.”

===Chinese===
「要是我干脆走出去，从此再也不信魔法了呢？」他自言自语道，「海伦说过，魔法只降临在相信魔法的人身上。那只要我一走了之，拼命不去相信，我就能变回原来的个头，露西也会回来，魔法也就*不*存在了。」

「是吗，可是，」那个声音说——每当菲利普自言自语，这个声音总爱跑来插嘴，「要是露西*偏*信呢？那不管*你*信不信，这一切对她来说都会照旧，她*就*回不去。再说，你知道你*非*信不可，因为这都是真的。」

「哦，烦死了！」菲利普说，「我累了。我不想再走了。」

「你不该丢下露西不管，」那个讨厌的声音说，「那样你也就用不着回头来找她了。」

===Original===
“But I can’t find my way. How can I find my way?”

“You know well enough. Fix your eyes on a far-off pillar and walk straight to it, and when you’re nearly there fix your eyes a little farther. You’re bound to come out somewhere.”

“But I’m tired and it’s so lonely,” said Philip.

“Lucy’s lonely too,” said the voice.

===Chinese===
「可我找不着路啊。叫我怎么找路呢？」

「你自己心里清楚得很。盯住远处的一根柱子，照直朝它走，快走到的时候，再把眼睛盯得稍远一点。走着走着，你总会从什么地方走出去的。」

「可我又累，这里又这么冷清。」菲利普说。

「露西也冷清着呢。」那个声音说。

===Original===
“Drop it!” said Philip. And he got up and began to walk again. Also he took the advice of that worrying voice and fixed his eyes on a distant pillar.

“But why should I bother?” he said; “this is a sort of dream.”

“Even if it *were* a dream,” said the voice, “there are adventures in it. So you may as well be adventurous.”

“Oh, all right,” said Philip, and on he went.

===Chinese===
「别说了！」菲利普说。他站起身，重新上路。他还听了那个烦人的声音的劝告，眼睛盯住一根远处的柱子。

「可我干吗要这么费劲呢？」他说，「这不过是一场梦罢了。」

「就算*是*梦，」那个声音说，「梦里也有历险啊。所以你不妨拿出点历险的劲头来。」

「哦，好吧。」菲利普说，接着又走了起来。

===Original===
And by walking very carefully and fixing his eyes a long way off, he did at last come right through the hall of silver pillars, and saw beyond the faint glow of the pillars the blue light of day. It shone very brightly through a very little door, and when Philip came to that door he went through it without hesitation. And there he was in a big field. It was rather like the illimitable prairie, only there were great patches of different-coloured flowers. Also there was a path across it, and he followed the path.

“Because,” he said, “I’m more likely to meet Lucy. Girls always keep to paths. They never explore.”

Which just shows how little he knew about girls.

===Chinese===
就这样，他走得小心翼翼，目光放得老远，终于穿过了银柱大厅，看见柱子的微光之外透进来一片白昼的蓝天。那光是从一扇很小的门里亮堂堂地照进来的。菲利普走到门前，毫不迟疑地跨了出去。这下他来到了一片开阔的野地里。这地方挺像那无边无际的大草原，只是原野上有一大片一大片颜色各异的野花。草地中间还横着一条小路，他便顺着小路走了下去。

「因为，」他说，「这样更容易碰上露西。女孩子总爱顺着路走，从来不肯探险。」

这话恰恰说明，他对女孩子了解得多么少。

===Original===
He looked back after a while, to see what the hall of pillars looked like from outside, but it was already dim in the mists of distance.

But ahead of him he saw a great rough building, rather like Stonehenge.

“I wish I’d come into the other city where the people are, and the soldiers, and the greyhounds, and the coconuts,” he told himself. “There’s nobody here at all, not even Lucy.”

The loneliness of the place grew more and more unpleasing to Philip. But he went on. It seemed more reasonable than to go back.

===Chinese===
走了一阵，他回头想看看那座柱厅从外面是什么样子，可它早已隐没在远处的雾霭里，只剩个影影绰绰的轮廓。

不过他看见前方矗立着一座粗粝的大建筑，挺像巨石阵（Stonehenge）的。

「真希望我进的是另一座城，城里有人，有士兵，有灵狗，还有椰子。」他暗自想道，「这地方一个人也没有，连露西都不在。」

这地方越来越冷清，越来越让菲利普喜欢不起来。可他还是继续走。往前走总比走回头路讲得通些。

===Original===
“I ought to be very hungry,” he said; “I must have been walking for hours.” But he wasn’t hungry. It may have been the magic, or it may have been the odd breakfast he had had. I don’t know. He spoke aloud because it was so quiet in that strange open country with no one in it but himself. And no sound but the clump, clump of his boots on the path. And it seemed to him that everything grew quieter and quieter till he could almost hear himself think. Loneliness, real loneliness is a dreadful thing. I hope you will never feel it. Philip looked to right and left, and before him, and on all the wide plain nothing moved. There were the grass and flowers, but no wind stirred them. And there was no sign that any living person had ever trodden that path﻿—except that there *was* a path to tread, and that the path led to the Stonehenge building, and even that seemed to be only a ruin.

“I’ll go as far as that anyhow,” said Philip; “perhaps there’ll be a signboard there or something.”

There was something. Something most unexpected. Philip reached the building; it was really very like Stonehenge, only the pillars were taller and closer together and there was one high solid towering wall; turned the corner of a massive upright and ran almost into the arms, and quite on to the feet of a man in a white apron and a square paper cap, who sat on a fallen column, eating bread and cheese with a clasp-knife.

===Chinese===
「照理说我该饿坏了，」他说，「我肯定已经走了好几个钟头了。」可他并不饿。这也许是魔法的缘故，也许是因为他吃的那顿奇怪的早饭。我说不好。他之所以开口说话，是因为那片陌生的旷野上除了他自己空无一人，太安静了。四下里没有别的声音，只有他的靴子踩在小路上的咚咚声。而且在他听来，周遭越来越静、越来越静，静得他几乎能听见自己心里的念头。孤独，真正的孤独，是件可怕的事。我希望你永远不要尝到那种滋味。菲利普左看看、右看看，又望望前方，整片辽阔的原野上什么也不动。有草，有花，可没有一丝风摇动它们。也看不出有任何活人踩过那条路的迹象——只有那条路本身确实是给人踩的，而且那路一直通向那座巨石阵似的建筑——就连那建筑，看上去也只剩一片废墟。

「好歹我先走到那儿再说，」菲利普说，「说不定那儿有块指路牌什么的。」

那儿果然有点东西。一样万万想不到的东西。菲利普走到了那建筑跟前；它真的很像巨石阵，只是石柱更高、排得更密，旁边还立着一面又高又结实的巍峨大墙。他绕过一根粗大的立柱拐了个弯，差点一头撞进一个人的怀里，还结结实实踩到了那人的脚上。那人系着白围裙，戴着方纸帽，正坐在一根倒下的柱子上，用一把折刀吃着面包和干酪。

===Original===
“I beg your pardon!” Philip gasped.

“Granted, I’m sure,” said the man; “but it’s a dangerous thing to do, Master Philip, running sheer on to chaps’ clasp-knives.”

He set Philip on his feet, and waved the knife, which had been so often sharpened that the blade was half worn away.

“Set you down and get your breath,” he said kindly.

“Why, it’s *you*!” said Philip.

===Chinese===
「请您原谅！」菲利普倒抽一口气说。

「原谅你，一定原谅，」那人说，「不过这可是桩危险事儿，菲利普少爷，直挺挺往人家的折刀上撞。」

他把菲利普扶起来站好，手里挥着那把折刀——这刀磨过太多次，刀刃都磨掉了一半。

「先坐定，喘口气。」他和气地说。

「咦，原来是*你*呀！」菲利普说。

===Original===
“Course it is. Who should I be if I wasn’t me? That’s poetry.”

“But how did you get here?”

“Ah!” said the man going on with his bread and cheese, while he talked quite in the friendliest way, “that’s telling.”

“Well, tell then,” said Philip impatiently. But he sat down.

“Well, you say it’s me. Who be it? Give it a name.”

===Chinese===
「当然是我。我要不是我，还能是谁呢？这话有诗意吧。」

「可您是怎么到这儿来的？」

「啊！」那人一边继续吃面包和干酪，一边用顶友好的口气说，「这可不能说。」

「不能说也得说。」菲利普不耐烦地说。不过他还是坐下了。

「那好，你说是我。是谁呀？给个名号。」

===Original===
“You’re old Perrin,” said Pip; “I mean, of course, I beg your pardon, you’re Mr. Perrin, the carpenter.”

“And what does carpenters do?”

“Carp, I suppose,” said Philip. “That means they make things, doesn’t it?”

“That’s it,” said the man encouragingly; “what sort of things now might old Perrin have made for you?”

“You made my wheelbarrow, I know,” said Philip, “and my bricks.”

===Chinese===
「你是老佩林，」皮普说，「哦不，当然，请您原谅，您是佩林先生（Mr. Perrin），那位木匠。」

「木匠是干什么营生的？」

「呃——做木工吧，」菲利普说，「就是说他们是造东西的，对不对？」

「对喽，」那人鼓励地说，「那老佩林兴许给你造过些什么家伙呢？」

「您做过我的手推车，这我知道，」菲利普说，「还有我的积木。」

===Original===
“Ah!” said Mr. Perrin, “now you’ve got it. I made your bricks, seasoned oak, and true to the thousandth of an inch, they was. And that’s how I got here. So now you know.”

“But what are you doing here?” said Philip, wriggling restlessly on the fallen column.

“Waiting for you. Them as knows sent me out to meet you, and give you a hint of what’s expected of you.”

“Well. What *is*?” said Philip. “I mean I think it’s very kind of you. What *is* expected?”

===Chinese===
「啊！」佩林先生说，「这就对上号了。你的积木是我做的，上好的陈年橡木，尺寸精确到千分之一英寸，没得挑。我就是这么到这儿来的。这下你知道了吧。」

「可您在这儿干什么呢？」菲利普问，在倒下的柱子上不安地扭来扭去。

「等你呗。上头心里有数的人打发俺来迎你，给你透个信儿，说说盼着你干点什么。」

「哦。那到底*是*什么呀？」菲利普说，「我是说，您真是太好了。到底盼着我干什么呢？」

===Original===
“Plenty of time,” said the carpenter, “plenty. Nothing ain’t expected of you till towards sundown.”

“I do think it was most awfully kind of you,” said Philip, who had now thought this over.

“You was kind to old Perrin once,” said that person.

“Was I?” said Philip, much surprised.

===Chinese===
「时间有的是，」木匠说，「多着呢。不到太阳偏西，什么也不指望你干。」

「我真是觉得您好心到了极点，」菲利普说，这话他已经细细琢磨过一遍了。

「你也对老佩林好过一回呀。」那位说。

「是吗？」菲利普说，大为惊讶。

===Original===
“Yes; when my little girl was ailing you brought her a lot of pears off your own tree. Not one of ’em you didn’t ’ave yourself that year, Miss Helen told me. And you brought back our kitten﻿—the sandy and white one with black spots﻿—when it strayed. So I was quite willing to come and meet you when so told. And knowing something of young gentlemen’s peckers, owing to being in business once next door to a boys’ school, I made so bold as to bring you a snack.”

He reached a hand down behind the fallen pillar on which they sat and brought up a basket.

“Here,” he said. And Philip, raising the lid, was delighted to find that he was hungry. It was a pleasant basketful. Meat pasties, red hairy gooseberries, a stone bottle of ginger-beer, a blue mug with Philip on it in gold letters, a slice of soda cake and two farthing sugar-sticks.

“I’m sure I’ve seen that basket before,” said the boy as he ate.

===Chinese===
「是啊。我家小闺女闹病那阵子，你从自家树上摘了好多梨给她送去。海伦小姐告诉俺，那年那些梨你自己一个也没尝着。还有我们家的小猫走丢了，是你给抱回来的——就是那只黄白相间、带黑点儿的——所以一来叫俺来接你，俺是心甘情愿。再说，俺从前做买卖的地方挨着一所男校，对年轻少爷们的肚量多少有点数，就斗胆给你捎了点吃的。」

他把手伸到他们坐的那根倒柱后面，拎上来一只篮子。

「给。」他说。菲利普掀开盖子，高兴地发现自己原来饿了。满满一篮子好东西：肉馅饼、红彤彤毛茸茸的醋栗、一瓶石瓶装的姜汁啤酒、一只蓝杯子——上面还用金字写着「菲利普」——一块苏打蛋糕，还有两根一法新的小糖棍。

「这篮子我肯定见过。」男孩一边吃一边说。

===Original===
“Like enough. It’s the one you brought them pears down in.”

“Now look here,” said Philip, through his seventh bite of pasty, “you *must* tell me how you got here. And tell me where you’ve got to. You’ve simply no idea how muddling it all is to me. Do tell me *everything*. Where are we, I mean, and why? And what I’ve got to do. And why? And when? Tell me every single thing.” And he took the eighth bite.

“You really don’t know, sir?”

===Chinese===
「多半见过。就是你装梨送来用的那只。」

「我说，」菲利普咬到馅饼第七口的时候说，「您*一定*得告诉我您是怎么到这儿来的。还得告诉我这到底是什么地方。您简直想不到，这一切把我搅得多糊涂。求您把*所有事*都告诉我。我是说，我们在哪儿，为什么？我要干什么，为什么？还有什么时候？一件也别落下。」说着他咬下了第八口。

「您当真一点都不知道，少爷？」

===Original===
“No,” said Philip, contemplating the ninth or last bite but one. It was a large pasty.

“Well then. Here goes. But I was always a poor speaker, and so considered even by friends at cricket dinners and whatnot.”

“But I don’t want you to speak,” said Philip; “just tell me.”

“Well, then. How did I get here? I got here through having made them bricks what you built this tumbledown old ancient place with.”

===Chinese===
「不知道。」菲利普说，眼睛盯着第九口——也就是倒数第二口。那是只很大的馅饼。

「那好吧。俺这就说说。不过俺天生嘴笨，就连板球宴会那些场合上的朋友们也这么说。」

「可我不要您『演讲』，」菲利普说，「您就随便讲讲。」

「那好。俺是怎么到这儿来的？是靠做那些积木来的，就是你搭这座破破烂烂老古董地方用的那些。」

===Original===
“*I* built?”

“Yes, with them bricks I made you. I understand as this was the first building you ever put up. That’s why it’s first on the road to where you want to get to!”

Philip looked round at the Stonehenge building and saw that it was indeed built of enormous oak bricks.

“Of course,” he said, “only I’ve grown smaller.”

===Chinese===
「*我*搭的？」

「是啊，用俺做给你的那些积木。俺听说这是你平生搭起的第一座房子。所以它才排在你去往目的地的路上，头一名！」

菲利普环顾那座巨石阵似的建筑，看清了它果真是用巨大的橡木积木搭成的。

「当然，」他说，「只不过我变小了。」

===Original===
“Or they’ve grown bigger,” said Mr. Perrin; “it’s the same thing. You see it’s like this. All the cities and things you ever built is in this country. I don’t know how it’s managed, no more’n what you do. But so it is. And as you made ’em, you’ve the right to come to them﻿—if you can get there. And you have got there. It isn’t everyone has the luck, I’m told. Well, then, you made the cities, but you made ’em out of what other folks had made, things like bricks and chessmen and books and candlesticks and dominoes and brass basins and every sort of kind of thing. An’ all the people who helped to make all them things you used to build with, they’re all here too. D’you see? *Making’s* the thing. If it was no more than the lad that turned the handle of the grindstone to sharp the knife that carved a bit of a cabinet or whatnot, or a child that picked a teazle to finish a bit of the cloth that’s glued on to the bottom of a chessman﻿—they’re all here. They’re what’s called the population of your cities.”

“I see. They’ve got small, like I have,” said Philip.

===Chinese===
「要么就是城变大了，」佩林先生说，「反正一回事。你听俺说，事情是这样。你搭过的那些城啊什么的，全在这个国家里。这里头是怎么弄的，俺不知道，你也一样不知道。可事实就是这样。这些东西是你搭的，你就有权利到它们这儿来——只要你能到得了。而你已经到这儿了。听说不是人人都有这个运气。那么，你搭了这些城，可你是拿别人做出来的东西搭的，像积木呀、棋子呀、书本呀、烛台呀、骨牌呀、黄铜盆呀，各种各样。凡是出过力、做过这些你用来搭城的东西的人，也全在这儿。明白不？*做*，这才是要紧的。哪怕只是个摇砂轮、给刻橱柜小件的刀子磨刃的小学徒，或者是个摘起绒草、给粘在棋子底下的小布片收口的娃娃——他们全在这儿。他们就是你这些城里所谓的『居民』。」

「我明白了。他们也变小了，跟我一样。」菲利普说。

===Original===
“Or the cities has got big,” said the carpenter; “it comes to the same thing. I wish you wouldn’t interrupt, Master Philip. You put me out.”

“I won’t again,” said Philip. “Only do tell me just one thing. How can you be here and at Amblehurst too?”

“We come here,” said the carpenter slowly, “when we’re asleep.”

“Oh!” said Philip, deeply disappointed; “it’s just a dream then?”

“Not it. We come here when we’re too sound asleep to dream. You go through the dreams and come out on the other side where everything’s real. That’s *here*.”

===Chinese===
「要么就是城变大了，」木匠说，「反正一回事。菲利普少爷，你别打岔行不行。你把俺的话头都搅断了。」

「我不再打岔了，」菲利普说，「只是请您告诉我一件事就行。您怎么能既在这儿，又在安布尔赫斯特（Amblehurst）呢？」

「我们到这儿来，」木匠慢吞吞地说，「是在睡着的时候。」

「哦！」菲利普说，失望透了，「那这不过是一场梦？」

「不是梦。我们是在睡得太沉、沉得连梦都不做的时候到这儿来的。你一路穿过那些梦，从另一头钻出来，那边的样样东西都是真的。*这儿*就是那一边。」

===Original===
“Go on,” said Philip.

“I dunno where I was. You do put me out so.”

“Pop you something or other,” said Philip.

“Population. Yes. Well, all those people as made the things you made the cities of, they live in the cities and they’ve made the insides to the houses.”

“What do they do?”

“Oh, they just live here. And they buy and sell and plant gardens and work and play like everybody does in other cities. And when they go to sleep they go slap through their dreams and into the other world, and work and play there, see? That’s how it goes on. There’s a lot more, but that’s enough for one time. You get on with your gooseberries.”

===Chinese===
「接着讲。」菲利普说。

「俺都不记得讲到哪儿了。你可真会搅和俺。」

「人口什么的吧。」菲利普说。

「居民。对喽。这么说吧，凡是做过你搭城用的那些东西的人，全住在城里，房子里的里里外外也都是他们置办出来的。」

「他们都做些什么呢？」

「哦，就是过日子呗。他们做买卖、开园子、干活、玩耍，跟别的城里人一个样。等他们睡着了，就一头穿过各自的梦，进到另一个世界里，在那边接着干活接着玩，明白不？就是这么接着茬儿的。还有好多话，一回讲这些够多的了。你快吃你的醋栗吧。」

===Original===
“But they aren’t all real people, are they? There’s Mr. Noah?”

“Ah, those is aristocracy, the ones you put in when you built the cities. They’re our old families. Very much respected. They’re all very high up in the world. Came over with the Conker, as the saying is. There’s the Noah family. They’re the oldest of all, of course. And the dolls you’ve put in different times and the tin soldiers, and of course all the Noah’s ark animals is alive except when you used them for building, and then they’re statues.”

“But I don’t see,” said Philip, “I really don’t see how all these cities that I built at different times can still be here, all together and all going on at once, when I know they’ve all been pulled down.”

“Well, I’m no scholard. But I did hear Mr. Noah say once in a lecture﻿—*he’s* a speaker, if you like﻿—I heard him say it was like when you take a person’s photo. The person is so many inches thick through and so many feet high and he’s round and he’s solid. But in the photo he’s *flat*. Because everything’s flat in photos. But all the same it’s him right enough. You get him into the photo. Then all you’ve got to do is to get ’im out again into where everything’s thick and tall and round and solid. And it’s quite easy, I believe, once you know the trick.”

===Chinese===
「可他们不全都是真人吧？还有诺亚先生（Mr. Noah）呢？」

「啊，那些是贵族，是你搭城的时候亲手放进去的。他们是我们的世家老户，备受敬重，个个都地位高得很。俗话说，是跟着『征服王』一块儿过来的。诺亚家就是一家，当然是所有家里最老的。还有你各个时期放进去的娃娃、锡兵，当然还有诺亚方舟（Noah's Ark）里的全部牲畜——它们都活着，只有被你拿来搭房子的时候，才变成雕像。」

「可我不明白，」菲利普说，「我真的不明白，我明明知道这些城后来都拆掉了，它们怎么还能都在这儿，凑在一块儿，同时还各自热热闹闹地过活着。我搭它们可是搭在不同的时候啊。」

「这个嘛，俺肚子里没多少墨水。不过俺确实听诺亚先生在一次演讲里说过——*他*才叫会讲话呢——俺听他说，这就跟给人照相一个理儿。人本来有几英寸厚、几英尺高，圆滚滚、结结实实的。可照片上的他*是扁的*。因为照片里样样东西都是扁的。但那千真万确还是他本人。你把他装进了照片里。接下来你只消再把他弄出来，放回那个样样东西都又厚又高又圆又结实的地方就行。俺琢磨着，只要摸着了窍门，这事儿容易得很。」

===Original===
“Stop,” said Philip suddenly. “I think my head’s going to burst.”

“Ah!” said the carpenter kindly. “I felt like that at first. Lie down and try to sleep it off a bit. Eddication does go to your head something crool. I’ve often noticed it.”

And indeed Philip was quite glad to lie down among the long grass and be covered up with the carpenter’s coat. He fell asleep at once.

An hour later he woke again, looked at the wrinkled-apple face of Mr. Perrin and began to remember.

===Chinese===
「打住，」菲利普猛地说，「我觉得我的脑袋要裂了。」

「啊！」木匠和气地说，「俺起先也这样。躺下睡一觉，把它睡散了就好。学问这东西确实往脑袋里钻，钻得厉害着呢。俺常瞧见这样。」

菲利普也确实乐意躺进深深的草丛里，让木匠的外衣盖在身上。他一下子就睡着了。

一个钟头后他又醒了，看见佩林先生那张皱皮苹果似的脸，渐渐想起了先前的事。

===Original===
“I’m glad *you’re* here anyhow,” he said to the carpenter; “it was horribly lonely. You don’t know.”

“That’s why I was sent to meet you,” said Mr. Perrin simply.

“But how did you know?”

“Mr. Noah sent for me early this morning. Bless you, he knows all about everything. Says he, ‘You go and meet ’im and tell ’im all you can. If he wants to be a Deliverer, let ’im,’ says Mr. Noah.”

===Chinese===
「反正*有你*在真好，」他对木匠说，「先前冷清得吓人。你不知道那是什么滋味。」

「所以才派俺来接你呀。」佩林先生直截了当地说。

「可您怎么知道的？」

「诺亚先生一大早就打发人叫俺去。保佑你，他什么事都知道。他说：『你去接他，能告诉他的都告诉他。他要想当解救者（Deliverer），就让他当。』诺亚先生是这么说的。」

===Original===
“But how do you begin being a Deliverer?” Philip asked, sitting up and feeling suddenly very grand and manly, and very glad that Lucy was not there to interfere.

“There’s lots of different ways,” said Mr. Perrin. “Your particular way’s simple. You just got to kill the dragon.”

“A *live* dragon?”

“Live!” said Mr. Perrin. “Why he’s all over the place and as green as grass he is. Lively as a kitten. He’s got a broken spear sticking out of his side, so someone must have had a try at baggin’ him, some time or another.”

===Chinese===
「可当解救者从哪儿开头呢？」菲利普问，一边坐起身来，忽然觉得自己又气派又有男子气，还庆幸露西不在跟前碍事。

「法子多得很，各有各的路数，」佩林先生说，「你这条特别的路子简单。你只消把那条龙宰了就成。」

「一条*活的*龙？」

「活的！」佩林先生说，「嗐，它满世界都是，绿得跟草似的。欢实得像只小猫。它肋上还戳着半截断掉的矛呢，可见总有谁在哪年哪月试过想拿住它。」

===Original===
“Don’t you think,” said Philip, a little overcome by this vivid picture, “that perhaps I’d better look for Lucy first, and be a Deliverer afterwards?”

“If you’re *afraid*,” said Mr. Perrin.

“I’m not,” said Philip doubtfully.

“You see,” said the carpenter, “what you’ve got to consider is: are you going to be the hero of this ’ere adventure or ain’t you? You can’t ’ave it both ways. An’ if you are, you may’s well make up your mind, cause killing a dragon ain’t the end of it, not by no means.”

===Chinese===
「您不觉得吗，」菲利普说，这幅活灵活现的图景让他有点胆怯，「我最好还是先去找露西，之后再当解救者？」

「你要是*怕*了的话。」佩林先生说。

「我才不怕。」菲利普说，语气不大笃定。

「你要想明白，」木匠说，「你掂量掂量的是：这场历险你到底当不当英雄？两头的好事不能全占。你要当呢，就索性横下心来，因为宰一条龙可不是完事儿，差得远呢。」

===Original===
“Do you mean there are more dragons?”

“Not dragons,” said the carpenter soothingly; “not dragons exactly. But there. I don’t want to lower your heart. If you kills the dragon, then afterwards there’s six more hard things you’ve got to do. And then they make you king. Take it or leave it. Only, if you take it we’d best be starting. And anyhow we may as well get a move on us, because at sundown the dragon comes out to drink and exercise of himself. You can hear him rattling all night among these ’ere ruins; miles off you can ’ear ’im of a still night.”

“Suppose I don’t want to be a Deliverer,” said Philip slowly.

“Then you’ll be a Destroyer,” said the carpenter; “there’s only these two situations vacant here at present. Come, Master Philip, sir, don’t talk as if you wasn’t going to be a man and do your duty for England, Home and Beauty, like it says in the song. Let’s be starting, shall us?”

===Chinese===
「您是说还有别的龙？」

「倒不是龙，」木匠宽慰他说，「不全是龙。不过嘛，俺也不想给你泄劲。你要是宰了这条龙，往后还有六件难事等着你。都办成了，他们就立你为王。干不干随你。只是你要是干，咱们最好这就动身。反正咱们也该紧着点儿，因为太阳一落山，那龙就要出来喝水、遛它自己了。夜里你能听见它在这一片废墟里哗啦哗啦地响；夜静的时候，隔着几里地都听得见。」

「要是我不想当解救者呢。」菲利普慢吞吞地说。

「那你就得当毁灭者（Destroyer），」木匠说，「眼下这里空缺的职位就这两个。来吧，菲利普少爷，别说话说得好像你不想当条汉子、不想为了英格兰、家园和美人尽本分似的，歌里就是这么唱的。咱们这就动身，好不好？」

===Original===
“You think I ought to be the Deliverer?”

“Ought stands for nothing,” said Mr. Perrin. “I think you’re a going to *be* the Deliverer; that’s what I think. Come on!”

As they rose to go, Philip had a brief fleeting vision of a very smart lady in a motor veil, disappearing round the corner of a pillar.

“Are there many motors about here?” he asked, not wishing to talk any more about dragons just then.

===Chinese===
「您觉得我应该当解救者？」

「应该不应该管什么用，」佩林先生说，「俺寻思你是*注定*要当解救者的；俺是这么看的。走吧！」

两人起身要走时，菲利普眼角里飞快闪过一个影子：一位罩着汽车面纱的时髦女士，绕过柱子拐角不见了。

「这一带汽车多吗？」他问，那会儿他不想再谈龙的事。

===Original===
“Not a single one,” said Mr. Perrin unexpectedly. “Nor yet phonographs, nor railways, nor factory chimneys, nor none of them loud ugly things. Nor yet advertisements, nor newspapers, nor barbed wire.”

After that the two walked silently away from the ruin. Philip was trying to feel as brave and confident as a Deliverer should. He reminded himself of St. George. And he remembered that the hero *never* fails to kill the dragon. But he still felt a little uneasy. It takes some time to accustom yourself to being a hero. But he could not help looking over his shoulder every now and then to see if the dragon was coming. So far it wasn’t.

“Well,” said Mr. Perrin as they drew near a square tower with a long flight of steps leading up to it, “what do you say?”

===Chinese===
「一辆也没有，」佩林先生的回答出人意料，「留声机也没有，铁路也没有，工厂烟囱也没有，那些吵人的丑家伙一概没有。广告没有，报纸没有，铁丝网也没有。」

此后两人便默默地离开废墟往前走。菲利普努力想让自己拿出解救者该有的胆量和信心。他拿圣乔治来给自己打气，也想起英雄*从来*不会宰龙失手。可他心里还是有点发虚。要把自己磨练成英雄，总得些时日。他还是忍不住不时回头张望，看龙追上来没有。到目前为止还没有。

「喏，」佩林先生说——这当口他们走近了一座方塔，塔前一道长长的台阶直通塔门，「你怎么说？」

===Original===
“I wasn’t saying anything,” said Philip.

“I mean are you going to be the Deliverer?”

Then something in Philip’s heart seemed to swell, and a choking feeling came into his throat, and he felt more frightened than he had ever felt before, as he said, looking as brave as he could:

“Yes. I am.”

===Chinese===
「我什么也没说啊。」菲利普说。

「我是问你到底当不当解救者？」

这时菲利普心里仿佛有什么东西涨了起来，喉咙口一阵发堵，他感到从未有过的害怕。他开了口，尽量装出勇敢的样子：

「当。我当。」

===Original===
Perrin clapped his hands.

And instantly from the doors of the tower and from behind it came dozens of people, and down the long steps, alone, came Mr. Noah, moving with careful dignity and carrying his yellow mat neatly rolled under his arm. All the people clapped their hands, till Mr. Noah, standing on the third step, raised his hands to command silence.

“Friends,” he said, “and fellow-citizens of Polistopolis, you see before you one who says that he is the Deliverer. He was yesterday arrested and tried as a trespasser, and condemned to imprisonment. He escaped and you all assumed that he was the Destroyer in disguise. But now he has returned and of his own free will he chooses to attempt the accomplishment of the seven great deeds. And the first of these is the killing of the great green dragon.”

The people, who were a mixed crowd of all nations, cheered loudly.

===Chinese===
佩林拍了拍手。

眨眼间，从塔门里、从塔背后涌出来好几十号人；长台阶上，诺亚先生独自一人缓步走下，举止庄重而讲究，胳膊底下整整齐齐夹着他那张卷好的黄垫子。众人都鼓起掌来，直到诺亚先生站在第三级台阶上，抬手示意肃静。

「朋友们，」他说，「波利斯托波利斯（Polistopolis）的同胞公民们，你们眼前站着一位自称解救者的人。他昨天被当作擅入者抓起来审判，判了监禁。他逃走了，你们大家都认定他是乔装的毁灭者。可如今他自己回来了，并且出于自由意志，选择去尝试完成那七项伟大的功绩。这第一项，就是宰掉那条绿色的大龙。」

这些人是一支各族混杂的队伍，此刻大声欢呼起来。

===Original===
“So now,” said Mr. Noah, “we will make him our knight.”

“Kneel,” said Mr. Noah, “in token of fealty to the Kingdom of Cities.”

Philip knelt.

“You shall now speak after me,” said Mr. Noah solemnly. “Say what I say,” he whispered, and Philip said it.

===Chinese===
「那么现在，」诺亚先生说，「我们要封他做我们的骑士。」

「跪下，」诺亚先生说，「以示对众城之国（the Kingdom of Cities）效忠。」

菲利普跪下了。

「现在你要跟着我念，」诺亚先生庄严地说，「我说什么你说什么。」他低声叮嘱，菲利普便照着说了。

===Original===
This was it. “I, Philip, claim to be the Deliverer of this great nation, and I pledge myself to carry out the seven great deeds that shall prove my claim to the Deliverership and the throne. I pledge my honour to be the champion of this city, and the enemy of its Destroyer.”

When Philip had said this, Mr. Noah drew forth a bright silver-hilted sword and held it over him.

“You must be knighted,” he said; “those among my audience who have read any history will be aware that no mere commoner can expect to conquer a dragon. We must give our would-be Deliverer every chance. So I will make him a knight.” He tapped Philip lightly on the shoulder and said, “Rise up, Sir Philip!”

This was really grand, and Philip felt new courage as Mr. Noah handed him the silver sword, and all the people cheered.

===Chinese===
誓词是这样的：「我，菲利普，声称为这个伟大民族的解救者，我立誓完成那七项伟大功绩，以证明我对解救者之位和王位的权利。我以名誉起誓，做这座城市的捍卫者，做它的毁灭者的仇敌。」

菲利普念完，诺亚先生抽出一柄亮闪闪的银柄剑，举在他头顶上。

「你必须受封为骑士，」他说，「我的听众里凡是读过点历史的都知道，一个平平常常的平民可别指望降服恶龙。我们必须给我们这位未来的解救者一切机会。所以我要封他为骑士。」他在菲利普肩上轻轻一拍，说道：「起来吧，菲利普爵士（Sir Philip）！」

这可真够气派的。诺亚先生把银剑递到他手里，众人齐声欢呼，菲利普只觉勇气倍增。

===Original===
But as the cheers died down, a thin and disagreeable voice suddenly said:

“But *I* claim to be the Deliverer too.”

It was like a thunderbolt. Everyone stopped cheering and stood with mouth open and head turned towards the person who had spoken. And the person who had spoken was the smartly dressed lady in the motor veil, whom Philip had seen among the ruins.

“A trespasser! a trespasser!” cried the crowd; “to prison with it!” and angry, threatening voices began to arise.

===Chinese===
可就在欢呼声渐渐平息时，一个尖细刺耳的声音突然开了腔：

「可是*我*也声称自己是解救者。」

这话犹如晴天霹雳。人人都不欢呼了，张着嘴，扭头朝说话的人望去。说话的正是那位衣着时髦、罩着汽车面纱的女士，菲利普方才在废墟里瞥见过的就是她。

「擅入者！擅入者！」人群喊道，「把她抓进牢里！」愤怒的、威胁的嚷嚷声四下里起来了。

===Original===
“I’m no more a trespasser than he is,” said the voice, “and if I say I am the Deliverer, you can’t stop me. I can kill dragons or do anything *he* can do.”

“Silence, trespasser,” said Mr. Noah, with cold dignity. “You should have spoken earlier. At present Sir Philip occupies the position of candidate to the post of King-Deliverer. There is no other position open to you except that of Destroyer.”

“But suppose the boy doesn’t do it?” said the voice behind the veil.

“True,” said Mr. Noah. “You may if you choose, occupy for the present the position of Pretender-in-Chief to the Claimancy of the Deliverership, an office now and here created expressly for you. The position of Claimant to the Destroyership is also,” he added reflectively, “open to you.”

===Chinese===
「我跟他一样，算不得什么擅入者，」那声音说，「只要我说自己是解救者，你们拦不住我。他能杀龙，他能干什么*我*就能干什么。」

「住口，擅入者，」诺亚先生说，带着冷冷的威严，「你早该开口。眼下菲利普爵士占据着解救者国王之位的候选席位。除毁灭者之外，再没有别的位子对你敞开。」

「可要是那孩子办不成呢？」面纱后面的声音说。

「也是，」诺亚先生说，「那么你若愿意，可以暂且就任『解救者名位之首席僭称官』，此职此刻此地专为你而设。毁灭者名位的申请席位，」他若有所思地补了一句，「也同样对你敞开。」

===Original===
“Then if he doesn’t do it,” said the veiled lady, “I can be the Deliverer.”

“You can try,” said Mr. Noah. “There are a special set of tasks to be performed if the claimant to the Deliverership be a woman.”

“What are they?” said the veiled lady.

“If Sir Philip fails you will be duly instructed in the deeds required of a Deliverer who is a woman. And now, my friends, let us retire and leave Sir Philip to deal with the dragon. We shall watch anxiously from yonder ramparts,” he added encouragingly.

===Chinese===
「那要是他办不成，」蒙面女士说，「我就能当解救者。」

「你可以一试，」诺亚先生说，「倘若解救者名位的申请人是个女子，另有一套专门的功绩要完成。」

「是哪些？」蒙面女士问。

「等菲利普爵士失败之后，自会有人把女子解救者应做的功绩一条条教给你。现在，朋友们，我们且退下，把恶龙留给菲利普爵士对付。我们会焦急地在那边城垒上守望。」他又鼓劲似的补了一句。

===Original===
“But isn’t anyone to help me?” said Philip, deeply uneasy.

“It is not usual,” said Mr. Noah, “for champions to require assistance with dragons.”

“I should think not indeed,” said the veiled lady; “but you’re not going the usual way about it at all. Where’s the princess, I should like to know?”

“There isn’t any princess,” said Mr. Noah.

===Chinese===
「就没人帮帮我吗？」菲利普说，心里七上八下。

「按惯例，」诺亚先生说，「捍卫者对付恶龙，是用不着帮手的。」

「可不是嘛，」蒙面女士说，「可你们这套压根儿就不按惯例来。我倒要问问，公主在哪儿？」

「这里没有什么公主。」诺亚先生说。

===Original===
“Then it won’t be a proper dragon-killing,” she said, with an angry shaking of skirts; “that’s all I can say.”

“I wish it *was* all,” said Mr. Noah to himself.

“If there isn’t a princess it isn’t fair,” said the veiled one; “and I shall consider it’s my turn to be Deliverer.”

“Be silent, woman,” said Mr. Noah.

===Chinese===
「那这屠龙就不成体统，」她说，气得裙子直抖，「我只能说这么多了。」

「我倒真希望你就说这么多了。」诺亚先生暗自想道。

「没有公主就不公平，」蒙面的那位说，「那我就要认为，这回轮到我当解救者了。」

「安静，女人。」诺亚先生说。

===Original===
“Woman, indeed,” said the lady. “I ought to have a proper title.”

“Your title is the Pretender to the﻿—”

“I know,” she interrupted; “but you forget you’re speaking to a lady. You can call me the Pretenderette.”

===Chinese===
「什么『女人』，」那位女士说，「我该有个像样的称号。」

「你的称号是僭位者……」

「我知道，」她打断道，「可您忘了，您是在对一位女士说话。您可以叫我女僭王（Pretenderette）。」

===Original===
Mr. Noah turned coldly from her and pressed two Roman candles and a box of matches into Philip’s hand.

“When you have arranged your plans and are quite sure that you will be able to kill the dragon, light one of these. We will then have a princess in readiness, and on observing your signal will tie her to a tree, or, since this is a district where trees are rare and buildings frequent, to a pillar. She will be perfectly safe if you make your plans correctly. And in any case you must not attempt to deal with the dragon without first lighting the Roman candle.”

“And the dragon will see it and go away.”

“Exactly,” said Mr. Noah. “Or perhaps he will see it and not go away. Time alone will show. The task that is without difficulties can never really appeal to a hero. You will find weapons, cords, nets, shields and various first aids to the young dragon-catcher in the vaults below this tower. Good evening, Sir Philip,” he ended warmly. “We wish you every success.”

===Chinese===
诺亚先生冷冷地转过身去不再理她，把两支罗马烛光烟花和一盒火柴塞进菲利普手里。

「等你安排好计划、确有把握能宰掉那龙的时候，就点燃一支。我们这边会备好一位公主，一见你的信号，就把她绑到树上去——不过此地树少建筑多，那就绑到柱子上。只要你的计划订得妥当，她保管安然无恙。而且无论如何，不先点燃罗马烛光烟花，你绝不可贸然去对付恶龙。」

「那龙一看见烟花就会走开。」

「正是，」诺亚先生说，「也或许它看见了却不走。只有时间能见分晓。毫无困难的差事，从来入不了真正的英雄的法眼。在这座塔的地窖里，你会找到兵器、绳索、罗网、盾牌，还有各式各样帮衬年轻捕龙人的家伙什。晚安，菲利普爵士，」他临了热络地说，「祝你马到成功。」

===Original===
And with that the whole crowd began to go away.

“*I* know who you ought to have for princess,” the Pretenderette said as they went. And Mr. Noah said:

“Silence in court.”

“This isn’t a court,” said the Pretenderette aggravatingly.

===Chinese===
说罢，整群人便开始散去。

「*我*知道你们该挑谁当公主。」人群走的时候，女僭王说。诺亚先生答道：

「法庭之上，肃静。」

「这又不是法庭。」女僭王气人的话头又来了。

===Original===
“Wherever justice is, is a court,” said Mr. Noah, “and I accuse you of contempt of it. Guards, arrest this person and take her to prison at once.”

There was a scuffling and a shrieking and then the voices withdrew gradually, the angry voice of even the Pretenderette growing fainter and fainter till it died away altogether.

Philip was left alone.

===Chinese===
「哪里有正义，哪里就是法庭，」诺亚先生说，「我指控你藐视法庭。卫兵们，拿下此人，即刻押入大牢。」

一阵扭打，一声尖叫，随后人声渐渐远去，连女僭王那怒气冲冲的嗓音也越来越低、越来越低，最后完全消失了。

只剩下菲利普一个人。

===Original===
His first act was to go up to the top of the tower and look out to see if he could see the dragon. He looked east and north and south and west, and he saw the ramparts of the fort where Mr. Noah and the others were now safely bestowed. He saw also other towers and cities in the distance, and he saw the ruins where he had met Mr. Perrin.
