And how on earth shall I get away from her?” He had not seen the spears and the quicksands and the molten metal, and he was waiting unhappily for her to alight, and for a game of hide and seek to begin, which he was not at all anxious to play.

Even as he wondered, the Hippogriff spread wings and flew away. And Philip was left alone on the island. But what did that matter? It was much better to be alone than with that Pretenderette. And for Philip there were no white-hot metal and spears and snares of quicksand, only dewy grass and sweet flowers and trees and safety and delight.

“If only Lucy were here,” he said.

===Chinese===
「好一个歹毒的地方。」女僭王说，「简直不知道还能挑个更歹毒的地方，把那个淘气孩子丢在这儿正合适。等我派人来提他回监狱的时候，他就该知道谁是主人了。喂，说你呢，有多快飞多快，回波利斯托波利斯去。听见没有？呃，我是说，请吧。」她补了一句，然后念出了那个魔法字眼。

菲利普正躲在近旁的灌木丛里偷看，他听见了那个魔法字眼（我可不敢告诉你们是什么），也头一回看清了女僭王的脸。他在灌木丛的藏身处浑身发抖、直打寒战。因为这位女僭王，是菲利普平生见过的唯一一个真正叫人讨厌的人。她就是露西的保姆，那个穿灰裙子、长着一双肥大脚板的保姆，就是她对他那么凶，还推倒了他的城市。

「她到底是*怎么*到*这儿*来的？」菲利普心里纳闷，「我又到底怎么才能躲开她呢？」他没有看见那些钢矛、流沙和金属熔液，正闷闷不乐地等她落地，等着一场捉迷藏开始——这游戏他可一点都不想玩。

他正想着，骏鹰已展翅飞走了。菲利普被独自留在了岛上。可这有什么要紧？独自一人，总比跟那个女僭王在一起强得多。而且在菲利普眼里，这里没有白热的金属、钢矛和流沙陷阱，只有带露的青草、芬芳的花木、安全与欢欣。

「要是露西也在就好了。」他说。

===Original===
When he was quite sure that the Pretenderette was really gone, he came out and explored the island. It had on it every kind of flower and fruit that you can think of, all growing together. There were gold oranges and white orange flowers, pink apple-blossom and red apples, cherries and cherry-blossom, strawberry flowers and strawberries, all growing together, wild and sweet.

At the back of his mind Philip remembered that he had, at some time or other, heard of an island where fruit and blossoms grew together at the same time, but that was all he could remember. He passed through the lovely orchards and came to a lake. It was frozen. And he remembered that, in the island he had heard of, there was a lake ready for skating even when the flowers and fruit were on the trees. Then he came to a little summerhouse built all of porcupine quills like Helen’s pen-box.

And then he knew. All these wonders were on the island that he and Helen had invented long ago﻿—the island that she used to draw maps of.

===Chinese===
等他确信女僭王真的走了，他才出来，把这座岛探了个遍。你叫得出名字的花果，岛上应有尽有，而且全都长在一起。有金黄的橙子和雪白的橙花，粉红的苹果花和红艳艳的苹果，樱桃和樱花，草莓花和草莓，全都长在一处，透着野趣与甜美。

菲利普心底里模模糊糊记起来，他从前好像听说过一座岛，岛上的果实和花同一时间生长开放，可他也只记得这么多。他穿过可爱的果园，来到一片湖边。湖面结着冰。他记起来了，在他听说过的那座岛上，有一片随时可以滑冰的湖，哪怕树上正开着花、结着果。接着他来到一座小凉亭前，亭子通体用豪猪刺搭成，就像海伦的笔盒一样。

这下他全明白了。这些奇景，全在他和海伦很久以前一起发明的这座岛上——就是她过去常画地图的那座岛。

===Original===
“It’s our very own island,” he said, and a glorious feeling of being at home glowed through him, warm and delightful. “We said no one else might come here! That’s why the Pretenderette couldn’t land. And why they call it the Island-where-you-mayn’t-go. I’ll find the bun tree and have something to eat, and then I’ll go to the boathouse and get out the *Lightning Loose* and go back for Lucy. I do wish I could bring her here. But of course I can’t without asking Helen.”

The *Lightning Loose* was the magic yacht Helen had invented for the island.

He soon found a bush whose fruit was buns, and a jam-tart tree grew near it. You have no idea how nice jam tarts can taste till you have gathered them yourself, fresh and sticky, from the tree. They are as sticky as horse-chestnut buds, and much nicer to eat.

As he went towards the boathouse he grew happier and happier, recognising, one after the other, all the places he and Helen had planned and marked on the map. He passed by the marble and gold house with “King’s Palace” painted on the door. He longed to explore it: but the thought of Lucy drove him on. As he went down a narrow leafy woodland path towards the boathouse, he passed the door of the dear little thatched cottage (labelled “Queen’s Palace” which was the house Helen had insisted that she liked best for her very own.

“How pretty it is; I wish Helen was here,” he said; “she helped to make it. I should never have thought of it without her. She ought to be here,” he said. With that he felt very lonely, all of a sudden, and very sad. And as he went on, wondering whether in all this magic world there might not somehow be some magic strong enough to bring Helen there to see the island that was their very own, and to give her consent to his bringing Lucy to it, he turned a corner in the woodland path, and walked straight into the arms of﻿—Helen.

===Chinese===
「这是我们完完全全自己的岛。」他说。一种回家般的美妙感觉涌遍全身，暖洋洋、喜滋滋。「我们说过，别人谁也不许来！所以女僭王才落不了地，所以大家才管它叫不许去的岛。我去找那棵小圆面包树，弄点吃的，然后到船屋去，把「闪电自由号」（the Lightning Loose）开出来，回去接露西。我真希望能把她带到这儿来。不过当然啦，不问过海伦，我可不能这么做。」

「闪电自由号」是海伦为这座岛发明的魔法游艇。

他很快就找到了一丛结小圆面包的灌木，旁边还长着一棵果酱挞树。你要是自己不从树上摘过新鲜黏手的果酱挞，就想象不出它能有多好吃。它们跟七叶树的嫩芽一样黏糊糊，滋味可好吃多啦。

他朝船屋走去，一路越来越快活，把他和海伦当初规划过、在地图上标注过的地方一一认了出来。他经过那座大理石加黄金的房子，门上写着「王宫」。他真想进去逛个痛快，可一想到露西，脚下便不停留。他顺着一条绿荫夹道的小径往船屋去，路过一栋可爱小茅屋的门口（门牌写着「王后宫」，这栋屋子，海伦当年一口咬定她最喜欢，要完全归她自己。

「多美啊；真希望海伦就在这儿。」他说，「这岛也有她一份，没有她我根本想不出这些。她本该在这儿来的。」他说着，忽然感到说不出的孤单，说不出的难过。他一路走着，心里琢磨着，在这整个魔法世界里，会不会偏偏有一种魔法，强大到足以把海伦带到这儿来，亲眼看看这座完完全全属于他们俩的岛，再点头答应他带露西同来。就在这时，他在林间小径上拐过一个弯，一头撞进了一双臂弯里——是海伦。

## IX On the *Lightning Loose* / 乘着「闪电自由号」

===Original===
“But how did you get here?” said Philip in Helen’s arms on the island.

“I just walked out at the other side of a dream,” she said; “how could I not come, when the door was open and you wanted me so?”

And Philip just said, “Oh, Helen!” He could not find any other words, but Helen understood. She always did.

“Come,” she said, “shall we go to your Palace or mine? I want my supper, and we’ll have our own little blue-and-white tea-set. Yes, I know you’ve had your supper, but it’ll be fun getting mine, and perhaps you’ll be hungry again before we’ve got it.”

They went to the thatched cottage that was Helen’s palace, because Philip had had almost as much of large buildings as he wanted for a little while. The cottage had a wide chimney and an open hearth; and they sat on the hearth and made toast, and Philip almost forgot that he had ever had any adventures and that the toast was being made on a hearth whose blue wood-smoke curled up among the enchanting treetops of a magic island.

And before they went to bed he had told her all about everything.

===Chinese===
「可是你是怎么到这儿来的？」在岛上，菲利普坐在海伦怀里问道。

「我不过是从梦的另一头走了出来，」她说，「门开着，你又这么盼着我来，我怎么能不来呢？」

菲利普只说了一句：「哦，海伦！」他找不出别的言语，可海伦懂。她一向都懂。

「来吧，」她说，「去你的宫殿还是我的？我要吃晚饭了，咱们用自己那套蓝白小茶具。对，我知道你吃过晚饭了，可给我张罗晚饭多有意思呀，说不定等饭菜摆好，你又饿了。」

他们去了海伦的宫殿——那栋茅草小屋，因为一时之间，菲利普对高堂大屋已经享用得够够的了。小屋有宽宽的烟囱和敞开的壁炉；他们坐在炉边烤面包片，菲利普几乎忘了自己还经历过什么冒险，也忘了这面包片是在一座魔法小岛迷人的树梢间盘旋着蓝色柴烟的壁炉上烤出来的。

上床睡觉前，他把一切原原本本都讲给了她听。

===Original===
“Oh, I am so glad you came!” he said over and over again; “it is so easy to tell you *here*, with all the magic going on. I don’t think I ever *could* have told you at the Grange with the servants all about, and the﻿—I mean Mr. Graham, and all the things as not magic as they could possibly be. Oh, Helen! where *is* Mr. Graham; won’t he hate your coming away from him?”

“He’s gone through a dream door too,” she said, “to see Lucy. Only he doesn’t know he’s really gone. He’ll think it’s a dream, and he’ll tell me about it when we both wake up.”

“When did you go to sleep?” said Philip.

“At Brussels. That telegram hasn’t come yet.”

“I don’t understand about time,” said Philip firmly, “and I never shall. I say, Helen, I was just looking for the *Lightning Loose*, to go off in her on a voyage of discovery and find Lucy.”

===Chinese===
「哦，你来了我真是太高兴了！」他翻来覆去地说，「在*这儿*跟你讲这些多容易呀，满世界都是魔法。我看在格兰奇庄园我*绝对*讲不出口，仆人们到处都是，还有那个——我是说格雷厄姆先生，还有那些非魔法得不能再非魔法的东西。哦，海伦！格雷厄姆先生*到底*在哪儿？你这样离开他，他不会气坏吗？」

「他也从一扇梦之门过来了，」她说，「去看露西。只是他不知道自己是真过去了。他会以为那是一场梦，等我们俩都醒了，他会讲给我听的。」

「你是什么时候睡着的？」菲利普问。

「在布鲁塞尔。那封电报还没到呢。」

「时间这种事我搞不懂，」菲利普斩钉截铁地说，「永远也搞不懂。我说海伦，我正要去找「闪电自由号」（the Lightning Loose），驾着它来一场发现之旅，找露西去。」

===Original===
“I don’t think you need,” she said; “I met a parrot on the island just before I met you and it was saying poetry to itself.”

“It would be,” said Philip, “if it was alive. I’m glad it *is* alive, though. What was it saying?”

“It was something like this,” she said, putting a log of wood on the fire:

> “Philip and Helen
 

 Have the island to dwell in,
 

 Hooray.
 

 They said of the island,
 

 ‘It’s your land and my land!’
 

 Hooray. Hooray. Hooray.
> “And till the ark
 

 Comes out of the dark
 

 There those two may stay
 

 For a happy while, and
 

 Enjoy their island
 

 Until the Giving Day.
 

 Hooray.
> “And then they will hear the giving voice,
 

 They will hear and obey,
 

 And when people come
 

 Who need a home,
 

 They’ll give the island away.
 

 Hooray.
> “The island with flower
 

 And fruit and bower,
 

 Forest and river and bay,
 

 Their very own island
 

 They’ll sigh and smile and
 

 They’ll give their island away.”

===Chinese===
「我看用不着，」她说，「遇见你之前，我刚好在岛上碰见一只鹦鹉，它正对自己念诗呢。」

「它肯定会念诗，」菲利普说，「要是它是活的话。不过它*确实*是活的，我很高兴。它念的什么？」

「大概是这么几句。」她说着，往火里添了块柴：

> 「菲利普和海伦
 

 有了岛来安身，
 

 万岁。
 

 他们曾说起这岛：
 

 『是你的地盘我的地盘！』
 

 万岁。万岁。万岁。
> 「等到方舟
 

 驶出黑暗，
 

 那两人尽可以留下，
 

 快活一阵子，然后
 

 享受他们的岛，
 

 直到赠予之日。
 

 万岁。
> 「那时他们会听见赠予之声，
 

 听见了就听从照办，
 

 等到有人前来，
 

 正需要一个家，
 

 他们就会把岛奉送。
 

 万岁。
> 「这岛上有花，
 

 有果，有凉亭，
 

 有森林、河流和海湾，
 

 他们完完全全自己的岛——
 

 他们会叹口气，笑一笑，
 

 把他们的岛奉送。」

===Original===
“What nonsense!” said Philip, “I never will.”

“All right, my Pipkin,” said Helen cheerfully; “I only told you just to show that you’re expected to stay here. ‘Philip and Helen have the island to dwell in.’ And now, what about bed?”

They spent a whole week on the island. It was exactly all that they could wish an island to be; because, of course, they had made it themselves, and of course they knew exactly what they wanted. I can’t describe that week. I only know that Philip will never forget it. Just think of all the things you could do on a magic island if you were there with your dearest dear, and you’ll know how Philip spent his time.

He enjoyed every minute of every hour of every day, and, best thing of all, that week made him understand, as nothing else could have done, that Helen still belonged to him, and that her marriage to Mr. Graham had not made her any the less Philip’s very own Helen.

===Chinese===
「胡说八道！」菲利普说，「我死也不给。」

「好啦，我的小皮普金（Pipkin），」海伦欢快地说，「我念给你听，只是想告诉你，你就该待在这儿。『菲利普和海伦有了岛来安身。』好了，该上床了吧？」

他们在岛上过了整整一个星期。那座岛正是他们能盼来的最好模样；因为岛本来就是他们自己造的，他们自然一清二楚自己要什么。那个星期我没法细讲。我只知道，菲利普一辈子也忘不了。你只要想想，要是你和自己最亲最爱的人待在一座魔法岛上，有多少事可以做，你就知道菲利普那个星期是怎么过的了。

他每一天的每个钟头的每一分钟都过得快活极了，而最妙的是，那个星期让他明白了一件任何别的东西都没法让他明白的事：海伦仍然属于他，她嫁给了格雷厄姆先生，也丝毫没有减少一分一毫——她依然是菲利普完完全全自己的海伦。

===Original===
And then came a day when Philip, swinging in a magnolia tree, looked out to sea and cried out, “A sail! a sail! Oh, Helen, here’s the ark! Now it’s all over. Let’s have Lucy to stay with us, and send the other people away,” he added, sliding down the tree-trunk with his face very serious.

“But we can’t, dear,” Helen reminded him. “The island’s ours, you know; and as long as it’s ours no one else can land on it. We made it like that, you know.”

“Then they can’t land?”

“No,” said Helen.

“Can’t we change the rule and let them land?”

“No,” said Helen.

===Chinese===
后来有一天，菲利普正荡着木兰树的枝条眺望大海，忽然喊了起来：「有帆！有帆！哦，海伦，方舟来了！这下全完了。咱们让露西留下来跟咱们住，把别的人统统打发走吧。」他滑下树干，脸上一本正经。

「可是不行呀，亲爱的。」海伦提醒他，「岛是咱们的，你知道；只要它归咱们，别人就上不了岸。当初咱们就是这么定的，你记得吧。」

「那他们就上不了岸了？」

「上不了。」海伦说。

「咱们不能改改规矩，让他们上岸吗？」

「不能。」海伦说。

===Original===
“Oh, it *is* a pity,” Philip said; “because the island is the place for islanders, isn’t it?”

“Yes,” said Helen, “and there’s no fear of the sea here; you remember we made it like that when we made the island?”

“Yes,” said Philip. “Oh, Helen, I *don’t* want to.”

“Then don’t,” said Helen.

“Ah, but I *do* want to, too.”

“Then do,” said she.

===Chinese===
「哦，真是太可惜了。」菲利普说，「岛本来就是给岛民住的地方呀，是不是？」

「是啊，」海伦说，「而且这儿没有海的恐惧；你还记得吧，造岛的时候咱们就是这么定的？」

「记得。」菲利普说，「哦，海伦，我*真不想*给。」

「那就不给。」海伦说。

「啊，可我又*确实*想给。」

「那就给。」她说。

===Original===
“But don’t you see, when you want to and don’t want to at the same time, what *are* you to do? There are so many things to think of.”

“When it’s like that, there’s one thing you mustn’t think of,” she said.

“What?” Philip asked.

“Yourself,” she said softly.

There was a silence, and then Philip suddenly hugged his sister and she hugged him.

“I’ll give it to them,” he said; “it’s no use. I know I ought to. I shall only be uncomfortable if I don’t.”

===Chinese===
「可你难道不明白，当你又想给又不想给的时候，*到底*该怎么办？要想的事情太多了。」

「到了这种时候，有一样东西你千万别去想。」她说。

「什么？」菲利普问。

「你自己。」她轻声说。

一阵沉默过后，菲利普突然搂住姐姐，姐姐也搂住了他。

「我给他们就是了。」他说，「不给也没用。我知道我该给。不给的话，我心里只会不好受。」

===Original===
Helen laughed. “My boy of boys!” she said. And then she looked sad. “Boy of my heart,” she said, “you know it’s not only giving up our island. If we give it away I must go. It’s the only place that there’s a door into out of my dreams.”

“I can’t let you go,” he said.

“But you’ve got your deeds to do,” she said, “and I can’t help you in those. Lucy can help you, but I can’t. You like Lucy now, don’t you?”

“Oh, I don’t mind her,” said Philip; “but it’s *you* I want, Helen.”

“Don’t think about that,” she urged. “Think what the islanders want. Think what it’ll be to them to have the island, to live here always, safe from the fear!”

“There are three more deeds,” said Philip dismally; “I don’t think I shall ever want any more adventures as long as I live.”

===Chinese===
海伦笑了。「我的好弟弟，天底下头一号的好弟弟！」她说。接着她神色黯淡下来。「我的心肝宝贝，」她说，「你知道，这不止是舍掉咱们这座岛。岛一送出去，我就得走了。我的梦里头，只有这地方开着一扇进得来的门。」

「我不能让你走。」他说。

「可你还有功绩要完成呀，」她说，「那些事我帮不上你。露西帮得上，我帮不上。你现在喜欢露西了，对不对？」

「哦，我不讨厌她。」菲利普说，「可我想要的是*你*呀，海伦。」

「别想这个。」她劝他，「想想岛民们要什么。想想这座岛对他们会是什么——从此世世代代住在这儿，再没有恐惧！」

「还有三件功绩呢。」菲利普蔫蔫地说，「我这辈子再也不想遇到什么冒险了。」

===Original===
“You’ll always want them,” she said, laughing at him gently, “always. And now let’s do the thing handsomely and give them a splendid welcome. Give me a kiss and then we’ll gather heaps of roses.”

So they kissed each other. But Philip was very unhappy indeed, though he felt that he was being rather noble and that Helen thought so too, which was naturally a great comfort.

There had been a good deal more of this talk than I have set down. Philip and Helen had hardly had time to hang garlands of pink roses along the quayside where the *Lightning Loose*, that perfect yacht, lay at anchor, before the blunt prow of the ark bumped heavily against the quayside﻿—and the two, dropping the rest of the roses, waved and smiled to the group on the ark’s terrace.

The first person to speak was Mr. Perrin, who shouted, “Here we are again!” like a clown.

Then Lucy said, “We know we can’t land, but the oracle said come and we came.” She leaned over the bulwark to whisper, “Who’s that perfect duck you’ve got with you?”

===Chinese===
「你一辈子都会想要的。」她温柔地笑着数落他，「一辈子都想。好了，咱们把这事情办得漂漂亮亮，给他们一个盛大的欢迎。亲我一下，然后咱们去采一大堆玫瑰。」

于是他们互相亲了亲。可菲利普心里实在难过，尽管他觉得自己这会儿相当崇高，海伦也这么觉得——这自然是个莫大的安慰。

这一番话，实际说的比我记下的多得多。菲利普和海伦刚沿着码头挂好粉红玫瑰的花环——那艘完美无缺的游艇「闪电自由号」正停泊在码头上——方舟那钝钝的船头就重重撞上了码头。两人丢下手里的玫瑰，朝方舟露台上的人群挥手微笑。

第一个开口的是佩林先生，他像马戏团小丑似的大喊：「我们又来啦！」

接着露西说：「我们知道上不了岸，可神谕说了要来，我们就来了。」她伏在舷墙上小声问：「你身边那位可人儿是谁呀？」

===Original===
Philip answered aloud:

“This is my sister Helen﻿—Helen this is Lucy.”

The two looked at each other, and then Helen held out her hands and she and Lucy kissed each other.

“I knew I should like you,” Lucy whispered, “but I didn’t know I should like you quite so much.”

Mr. Noah and Mr. Perrin were both bowing to Helen, a little stiffly but very cordially all the same, and quite surprisingly without surprise. And the Lord High Islander was looking at her with his own friendly jolly schoolboy grin.

===Chinese===
菲利普大声回答：

「这是我姐姐海伦——海伦，这是露西。」

两人对望了一眼，海伦伸出手来，她和露西互相亲了亲。

「我就知道我会喜欢你，」露西小声说，「可我不知道我会这么喜欢你。」

诺亚先生和佩林先生都向海伦鞠躬，动作有点僵硬，却十分热诚，而且出奇地毫不惊讶。至高岛主正望着她，脸上挂着他那友善快活的顽童式笑容。

===Original===
“If you will embark,” said Mr. Noah politely, “we can return to the mainland, and I will explain to you your remaining deeds.”

“Tell them, Pip,” said Helen.

“We don’t want to embark﻿—at present,” said Philip shyly. “We want you to land.”

“No one may land on the island save two,” said Mr. Noah. “I am glad you are the two. I feared one of the two might be the Pretenderette.”

“Not much,” said Philip. “It’s Helen’s and mine. We made it. And we want to give it to the islanders to keep. For their very own,” he added, feeling that it would be difficult for anyone to believe that such a glorious present was really being made just like that, without speeches, as if it had been a little present of a pencil sharpener or a peg-top.

===Chinese===
「要是两位肯登船，」诺亚先生彬彬有礼地说，「我们便可返回大陆，我再向您解释余下的功绩。」

「告诉他们吧，皮普。」海伦说。

「我们不想上船——至少现在不想。」菲利普害羞地说，「我们想请你们上岸。」

「除二人外，谁也不得在此岛登岸。」诺亚先生说，「我很高兴你们就是那两位。我还担心那两位里头有一个会是女僭王呢。」

「才不会呢。」菲利普说，「这岛是海伦和我的，是我们造的。我们想把它送给岛民们，永远归他们——完完全全归他们自己。」他补充道，心里明白任谁也很难相信，这样一份光彩夺目的大礼竟真是这么随随便便送出去的，连句致辞都没有，好像送的不过是一只卷笔刀或者一个陀螺。

===Original===
He was right.

“To keep?” said the Lord High Islander; “for our very own? Always?”

“Yes,” said Philip. “And there’s no fear here. You’ll *really* be ‘happy troops’ now.”

For a moment nobody said anything, though all the faces were expressive. Then the Lord High Islander spoke.

“Well,” he said, “of all the brickish bricks﻿—” and could say no more.

===Chinese===
他没说错。

「留着？」至高岛主说，「完完全全归我们自己？永远？」

「对。」菲利普说，「而且这儿没有恐惧。这下你们可*真正*是『快乐队伍』啦。」

有一小会儿谁也没说话，虽然每张脸都写满了心事。然后至高岛主开了口。

「哎呀，」他说，「您可真是好汉中的好汉、大好人里的大好人——」往后的话就说不出来了。

===Original===
“There are lots of houses,” said Philip, “and room for all the animals, and the island is thirty miles round, so there’s lots of room for the animals and everything.” He felt happier than he had ever done in his life. Giving presents is always enjoyable, and this was such a big and beautiful present, and he loved it so.

“I always did say Master Pip was a gentleman, and I always shall,” Mr. Perrin remarked.

“I congratulate you,” said Mr. Noah, “and I am happy to announce that your fifth deed is now accomplished. You remember our empty silver fruit-dishes? Your fifth deed was to be the supplying of Polistarchia with fruit. This island is the only place in the kingdom where fruit grows. The ark will serve to convey the fruit to the mainland, and the performance of this deed raises you to the rank of Duke.”

“Philip, you’re a dear,” said Lucy in a whisper.

“Shut up,” said Philip fiercely.

===Chinese===
「岛上房子多得很，」菲利普说，「装得下所有的动物。岛一圈有三十英里，动物们和一切东西都有的是地方。」他觉得这辈子从没这么快活过。送礼物总是件乐事，何况这份礼物这么大、这么美，又是他这么心爱的。

「我早就说过，皮普少爷是位绅士，我往后也一直这么说。」佩林先生评论道。

「恭喜您。」诺亚先生说，「我很高兴地宣布，您的第五项功绩现已完成。还记得我们那些空空的银果盘吗？您的第五项功绩，就是为波利斯托基亚供应水果。这岛是王国里唯一产水果的地方。方舟正好用来把水果运往大陆，而这件功绩的完成，使您荣升公爵。」

「菲利普，你真是个宝贝。」露西悄声说。

「闭嘴。」菲利普凶巴巴地说。

===Original===
“Three cheers,” said a familiar voice, “for the Duke of Donors.”

“Three cheers,” repeated the Lord High Islander, “for the Duke of Donors.”

What a cheer! All the islanders cheered and the M.A.’s and Lucy and Mr. Perrin and Mr. Noah, and from the inside of the ark came enthusiastic barkings and gruntings and roarings and squeakings﻿—as the animals of course joined in as well as they could. Thousands of gulls, circling on white wings in the sun above, added their screams to the general chorus. And when the sound of the last cheer died away, a little near familiar voice said:

“Well done, Philip! I’m proud of you.”

It was the parrot who, perched on the rigging of the *Lightning Loose*, had started the cheering.

===Chinese===
「来三声欢呼，」一个熟悉的声音说，「为捐赠公爵（the Duke of Donors）。」

「三声欢呼，」至高岛主跟着喊，「为捐赠公爵。」

好一场欢呼！岛民们欢呼，M.A.（文学硕士）们欢呼，露西、佩林先生、诺亚先生也欢呼，方舟里还传出兴高采烈的汪汪声、呼噜声、吼叫声和吱吱声——动物们自然也尽力加入了进来。成千上万只海鸥在头顶的阳光里展开白翅膀盘旋，把它们的尖叫也添进这大合唱。等最后一阵欢呼的余音散尽，一个近旁的熟悉小声音说道：

「干得好，菲利普！我为你骄傲。」

是那只鹦鹉，它栖在「闪电自由号」的索具上，带头喊起了欢呼。

===Original===
“So that’s all right,” it said, fluttered on to Philip’s shoulder and added, “I’ve heard you calling for me on the island all the week. But I felt I needed a rest. I’ve been talking too much. And that Pretenderette. And that cage. I assure you I needed a little time to get over my adventures.”

“We have all had our adventures,” said Mr. Noah gently. And Helen said:

“Won’t you land and take possession of the island? I’m sure we are longing to hear each other’s adventures.”

“You first,” said Mr. Noah to the Lord High Islander, who stepped ashore very gravely.

When Helen saw him come forward, she suddenly kissed Philip, and as the Lord High Islander’s foot touched the shore of that enchanted island, she simply and suddenly vanished.

===Chinese===
「这就都妥啦。」它说着，扑到菲利普肩上，又道，「整整一个星期，我都听见你在岛上喊我。可我觉得自己需要休息。我话说得太多了。还有那个女僭王，还有那只笼子。我向你保证，我需要一点工夫，才能缓过我这场冒险来。」

「咱们人人都经历了一场冒险。」诺亚先生温和地说。海伦则说：

「诸位不上岸来接管这座岛吗？我相信大家一定急着要互讲各人的冒险呢。」

「您先请。」诺亚先生对至高岛主说。至高岛主神情庄重地踏上了岸。

海伦见他走上前来，突然亲了亲菲利普，而至高岛主的脚踏上那座魔法小岛的一瞬，她就那么突兀地消失了。

===Original===
“Oh!” cried Philip, “I wish I hadn’t.” And his mouth trembled as girls’ mouths do if they are going to cry.

“The more a present costs you, the more it’s worth,” said Mr. Noah. “This has cost you so much, it’s the most splendid present in the world.”

“I know,” said Philip; “make yourselves at home, won’t you?” he just managed to say. And then he found he could not say any more. He just turned and went into the forest. And when he was alone in a green glade, he flung himself down on his face and lay a long time without moving. It had been such a happy week. And he was so tired of adventures.

When at last he sniffed with an air of finality and raised his head, the first thing he saw was Lucy, sitting quite still with her back to him.

“Hullo!” he said rather crossly, “what are you doing here?”

===Chinese===
「哦！」菲利普喊道，「我真不该送。」他的嘴角颤抖起来，像要哭的女孩子那样。

「礼物让你付出的越多，它就越贵重。」诺亚先生说，「这份礼物让你付出了这么多，它是世上最了不起的礼物。」

「我知道。」菲利普说，「各位请随便，就当自己家，好吗？」他勉强说出这句，就再也说不出话了。他转身走进了森林。等独自到了一块绿林空地上，他一头扑倒在地，一动不动躺了很久很久。那是多么快活的一个星期啊。而他已经多么厌倦冒险了。

等他终于斩钉截铁地吸了吸鼻子、抬起头来，头一眼看见的就是露西——她一动不动地坐着，背对着他。

「喂！」他有点没好气地说，「你在这儿干什么？」

===Original===
“Saying the multiplication table,” said Lucy promptly and turned her head, “so as not even to think about you. And I haven’t even once turned round. I knew you wanted to be alone. But I wanted to be here when you’d done being alone. See? I’ve got something to say to you.”

“Fire ahead,” said Philip, still grumpy.

“I think you’re perfectly splendid,” said Lucy very seriously, “and I want it to be real pax forever. And I’ll help you in the rest of the adventures. And if you’re cross, I’ll try not to mind. Napoleon was cross sometimes, I believe,” she added pensively, “and Julius Caesar.”

“Oh, that’s all right,” said Philip very awkwardly.

“Then we’re going to be real chums?”

===Chinese===
「背乘法口诀。」露西立刻回答，转过头来，「这样就连想你都不想了。我一次都没回过头。我知道你想一个人待着。可我想等你一个人待够了的时候，我就在旁边。明白吗？我有话要对你说。」

「说吧。」菲利普说，还闷闷的。

「我觉得你棒极了。」露西一本正经地说，「我要我们永远真正地 Pax（拉丁语『和平』，孩子间讲和的约定语）。往后剩下的冒险我都帮你。要是你发脾气，我尽量不往心里去。拿破仑也发过脾气，我记得，」她若有所思地补了一句，「尤利乌斯·恺撒也是。」

「哦，那没什么。」菲利普十分别扭地说。

「那我们就是真朋友、好哥们儿了？」

===Original===
“Oh yes, if you like. Only﻿—I don’t mind just this once; and it was decent of you to come and sit there with your back to me﻿—only I hate gas.”

“Yes,” said Lucy obediently, “I know. Only sometimes you feel you must gas a little or burst of admiration. And I’ve got your proper clothes in a bundle. I’ve been carrying them about ever since the islanders’ castle was washed away. Here they are.”

She produced the bundle. And this time Philip was really touched.

“Now I *do* call that something like,” he said. “The seaweed dress is all right here, but you never know what you may have to go through when you’re doing adventures. There might be thorns or snakes or anything. I’m jolly glad to get my boots back too. I say, come on. Let’s go to Helen’s palace and get a banquet ready. I know there’ll have to be a banquet. There always is, here. I know a first-rate bun-tree quite near here.”

“The coconut-ice plants looked beautiful as I came along,” said Lucy. “What a lovely island it is. And you made it!”

===Chinese===
「哦，行，随你。只是——就这一次我不介意；你肯跑来背对着我坐在那儿，够意思——只是我讨厌听恭维话。」

「是。」露西乖乖地应道，「我知道。只是有时候心里佩服得受不了，不恭维两句就要憋破肚子。对了，你的正经衣裳我打成一个包了。从岛民们的城堡被冲走那天起，我一直随身带着。喏，给你。」

她把包袱拿了出来。这一回，菲利普是真心感动了。

「这*才*叫够意思。」他说，「海草裙在这儿穿挺好，可干冒险这一行，天知道要吃些什么苦头。说不定有荆棘呀、蛇呀什么的。能拿回我的靴子我也太高兴了。我说，走吧。咱们去海伦的宫殿，准备一场宴会。我知道准得有一场宴会，这儿总是有。我知道离这儿不远有棵顶呱呱的小圆面包树。」

「我来的路上，椰子冰植株看着可漂亮了。」露西说，「多好的岛呀。还是你们俩造的呢！」

===Original===
“No gas,” said Philip warningly. “Helen and I made it.”

“She’s the dearest darling,” said Lucy.

“Oh, well,” said Philip with resignation, “if you must gas, gas about her.”

The banquet was all that you can imagine of interesting and magnificent. And Philip was, of course, the hero of the hour. And when the banquet was finished and the last guest had departed to its own house﻿—for the houses on the island were of course all ready to be occupied, furnished to the last point of comfort, with pincushions full of pins in every room, Mr. Noah and Lucy and Philip sat down on the terrace steps among the pink roses for a last little talk.

“Because,” said Philip, “we shall start the first thing in the morning. So please will you tell me now what the next deed is that I have to do?”

===Chinese===
「不许恭维。」菲利普警告说，「是海伦和我造的。」

「她是最最亲爱的小可爱。」露西说。

「哦，好吧。」菲利普认命地说，「要是你非恭维不可，那就恭维她吧。」

这场宴会要多有趣有多有趣、要多气派有多气派，尽你想象去吧。菲利普当然成了当日的英雄。宴会结束，最后一位客人回自己家去了——岛上的房子自然全都随时可住，家具舒适得一应俱全，每间屋里还摆着插满针的针插——随后诺亚先生、露西和菲利普在粉红玫瑰丛中的露台台阶上坐下，做最后的闲谈。

「因为，」菲利普说，「我们明天一早头一件事就是出发。所以请您现在就告诉我，我的下一件功绩是什么，行吗？」

===Original===
“Will you go by ark?” Mr. Noah asked, rolling up his yellow mat to make an elbow rest and leaning on it; “I shall be delighted.”

“I thought,” said Philip, “we might go in the *Lightning Loose*. I’ve never sailed her yet, you know. Do you think I *could*?”

“Of course you can,” said Mr. Noah; “and if not, Lucy can show you. Your charming yacht is steered on precisely the same principle as the ark. And in this land all the winds are favourable. You will find the yacht suitably provisioned. And I may add that you can go most of the way to your next deed by water﻿—first the sea and then the river.”

“And what,” asked Philip, “is the next deed?”

“In the extreme north of Polistarchia,” said Mr. Noah instructively, “lies a town called Somnolentia. It used to be called Briskford in happier days. A river then ran through the town, a rapid river that brought much gold from the mountains. The people used to work very hard to keep the channel clear of the lumps of gold which continually threatened to choke it. Their fields were then well-watered and fruitful, and the inhabitants were cheerful and happy. But when the Hippogriff was let out of the book, a Great Sloth got out too. Evading all efforts to secure him, the Great Sloth journeyed northward. He is a very large and striking animal, and by some means, either fear or admiration, he obtained a complete ascendancy over the inhabitants of Briskford. He induced them to build him a temple of solid gold, and while they were doing this the river bed became choked up and the stream was diverted into another channel far from the town. Since then the place is fallen into decay. The fields are parched and untilled. Such water as the people need for drinking is drawn by great labour from a well. Washing has become shockingly infrequent.”

===Chinese===
「坐方舟去吗？」诺亚先生问，一边把黄垫子卷起来当肘枕，倚在上面，「那我就太高兴了。」

「我想，」菲利普说，「我们可以坐「闪电自由号」去。您知道，我还从来没驾驶过它呢。您觉得我*行*吗？」

「您当然行。」诺亚先生说，「就算不行，露西也能教您。您那艘迷人的游艇，掌舵原理和方舟一模一样。而且在这片国土上，所有风向都是顺的。您会发现艇上给养一应俱全。我还可以补充一句：去下一件功绩的路上，大半程都能走水路——先是海，然后是河。」

「那么，」菲利普问，「下一件功绩是什么？」

「在波利斯托基亚的最北边，」诺亚先生诲人不倦地说，「有一座叫索姆诺伦蒂亚（Somnolentia）的城。在更兴旺的年代，它叫布里斯克福德。那时有条河穿城而过，是条湍急的河，从山里带来许多金子。人们辛勤劳作，不断清挖河道里那些随时可能把它堵塞的金块。他们的田地那时灌溉充足、物产丰饶，居民们快快活活。可自从骏鹰从书里放出来，一头大树懒也跟着溜了出来。它躲过了所有捉拿它的努力，一路向北去了。它是头非常巨大、非常惹眼的动物，也不知凭着什么——也许是畏惧，也许是崇拜——它把布里斯克福德的居民彻底降服了。它诱使他们为它修了一座纯金的庙，修庙的时候，河床淤塞了，河水改道，流进了远离城镇的另一条河道。打那以后，这地方就败落了。田地干裂，无人耕种。居民喝水，得费九牛二虎之力从井里打。洗漱更是少得吓人。」

===Original===
“Are we to teach the dirty chaps to wash?” asked Philip in disgust.

“Do not interrupt,” said Mr. Noah. “You destroy the thread of my narrative. Where was I?”

“Washing infrequent,” said Lucy; “but if the fields are dried up, what do they live on?”

“Pineapples,” replied Mr. Noah, “which grow freely and do not need much water. Gathering these is the sole industry of this degraded people. Pineapples are not considered a fruit but a vegetable,” he added hastily, seeing another question trembling on Philip’s lips. “Whatever of their waking time can be spared from the gathering and eating of the pineapples is spent in singing choric songs in honour of the Great Sloth. And even this time is short, for such is his influence on the Somnolentians that when he sleeps they sleep too, and,” added Mr. Noah impressively, “he sleeps almost all the time. Your deed is to devise some means of keeping the Great Sloth awake and busy. And I think you’ve got your work cut out. When you’ve disposed of the Great Sloth you can report yourself to me here. I shall remain here for some little time. I need a holiday. The parrot will accompany you. It knows its way about as well as any bird in the land. Good night. And good luck! You will excuse my not being down to breakfast.”

===Chinese===
「是要我们去教那些脏家伙洗澡吗？」菲利普厌恶地问。

「别打岔。」诺亚先生说，「你把我的叙事线索全搅乱了。我讲到哪儿了？」

「讲到洗漱少得吓人。」露西说，「可田都干了，他们靠什么活呀？」

「吃菠萝。」诺亚先生答道，「菠萝遍地自生，也不怎么费水。采集菠萝是这群堕落子民唯一的营生。菠萝不算水果，算蔬菜。」他瞥见又一个问题已经在菲利普嘴边打颤，赶忙补充，「除了采菠萝、吃菠萝，他们醒着的时间里剩下的工夫，全都用来合唱颂扬大树懒的赞歌。可就连这点时间也不多，因为大树懒对索姆诺伦蒂亚人的影响实在太大，它一睡，他们也跟着睡，而，」诺亚先生加重语气补充道，「它几乎无时无刻不在睡。您的功绩，就是想出法子让这头大树懒醒着、忙着。我看您这差事可不轻省。处置完大树懒，您可以到这儿来向我复命。我要在这儿盘桓些时日，我需要休假。鹦鹉会陪您去，这一带的路，它认得不比全国任何一只鸟差。晚安。祝好运！明早我不下来用早餐了，请恕不奉陪。」

===Original===
And the next morning, dewy-early, Philip and Lucy and the parrot went aboard the yacht and loosed her from her moorings, and Lucy showed Philip how to steer, and the parrot sat on the mast and called out instructions.

They made for the mouth of a river. (“I never built a river,” said Philip. “No,” said the parrot, “it came out of the poetry book.”) And when they were hungry they let down the anchor and went into the cabin for breakfast. And two people sprang to meet them, almost knocking Lucy down with the violence of their welcome. The two people were Max and Brenda.

“Oh, you dear dogs,” Lucy cried, and Philip patted them, one with each hand, “how did you get here?”

“It was a little surprise of Mr. Noah’s,” said the parrot.

Max and Brenda whined and barked and gushed.

===Chinese===
第二天清晨，露水未晞，菲利普、露西和鹦鹉上了游艇，解缆离泊。露西教菲利普掌舵，鹦鹉蹲在桅杆上高声指点。

他们朝一条河的河口驶去。（「我可从来没造过河。」菲利普说。「是啊，」鹦鹉说，「它是从诗集里跑出来的。」）等饿了，他们抛下锚，进舱吃早饭。有两位飞奔出来迎接，那份热烈的欢迎差点把露西撞个跟头。这两位就是麦克斯和布伦达。

「哦，你们这两只亲爱的小狗。」露西喊道。菲利普一手一只地拍着它们，「你们怎么到这儿来的？」

「这是诺亚先生安排的小惊喜。」鹦鹉说。

麦克斯和布伦达呜呜叫着，热情四溢。

===Original===
“I wish we could understand what they’re saying,” said Lucy.

“If you only knew the magic word that the Hippogriff obeys,” said the parrot, “you could say it, and then you’d understand all animal talk. Only, of course, I mustn’t tell it you. It’s one of the eleven mysteries.”

“But I know it,” said Philip, and at once breathed the word in the tiny silky ear of Brenda and then in the longer silkier ear of Max, and instantly﻿—

“Oh, my dears!” they heard Brenda say in a softly shrill excited voice; “oh, my dearie dears! We *are* so pleased to see you. I’m only a poor little faithful doggy; I’m not clever, you know, but my affectionate nature makes me almost mad with joy to see my dear master and mistress again.”

“Very glad to see you, sir,” said Max with heavy politeness. “I hope you’ll be comfortable here. There’s no comfort for a dog like being with his master.”

===Chinese===
「要是我们能听懂它们说话就好了。」露西说。

「只要你们知道骏鹰服从的那个魔法字眼，」鹦鹉说，「念上一遍，就能听懂所有动物的话。只是，我当然不能告诉你们。那是十一大奥秘之一。」

「可我知道呀。」菲利普说着，立刻对着布伦达那只丝绒般的小耳朵念了一遍那个字眼，又对着麦克斯那只更长、更丝绒的耳朵念了一遍。刹那间——

「哦，我的宝贝们！」他们听见布伦达用一种软软的、又尖又激动的嗓音说，「哦，我亲亲的宝贝们！再见到你们，我们*真是*太高兴啦。我不过是只可怜的忠心小狗，我不聪明，这您知道，可我这多情的性子，一见到亲爱的男主人和女主人，就高兴得几乎发疯。」

「很高兴见到您，少爷。」麦克斯语气郑重地客套道，「愿您在船上住得舒心。对狗来说，跟着主人，就是天下第一等的舒心了。」

===Original===
And with that he sat down and went to sleep, and the others had breakfast. It is rather fun cooking in yachts. And there was something new and charming in Brenda’s delicate way of sitting up and begging and saying at the same time, “I do *hate* to bother my darling master and mistress, but if you *could* spare another *tiny* bit of bacon﻿—Oh, *thank you*, how good and generous you are!”

They sailed the yacht successfully into the river which presently ran into the shadow of a tropical forest. Also out of a book.

“You might go on during the night,” said the parrot, “if the dogs would steer under my directions. You could tie one end of a rope to their collars and another to the helm. It’s easier than turning spits.”

“Delighted!” said Max; “only, of course, it’s understood that we sleep through the day?”

“Of course,” said everybody. So that was settled. And the children went to bed.

===Chinese===
说完他就坐下睡着了，其余的人吃早饭。在游艇上做饭挺有意思。布伦达坐直身子作揖讨食的样子也自有一种新鲜的魅力，它一边讨一边说：「人家*实在*不想烦扰亲爱的主人主母，可要是您*能*再匀那么*一小小*条培根——哦，*谢谢*您，您真好，真大方！」

他们顺顺当当地把游艇开进了那条河，河道不久便没入一座热带森林的浓荫里。这座森林同样是从书里跑出来的。

「夜里也可以接着赶路，」鹦鹉说，「只要狗儿们肯在我的指挥下掌舵。绳子一头拴它们的项圈，一头拴舵柄就行了。可比转烤肉叉省事多了。」

「乐意效劳！」麦克斯说，「只是，当然喽，说好了白天我们睡觉，对吧？」

「当然。」大家齐声说。事情就这么定了。孩子们上床睡觉去了。

===Original===
It was in the middle of the night that the parrot roused Philip with his usual gentle beak-touch. Then﻿—

“Wake up,” it said; “this is not the right river. It’s not the right direction. Nothing’s right. The ship’s all wrong. I’m very much afraid someone has been opening a book and this river has got out.”

Philip hurried out on deck, and by the light of the lamps from the cabin, gazed out at the banks of the river. At least he looked for them. But there weren’t any banks. Instead, steep and rugged cliffs rose on each side, and overhead, instead of a starry sky, was a great arched roof of a cavern glistening with moisture and dark as a raven’s feathers.

“We must turn back,” said Philip. “I don’t like this at all.”

“Unfortunately,” said the parrot, “there is no room to turn back, and the *Lightning Loose* is not constructed for going backwards.”

===Chinese===
半夜里，鹦鹉用它惯常的温柔喙触把菲利普唤醒。接着——

「醒醒，」它说，「这不是原来那条河。方向不对。什么都不对。这船全不对劲。我非常担心，有人打开了书，把这条河放出来了。」

菲利普急忙跑到甲板上，就着舱里灯光望向河的两岸——至少他想找两岸。可两岸根本没有。取而代之的是两边陡峭嶙峋的峭壁，头顶上也不是星空，而是一面巨大的洞窟拱顶，湿津津地闪光，黑得像乌鸦的羽毛。

「我们得退回去。」菲利普说，「我一点儿也不喜欢这样。」

「不巧，」鹦鹉说，「没有掉头的余地，而且「闪电自由号」造出来就不是倒着开的。」

===Original===
“Oh, dear,” whispered Brenda, “I wish we hadn’t come. Dear little dogs ought to be taken comfortable care of and not be sent out on nasty ships that can’t turn back when it’s dangerous.”

“My dear,” said Max with slow firmness, “dear little dogs can’t help themselves now. So they had better look out for chances of helping their masters.”

“But what can we *do*, then?” said Philip impatiently.

“I fear,” said the parrot, “that we can do nothing but go straight on. If this river is in a book it will come out somewhere. No river in a book ever runs underground and stays there.”

“I shan’t wake Lucy,” said Philip; “she might be frightened.”

===Chinese===
「哦，天哪。」布伦达悄声说，「真希望我们没来。亲爱的小狗本该被舒舒服服地照顾着，不该被派到糟糕的船上，出了危险连个头都掉不了。」

「亲爱的，」麦克斯慢条斯理而又坚定地说，「眼下亲爱的小狗们自己也帮不了自己。所以它们最好留神找机会帮主人们的忙。」

「那我们*到底*能做什么呢？」菲利普不耐烦地说。

「恐怕，」鹦鹉说，「我们除了照直往前，什么也做不了。这条河要是在书里，它总会在什么地方流出来。书里的河从没有钻进地下就不出来的。」

「我不叫醒露西，」菲利普说，「她可能会害怕。」

===Original===
“You needn’t,” said Lucy, “she’s awake, and she’s no more frightened than you are.”

(“You hear that,” said Max to Brenda; “you take example by her, my dear!”)

“But if we are going the wrong way, we shan’t reach the Great Sloth,” Lucy went on.

“Sooner or later, one way or another, we shall come to him,” said the parrot; “and time is of no importance to a Great Sloth.”

It was now very cold, and our travellers were glad to wrap themselves in the flags of all nations with which the yacht was handsomely provided. Philip made a sort of tabard of the Union Jack and the old Royal Arms of England, with the lilies and leopards; and Lucy wore the Japanese flag as a shawl. She said the picture of the sun on it made her feel warm. But Philip shivered under his complicated crosses and lions, as the *Lightning Loose* swept on over the dark tide between the dark walls and under the dark roof of the cavern.

===Chinese===
「用不着你叫，」露西说，「她醒着呢，而且她可不比你更害怕。」

（「听见没有，」麦克斯对布伦达说，「你跟她学着点儿，亲爱的！」）

「可要是我们走错了路，就到不了大树懒那儿了。」露西接着说。

「迟早的事，殊途同归，我们总会遇上它。」鹦鹉说，「对一头大树懒来说，时间无所谓。」

这时天气冷极了，我们的旅人们乐得裹上游艇上体面备下的万国旗。菲利普把米字旗和绣着百合花与豹的英格兰旧王室纹章旗拼成一件骑士罩袍式的衣裳；露西则把日本旗当披肩。她说旗子上那个太阳图案让她觉得暖和。可菲利普裹着他那满是十字和狮子的复杂行头，照样瑟瑟发抖，「闪电自由号」在黑暗的潮水上疾驰，两侧是黑墙，头顶是黑洞洞的窟顶。

===Original===
“Cheer up,” said the parrot. “Think what a lot of adventures you’re having that no one else has ever had: think what a lot of things you’ll have to tell the other boys when you go to school.”

“The other boys wouldn’t believe a word of it,” said Philip in gloom. “I wouldn’t unless I knew it was true.”

“What I think is,” said Lucy, watching the yellow light from the lamps rushing ahead along the roof, “that we shan’t want to tell people. It’ll be just enough to know it ourselves and talk about it, just Philip and me together.”

“Well, as to that﻿—” the parrot was beginning doubtfully, when he broke off to exclaim:

“Do my claws deceive me or is there a curious vibration, and noticeable acceleration of velocity?”

“Eh?” said Philip, which is not manners, and he knew it.

===Chinese===
「振作点。」鹦鹉说，「想想你们正经历着多少别人从没经历过的冒险：想想等你上了学，有多少事可以讲给别的男孩听。」

「别的男孩一个字也不会信。」菲利普阴郁地说，「要不是我自己知道这是真的，连我都不信。」

「我倒觉得，」露西望着灯光沿着洞顶向前飞掠的黄色光焰说，「我们不会想讲给别人听的。光是我们自己知道、我们自己聊聊就够了，就菲利普和我两个。」

「唔，说到这个——」鹦鹉将信将疑地开口，忽然又顿住，惊叫起来：

「是我的爪子骗了我，还是船身有点异样的震动，速度也明显加快了？」

「啊？」菲利普应了一声。这么应人可没礼貌，他自己也知道。

===Original===
“He means,” said Max stolidly, “aren’t we going rather fast and rather wobbly?”

We certainly were. The *Lightning Loose* was going faster and faster along that subterranean channel, and every now and then gave a lurch and a shiver.

“Oh!” whined Brenda; “this is a dreadful place for dear little dogs!”

“Philip!” said Lucy in a low voice, “I know something is going to happen. Something dreadful. We *are* friends, aren’t we?”

“Yes,” said Philip firmly.

===Chinese===
「他是说，」麦克斯不紧不慢地说，「我们是不是跑得有点太快、晃得有点太厉害了？」

可不是嘛。「闪电自由号」沿着那条地下河道越跑越快，还不时猛地一歪、一颤。

「哦！」布伦达哀叫，「这地方对亲爱的小狗来说太可怕啦！」

「菲利普！」露西低声说，「我知道要出事了。什么可怕的事。我们*已经*是朋友了，对不对？」

「对。」菲利普坚定地说。

===Original===
“Then I wish you’d kiss me.”

“I can like you just as much without that,” said Philip uneasily. “Kissing people﻿—it’s silly, don’t you think?”

“Nobody’s kissed me since daddy went away,” she said, “except Helen. And you don’t mind kissing Helen. She *said* you were going to adopt me for your sister.”

“Oh! all right,” said Philip, and put his arm round her and kissed her. She felt so little and helpless and bony in his arm that he suddenly felt sorry for her, kissed her again more kindly and then, withdrawing his arm, thumped her hearteningly on the back.

“Be a man,” he said in tones of comradeship and encouragement. “I’m perfectly certain nothing’s going to happen. We’re just going through a tunnel, and presently we shall just come out into the open air again, with the sky and the stars going on as usual.”

===Chinese===
「那我希望你亲亲我。」

「不亲我也一样喜欢你呀。」菲利普不自在地说，「亲来亲去的——多傻，你不觉得吗？」

「爸爸走了以后，就没人亲过我了。」她说，「除了海伦。你亲海伦倒不介意。她*说*过，你要收我当妹妹的。」

「哦！行吧。」菲利普说着，伸出胳膊搂住她，亲了亲。她在怀里显得那么瘦小、那么无依无靠，他忽然心疼起来，又更温柔地亲了她一下，然后抽回胳膊，在她背上给她打气似的一拍。

「拿出男子汉的样子来。」他用同志加鼓励的口吻说，「我百分百肯定什么事儿也不会有。我们不过是过一条隧道，一会儿就又出到外头的天底下了，天空照旧，星星照旧。」

===Original===
He spoke this standing on the prow beside Lucy, and as he spoke she clutched his arm.

“Oh, look,” she breathed, “oh, listen!”

He listened. And he heard a dull echoing roar that got louder and louder. And he looked. The light of the lamps shone ahead on the dark gleaming water, and then quite suddenly it did not shine on the water because there was no longer any water for it to shine on. Only great empty black darkness. A great hole, ahead, into which the stream poured itself. And now they were at the edge of the gulf. The *Lightning Loose* gave a shudder and a bound and hung for what seemed a long moment on the edge of the precipice down which the underground river was pouring itself in a smooth sleek stream, rather like poured treacle, over what felt like the edge of everything solid.

The moment ended, and the little yacht, with Philip and Lucy and the parrot and the two dogs, plunged headlong over the edge into the dark unknown abyss below.

“It’s all right, Lu,” said Philip in that moment. “I’ll take care of you.”

And then there was silence in the cavern﻿—only the rushing sound of the great waterfall echoed in the rocky arch.

===Chinese===
这话他是站在船头、挨着露西说的，说着说着，她一把抓住了他的胳膊。

「哦，你看，」她屏着气说，「哦，你听！」

他听了。他听见一阵沉闷的、回声滚滚的轰鸣，越来越响。他看了。灯光照亮前方黑亮的水面，忽然之间，光不再照在水面上——因为前面已经没有水面可照了。只剩巨大、空荡荡的漆黑。前方是一个大窟窿，河水正把自己整个灌进去。此刻他们已到了深渊的边缘。「闪电自由号」一颤、一跃，在悬崖边上悬了仿佛好长的一刻——地下河正顺着崖壁倾泻而下，水流光滑平顺，倒像是倒下去的糖浆，翻过那仿佛一切坚实之物的尽头。

这一刻过去了，小小的游艇载着菲利普、露西、鹦鹉和两条狗，一头扎过崖沿，坠向下头黑暗的未知深渊。

「别怕，卢。」就在那一瞬，菲利普说，「我会照顾你的。」

随后洞窟里一片寂静——只有大瀑布的奔流之声，在石拱间回荡。

## X The Great Sloth / 大树懒

===Original===
You have heard of Indians shooting rapids in their birch-bark canoes? And perhaps you have yourself sailed a toy boat on a stream, and made a dam of clay, and waited with more or less patience till the water rose nearly to the top, and then broken a bit of your dam out and made a waterfall and let your boat drift over the edge of it. You know how it goes slowly at first, then hesitates and sweeps on more and more quickly. Sometimes it upsets; and sometimes it shudders and strains and trembles and sways to one side and to the other, and at last rights itself and makes up its mind, and rushes on down the stream, usually to be entangled in the clump of rushes at the stream’s next turn. This is what happened to that good yacht, the *Lightning Loose*. She shot over the edge of that dark smooth subterranean waterfall, hung a long breathless moment between still air and falling water, slid down like a flash, dashed into the stream below, shuddered, reeled, righted herself and sped on. You have perhaps been down the water chute at Earl’s Court? It was rather like that.

“It’s﻿—it’s all right,” said Philip, in a rather shaky whisper. “She’s going on all right.”

“Yes,” said Lucy, holding his arm very tight; “yes, I’m sure she’s going on all right.”

“Are we drowned?” said a trembling squeak. “Oh, Max, are we really drowned?”

“I don’t think so,” Max replied with caution. “And if we are, my dear, we cannot undrown ourselves by screams.”

===Chinese===
你听说过印第安人驾着桦皮独木舟冲激流吧？也许你自己也在小溪上放过玩具船：用黏土垒一道坝，耐着或大或小的性子等水涨到快齐坝顶，再抠开一小块，造出一挂瀑布，让小船从瀑布沿上漂下去。你知道它是怎么走的：起初慢吞吞，跟着犹犹豫豫，然后越冲越快。有时它会翻掉；有时它抖抖索索、憋足了劲、摇摇晃晃歪向这边又歪向那边，最后自己正过来，下了决心，顺流猛冲下去——多半一头缠进下一道河湾的灯心草丛里。那艘好船「闪电自由号」遇上的正是这般光景。它射过那道黑沉沉、平溜溜的地下瀑布的边沿，在静空气和落水之间悬了长长一口气工夫，跟着闪电似的滑下去，扎进下头的溪流，一哆嗦，一个趔趄，自己正了过来，继续飞驰。你也许坐过厄尔斯考特（Earl’s Court）的滑水槽？跟那个差不多。

「没——没事，」菲利普用发颤的气声说，「它走得挺好。」

「嗯。」露西说，把他的胳膊攥得紧紧的，「嗯，我保证它走得挺好。」

「我们淹死了吗？」一个发抖的尖嗓子问，「哦，麦克斯，我们真淹死了吗？」

「我看没有。」麦克斯谨慎地回答，「就算淹死了，亲爱的，靠尖叫也尖叫不活过来呀。」

===Original===
“Far from it,” said the parrot, who had for the moment been rendered quite speechless by the shock. And you know a parrot is not made speechless just by any little thing. “So we may just as well try to behave,” it said.

The lamps had certainly behaved, and behaved beautifully; through the wild air of the fall, the wild splash as the *Lightning Loose* struck the stream below, the lamps had shone on, seemingly undisturbed.

“An example to us all,” said the parrot.

“Yes, but,” said Lucy, “what are we to do?”

“When adventures take a turn one is far from expecting, one does what one can,” said the parrot.

===Chinese===
「远没到那地步。」鹦鹉说。这一下惊吓，把它一时弄得说不出话来——你知道，随随便便一点小事可没法让一只鹦鹉哑口无言。「所以咱们不妨尽量放规矩些。」它说。

那些灯倒真是规规矩矩，规矩得漂亮；穿过瀑布狂乱的风、穿过「闪电自由号」扎进下面溪流时狂乱的飞溅，它们一直亮着，看上去纹丝未动。

「真是咱们大家的好榜样。」鹦鹉说。

「是没错，可是，」露西说，「我们现在该怎么办？」

「当冒险来了个谁也万万想不到的转弯时，人就尽人事听天命。」鹦鹉说。

===Original===
“And what’s that?”

“Nothing,” said the parrot. “Philip has relieved Max at the helm and is steering a straight course between the banks﻿—if you can call them banks. There is nothing else to be done.”

There plainly wasn’t. The *Lightning Loose* rushed on through the darkness. Lucy reflected for a moment and then made cocoa. This was real heroism. It cheered everyone up, including the cocoa-maker herself. It was impossible to believe that anything dreadful was going to happen when you were making that soft, sweet, ordinary drink.

“I say,” Philip remarked when she carried a cup to him at the wheel, “I’ve been thinking. All this is out of a book. Someone must have let it out. I know what book it’s out of too. And if the whole story got out of the book we’re all right. Only we shall go on for ages and climb out at last, three days’ journey from Trieste.”

“I see,” said Lucy, and added that she hated geography. “Drink your cocoa while it’s hot,” she said in motherly accents, and “what book is it?”

===Chinese===
「那是什么意思？」

「没什么意思。」鹦鹉说，「菲利普已经接替麦克斯掌舵，正在两岸之间笔直地开——要是那也配叫岸的话。别的没什么可干的了。」

确实没什么可干的。「闪电自由号」在黑暗里一路疾驰。露西想了想，然后动手冲可可。这可是真正的英雄壮举。它让每个人都精神起来，连冲可可的人自己也包括在内。当你正在调这种柔软、香甜、家常的饮料时，你不可能相信会发生什么可怕的事。

「我说，」露西把一杯可可送到舵边时，菲利普说，「我一直在想。这一切全是从一本书里出来的。准是谁把它放出来的。我还知道是哪本书。要是整本书的故事都跑了出来，那我们就没事。只不过我们要走上老半天，最后爬出去的时候，离的里雅斯特还有三天路程。」

「明白了。」露西说，又补了一句她讨厌地理。「趁热把可可喝了。」她用小妈妈的口气说，跟着又问，「是哪本书？」

===Original===
“It’s *The Last Cruise of the Teal*,” he said. “Helen gave it me just before she went away. It’s a ripping book, and I used it for the roof of the outer court of the Hall of Justice. I remember it perfectly. The chaps on the *Teal* made torches of paper soaked in paraffin.”

“We haven’t any,” said Lucy; “besides our lamps light everything up all right. Oh! there’s Brenda crying again. She hasn’t a shadow of pluck.”

She went quickly to the cabin where Max was trying to cheer Brenda by remarks full of solid good sense, to which Brenda paid no attention whatever.

“I knew how it would be,” she kept saying in a whining voice; “I told you so from the beginning. I wish we hadn’t come. I want to go home. Oh! what a dreadful thing to happen to dear little dogs.”

===Chinese===
「是《水鸭号的最后航程》（*The Last Cruise of the Teal*）。」他说，「海伦临走前给我的。是本特别带劲的书，我拿它当了正义大厅外院的屋顶。我记得清清楚楚。『水鸭号』上那些家伙用浸过石蜡的纸做火把。」

「我们可没有那些。」露西说，「再说咱们的灯什么都照得亮堂堂的。哦！布伦达又哭了。她连一丁点儿的胆量都没有。」

她快步走进船舱，麦克斯正在那儿用一堆句句在理的话给布伦达打气，可布伦达一个字也没听进去。

「我早知道会这样。」她翻来覆去地哀声抱怨，「我从头一天就说过。真希望咱们没来。我要回家。哦！亲爱的小狗遭这种罪，多可怕呀。」

===Original===
“Brenda,” said Lucy firmly, “if you don’t stop whining you shan’t have any cocoa.”

Brenda stopped at once and wagged her tail appealingly.

“Cocoa?” she said, “did anyone say cocoa? My nerves are so delicate. I know I’m a trial, dear Max, it’s no use your pretending I’m not, but there is nothing like cocoa for the nerves. Plenty of sugar, please, dear Lucy. Thank you *so* much! Yes, it’s *just* as I like it.”

“There will be other things to eat by and by,” said Lucy. “People who whine won’t get any.”

“I’m sure nobody would *dream* of whining,” said Brenda. “I know I’m too sensitive; but you can do anything with dear little dogs by kindness. And as for whining﻿—do you know it’s a thing I’ve never been subject to, from a child, never. Max will tell you the same.”

Max said nothing, but only fixed his beautiful eyes hopefully on the cocoa jug.

===Chinese===
「布伦达，」露西严厉地说，「你再哼哼唧唧，就不给你可可喝。」

布伦达立刻不哼了，讨饶似的摇起了尾巴。

「可可？」她说，「刚才谁说可可了？我的神经可娇气了。我知道我是个麻烦，亲爱的麦克斯，你装着不麻烦也没用，可要说治神经，没什么比得上可可。请多放糖，亲爱的露西。多谢*您*啦！对啦，*正*合我的口味。」

「待会儿还有别的吃的。」露西说，「哼哼唧唧的人可没份儿。」

「我保证谁也*绝不会*梦到哼哼唧唧。」布伦达说，「我知道我太敏感；可只要善待亲爱的小狗，叫它们干什么都行。至于哼哼唧唧——您可知道，我从小到大从来不犯这毛病，从来没有。麦克斯也可以作证。」

麦克斯什么也没说，只把那双漂亮的眼睛满怀期待地盯住了可可壶。

===Original===
And all the time the yacht was speeding along the underground stream, beneath the vast arch of the underground cavern.

“The worst of it is we may be going ever so far away from where we want to get to,” said Philip, when Max had undertaken the steering again.

“All roads,” remarked the parrot, “lead to Somnolentia. And besides the ship is travelling due north﻿—at least so the ship’s compass states, and I have no reason as yet for doubting its word.”

“Hullo!” cried more than one voice, and the ship shot out of the dark cavern into a sheet of water that lay spread under a white dome. The stream that had brought them there seemed to run across one side of this pool. Max, directed by the parrot, steered the ship into smooth water, where she lay at rest at last in the very middle of this great underground lake.

“*This* isn’t out of *The Cruise of the Teal*,” said Philip. “They must have shut that book.”

===Chinese===
而这一切工夫里，游艇始终在地下洞穴的巨大拱顶下，沿着地下溪流一路飞驰。

「最糟糕的是，咱们说不定正往离目的地老远老远的地方开。」麦克斯重新接过舵后，菲利普说。

「条条大路，」鹦鹉评论道，「通索姆诺伦蒂亚。再说船正朝着正北开——至少船上的罗盘是这么说的，眼下我还没有理由怀疑它的话。」

「哎呀！」不止一个声音喊了起来。船冲出了黑暗的洞穴，驶进一片铺展在白色穹顶下的水面。把他们带到这里的那条溪流，似乎横穿过这片水潭的一侧。麦克斯照鹦鹉的指点，把船开进平缓的水里，它终于安安稳稳地停在了这座地下大湖的正中央。

「*这个*可不是《水鸭号巡航记》里的。」菲利普说，「那本书准是合上了。」

===Original===
“I think it’s out of a book about Mexico or Peru or Ingots or some geographical place,” said Lucy; “it had a green-and-gold binding. I think you used it for the other end of the outer justice court. And if you did, this dome’s solid silver, and there’s a hole in it, and under this dome there’s untold treasure in gold incas.”

“What’s incas?”

“Gold bars, I believe,” said Lucy; “and Mexicans come down through the hole in the roof and get it, and when enemies come they flood it with water. It’s flooded now,” she added unnecessarily.

“I wish adventures had never been invented,” said Brenda. “No, dear Lucy, I am not whining. Far from it. But if a dear little dog might suggest it, we should all be better in a home, should we not?”

All eyes now perceived a dark hole in the roof, a round hole exactly in the middle of the shining dome. And as they gazed the dark hole became light. And they saw above them a white shining disk like a very large and very bright moon. It was the light of day.

===Chinese===
「我看这是从一本讲墨西哥呀、秘鲁呀、英格特（Ingots）呀，反正什么讲地理的地方的书里出来的。」露西说，「那本书是绿底烫金封皮。我记得你拿它垫了正义大厅外院的另一头。要真是它，这穹顶是纯银的，顶上有个洞，穹顶底下埋着数不清的金英卡斯（incas）财宝。」

「什么叫英卡斯？」

「我想是金条吧。」露西说，「墨西哥人从屋顶的洞里下来取金子，敌人来了就放水把这儿淹了。现在就淹着呢。」她又多此一举地补了一句。

「我真希望从来没人发明冒险。」布伦达说，「不，亲爱的露西，我不是在哼哼。远不是。可要是亲爱的小狗可以提个建议的话——咱们大家待在家里岂不更好，您说是不是？」

现在所有人都看见了穹顶上的一个黑洞——就在那闪亮穹顶正中央的一个圆洞。他们望着望着，黑洞亮了起来。他们看见头顶上有一个白亮的圆盘，像一轮又大又亮的月亮。那是白昼的光。

===Original===
“Someone has opened the trapdoor,” said Lucy. “The Ingots always closed their treasure-vaults with trapdoors.”

The bright disk was obscured; confused shapes broke its shining roundness. Then another disk, small and very black appeared in the middle of it; the black disk grew larger and larger and larger. It was coming down to them. Slowly and steadily it came; now it reached the level of the dome, now it hung below it; down, down, down it came, past the level of their eager eyes and splashed in the water close by the ship. It was a large empty bucket. The rope which held it was jerked from above; the bucket dipped and filled and was drawn up again slowly and steadily till it disappeared in the hole in the roof.

“Quick,” said the parrot, “get the ship exactly under the hole, and next time the bucket comes down you can go up in it.”

“This is out of the *Arabian Nights*, I think,” said Lucy, when the yacht was directly under the hole in the roof. “But who is it that keeps on opening the books? Somebody must be pulling Polistopolis down.”

“The Pretenderette, I shouldn’t wonder,” said Philip gloomily. “She isn’t the Deliverer, so she must be the Destroyer. Nobody else can get into Polistarchia, you know.”

===Chinese===
「有人打开了活板门。」露西说，「英格特人向来用活板门封闭他们的藏宝库。」

亮圆盘暗了一下；乱糟糟的影子破了它闪亮的圆。接着圆盘正中又出现一个小小的、黑黑的圆盘；黑圆盘越来越大、越来越大、越来越大。它正朝他们下来。它慢慢悠悠、稳稳当当地下来；一会儿到了穹顶的高度，一会儿又悬到穹顶底下；下来，下来，下来，掠过他们翘首张望的眼睛，扑通一声溅落在船边的水里。那是一只空空的大吊桶。牵桶的绳子从上头猛地一扯；吊桶一歪，灌满了水，又被慢慢悠悠、稳稳当当地提上去，直到消失在穹顶的洞里。

「快，」鹦鹉说，「把船开到洞的正下方，等吊桶再下来，你们就能坐它上去了。」

「我看这是《一千零一夜》里的。」游艇开到洞正下方时，露西说，「可到底是谁在不停地打开那些书呀？准是有人在拆波利斯托波利斯。」

「八成是女僭王。」菲利普阴沉沉地说，「她不是解救者，那准是毁灭者。要知道，别的人可进不了波利斯托基亚。」

===Original===
“There’s me.”

“Oh, you’re Deliverer too.”

“Thank you,” said Lucy gratefully. “But there’s Helen.”

“She was only on the Island, you know; she couldn’t come to Polistarchia. Look out!”

The bucket was descending again, and instead of splashing in the water it bumped on the deck.

===Chinese===
「还有我呢。」

「哦，你也算解救者。」

「谢谢你。」露西感激地说，「可还有海伦呢。」

「她只能在岛上，你知道；她来不了波利斯托基亚。小心！」

吊桶又下来了，这回它没溅进水里，而是咚的一声撞在甲板上。

===Original===
“You go first,” said Philip to Lucy.

“And you,” said Max to Brenda.

“Oh, I’ll go first if you like,” said Philip.

“Yes,” said Max, “I’ll go first if you like, Brenda.”

You see Philip felt that he ought to give Lucy the first chance of escaping from the poor *Lightning Loose*. Yet he could not be at all sure what it was that she would be escaping to. And if there was danger overhead, of course he ought to be the one to go first to face it. And the worthy Max felt the same about Brenda.

===Chinese===
「你先上。」菲利普对露西说。

「你先上。」麦克斯对布伦达说。

「哦，要是你们乐意，就我先上。」菲利普说。

「好啊，」麦克斯说，「要是你们乐意，就我先上，布伦达。」

你瞧，菲利普觉得，第一个逃离可怜的「闪电自由号」的机会，理应让给露西。可他一点儿也拿不准，露西逃去的那地方是什么光景。而要是头顶上有危险，那当然又该他打头阵去面对。可敬的麦克斯对布伦达也是同样的心思。

===Original===
And Lucy felt just the same as they did. I don’t know what Brenda felt. She whined a little. Then for one moment Lucy and Philip stood on the deck each grasping the handle of the bucket and looking at each other, and the dogs looked at them, and the parrot looked at everyone in turn. An impatient jerk and shake of the rope from above reminded them that there was no time to lose.

Lucy decided that it was more dangerous to go than to stay, just at the same moment when Philip decided that it was more dangerous to stay than to go, so when Lucy stepped into the bucket Philip helped her eagerly. Max thought the same as Philip, and I am afraid Brenda agreed with them. At any rate she leaped into Lucy’s lap and curled her long length round just as the rope tightened and the bucket began to go up. Brenda screamed faintly, but her scream was stifled at once.

“I’ll send the bucket down again the moment I get up,” Lucy called out; and a moment later, “it feels awfully jolly, like a swing.”

And so saying she was drawn up into the hole in the roof of the dome. Then a sound of voices came down the shaft, a confused sound; the anxious little party on the *Lightning Loose* could not make out any distinct words. They all stood staring up, expecting, waiting for the bucket to come down again.

“I hate leaving the ship,” said Philip.

===Chinese===
而露西的心思跟他们一模一样。布伦达什么心思我不知道。她哼唧了两声。有那么一刻，露西和菲利普站在甲板上，一人攥着吊桶的一边提手，互相望着；两条狗望着他们；鹦鹉挨个儿望望大家。上头不耐烦地把绳子一扯一抖，提醒他们事不宜迟。

露西刚认定「去」比「留」更危险，菲利普恰恰在同一瞬间认定「留」比「去」更危险。于是露西跨进吊桶时，菲利普热心地扶了她一把。麦克斯跟菲利普想的一样，恐怕布伦达也赞成他们。反正绳子一紧、吊桶开始上升的那一刻，她纵身跳进露西怀里，把长长的身子盘成一团。布伦达尖叫了一声，可那声尖叫立刻就被捂住了。

「我一上去就把吊桶放下来。」露西喊道；过了一会儿又喊，「这滋味好玩极了，像荡秋千。」

她就这样被提进了穹顶的洞里。接着一阵人声顺着竖井传下来，乱哄哄的；「闪电自由号」上这伙焦急的小人儿一个字也分辨不出。他们都站着仰望，盼着，等着吊桶再放下来。

「我真舍不得离开这条船。」菲利普说。

===Original===
“You shall be the last to leave her,” said the parrot consolingly; “that is if we can manage about Max without your having to sit on him in the bucket if he gets in first.”

“But how about you?” said Philip.

A little arrogantly the parrot unfolded half a bright wing.

“Oh!” said Philip enlightened and reminded. “Of course! And you might have flown away at any time. And yet you stuck to us. I say, you know, that was jolly decent of you.”

“Not at all,” said the parrot with conscious modesty.

===Chinese===
「你会是最后一个离开它的。」鹦鹉安慰道，「前提是咱们能安排好麦克斯，不用你坐进吊桶里压着它，万一它先抢了进去的话。」

「那你自己呢？」菲利普说。

鹦鹉带点儿傲气地展开半边亮闪闪的翅膀。

「哦！」菲利普恍然大悟，也想起来了，「当然！你随时都可以飞走的呀。可你一直守着我们。我说，你可真够意思。」

「哪里哪里。」鹦鹉自觉谦虚地说。

===Original===
“But it was,” Philip insisted. “You might have﻿—hullo!” cried Philip. The bucket came down again with a horrible rush. They held their breaths and looked to see the form of Lucy hurtling through the air. But no, the bucket swung loose a moment in midair, then it was hastily drawn up, and a hollow metallic clang echoed through the cavern.

“Brenda!” the cry was wrung from the heart of the sober self-contained Max.

“My wings and claws!” exclaimed the parrot.

“Oh, bother!” said Philip.

There was some excuse for these expressions of emotion. The white disk overhead had suddenly disappeared. Someone up above had banged the lid down. And all the manly hearts were below in the cave, and brave Lucy and helpless Brenda were above in a strange place, whose dangers those below could only imagine.

===Chinese===
「可你就是够意思。」菲利普坚持道，「你本来可以——喂！」菲利普叫起来。吊桶又猛冲下来。他们屏住呼吸，等着看见露西的身影从空中坠落。可是没有，吊桶在半空晃荡了片刻，随即被匆匆提了上去，一声空空的金属撞击回荡在洞穴里。

「布伦达！」这一声是从沉稳克制的麦克斯心底里绞出来的。

「我的翅膀和爪子！」鹦鹉惊呼。

「哦，见鬼！」菲利普说。

这几声情绪爆发情有可原。头顶的白圆盘突然消失了。上头有人砰地把盖子关上了。所有男子汉的心都留在下头洞里，而勇敢的露西和没着没落的布伦达在上头一个陌生地方，那里的凶险，底下的人只能凭空想象。

===Original===
“I wish *I’d* gone,” said Philip. “Oh, I *wish* I’d gone.”

“Yes, indeed,” said Max, with a deep sigh.

“I feel a little faint,” said the parrot; “if someone would make a cup of cocoa.”

Thus did the excellent bird seek to occupy their minds in that first moment of disaster. And it was well that the captain and crew were thus saved from despair. For before the kettle boiled, the lid of the shaft opened about a foot and something largeish, roundish and lumpish fell heavily and bounced upon the deck of the *Lightning Loose*.

It was a pineapple, fresh, ripe and juicy. On its side was carved in large letters of uncertain shape the one word “**Wait**.”

===Chinese===
「要是*我*上去了就好了。」菲利普说，「哦，我*真希望*上去的是我。」

「是啊，可不是嘛。」麦克斯深深叹了口气。

「我觉得有点头晕。」鹦鹉说，「要是有人能去冲杯可可。」

大祸临头的头一刻，这只了不起的鸟就是这样想法子让大家有点事干。也好在船长和船员们因此没有陷进绝望。因为水壶还没烧开，竖井的盖子就开了一道缝，一个挺大的、圆圆的、沉甸甸的东西重重落下来，在「闪电自由号」的甲板上弹了两下。

那是一只菠萝，新鲜、熟透、汁水饱满。它一侧刻着一个形状歪歪扭扭的大字：「**等着**」。

===Original===
It was good advice and they took it. Really I do not see what else they could have done in any case. And they ate the pineapple. And presently everyone felt extremely sleepy.

“Waiting is one of those things that you can do as well asleep as awake, or even better,” said the parrot. “Forty winks will do us all the good in the world.” He put his head under his wing where he sat on the binnacle.

“May I turn in alongside you, sir?” Max asked. “I shan’t feel the dreadful loneliness so much then.”

So Philip and Max curled up together on the deck, warmly covered with the spare flags of all nations, and the forty winks lasted for the space of a good night’s rest﻿—about ten hours, in fact. So ten hours’ waiting was got through quite easily. But there was more waiting to do after they woke up, and that was not so easy.

---

===Chinese===
这是条好建议，他们照办了。说真的，我也看不出他们本来还能有什么别的办法。他们把菠萝吃了。不一会儿，人人都困得不行。

「等待这种事，睡着做和醒着做一样好，甚至更好。」鹦鹉说，「打个盹儿对咱们大家只有天大的好处。」它蹲在罗盘柜上，把头埋进了翅膀底下。

「我能挨着您睡吗，少爷？」麦克斯问，「那样我就没那么害怕可怕的孤单了。」

于是菲利普和麦克斯在甲板上蜷作一堆，暖暖和和地盖着备用的万国旗。这一盹打出了整整一夜好觉的长度——实际上约莫十个钟头。于是十个钟头的等待轻轻松松就熬过去了。可他们醒来之后还有得等，那可就不这么轻松了。

===Original===
When Lucy, sitting in the bucket with Brenda in her lap, felt the bucket lifted from the deck and swung loose in the air, it was as much as she could do to refrain from screaming. Brenda *did* scream, as you know, but Lucy stifled the sound in the folds of her frock.

Lucy bit her lips, made a great effort and called out that remark about the bucket-swing, just as though she were quite comfortable. It was very brave of her and helped her to go on being brave.

The bucket drew slowly up and up and up and passed from the silver dome into the dark shaft above. Lucy looked up. Yes, it was daylight that showed at the top of the shaft, and the rope was drawing her up towards it. Suppose the rope broke? Brenda was quite quiet now. She said afterwards that she must have fainted. And now the light was nearer and nearer. Now Lucy was in it, for the bucket had been drawn right up, and hands were reached out to draw it over the side of what seemed like a well. At that moment Lucy saw in a flash what might happen if the owners of the hands, in their surprise, let go the bucket and the windlass. She caught Brenda in her hands and threw the dog out on to the dry ground, and threw herself across the well parapet. Just in time, for a shout of surprise went up and the bucket went down, clanging against the well sides. The hands *had* let go.

Lucy clambered over the well side slowly, and when her feet stood on firm ground she saw that the hands were winding up the bucket again, and that it came very easily.

“Oh, don’t!” she said. “Let it go right down! There are some more people down there.”

===Chinese===
当露西坐在吊桶里、怀里抱着布伦达、感到吊桶被从甲板上提起、在半空中荡开的那一瞬，她费了好大的劲才没叫出声来。布伦达倒是*确实*叫了，这你们知道，可露西把那声音捂进了裙子的褶子里。

露西咬住嘴唇，狠命振作了一下，大声说出了那句「像荡秋千」的话，装得舒舒服服似的。这非常勇敢，也帮她继续勇敢下去。

吊桶慢慢悠悠地升啊、升啊、升啊，从银穹顶升进了上头黑洞洞的竖井。露西抬头看。没错，井口透亮的是天光，绳子正把她往那儿拉。万一绳子断了呢？布伦达这时安安静静。她后来说，自己准是吓晕了。光越来越近、越来越近。现在露西进到光里了，吊桶已经升到头，几只手伸过来，要把桶拉过一道像是井沿的边。就在那一刻，露西脑子里电光一闪：万一这些手的主人吃了一惊，撒开吊桶和绞盘，那可怎么好？她双手抄起布伦达，把狗扔到干地上，自己一下子扑过了井栏。恰恰来得及，因为一声惊叫响起来，吊桶坠了下去，哐哐地撞着井壁。那些手*果然*撒开了。

露西慢慢爬过井沿，双脚站上坚实的地面。她看见那几只手又在往上绞吊桶，绞得轻轻松松。

「哦，别！」她说，「让它一直落到底！下面还有几个人呢。」

===Original===
“Sorry, but it’s against the rules. The bucket only goes down this well forty times a day. And that was the fortieth time.”

They pulled the bucket in and banged down the lid of the well. Someone padlocked it and put the key in his pocket. And Lucy and he stood facing each other. He was a little round-headed man in a curious stiff red tunic, and there was something about the general shape of him and his tunic which reminded Lucy of something, only she could not remember what. Behind him stood two others, also red-tunicked and round-headed.

Brenda crouched at Lucy’s feet and whined softly, and Lucy waited for the strangers to speak.

“You shouldn’t do that,” said the red-tunicked man at last, “it was a great shock to us, your bobbing up as you did. It will keep us awake at night, just remembering it.”

“I’m sorry,” said Lucy.

===Chinese===
「抱歉，这是违反规矩的。这口井的吊桶一天只能下四十次。刚才那次正好是第四十次。」

他们把吊桶拉进来，砰地盖上井盖。有人上了挂锁，把钥匙揣进兜里。露西和他面对面站着。他是个圆脑袋的小个子男人，穿着一件样式古板的红色短褂，那身形和短褂的样子总让露西想起什么东西，只是想不起是什么。他身后还站着两个，也是红短褂、圆脑袋。

布伦达蹲在露西脚边，轻轻地呜咽，露西等着陌生人们先开口。

「你们不该那样。」穿红短褂的男人终于说话了，「你们就这么冷不丁冒上来，可把我们吓了一大跳。光是一想起来，夜里就该睡不着了。」

「对不起。」露西说。

===Original===
“You should always come into strange towns by the front gate,” said the man; “try to remember that, will you? Good night.”

“But you’re not going off like this,” said Lucy. “Let me write a note and drop it down to the others. Have you a bit of pencil, and paper?”

“No,” said the strange people, staring at her.

“Haven’t you anything I can write on?” Lucy asked them.

“There’s nothing here but pineapples,” said one of them at last.

===Chinese===
「进生地方，永远应该走正门。」那人说，「想法记住这一条，行吗？晚安。」

「可你们不能就这么走了呀。」露西说，「让我写张字条，扔下去给底下的人。你们有铅笔头吗？有纸吗？」

「没有。」陌生人们说着，直勾勾盯着她。

「你们有什么我能往上写字的东西吗？」露西问他们。

「这儿除了菠萝，什么也没有。」其中一个终于开口。

===Original===
So she cut a pineapple from among the hundreds that grew among the rocks near by, and carved “**Wait**” on it with her penknife.

“Now,” she said, “open that well lid.”

“It’s as much as our lives are worth,” said the leader.

“No it isn’t,” said Lucy; “there’s no law against dropping pineapples into the well. You know there isn’t. It isn’t like drawing water. And if you don’t I shall set my little dog at you. She is very fierce.”

Brenda was so flattered that she showed her teeth and growled.

===Chinese===
于是她从近旁石头缝里长着的成百个菠萝里割下一个，用小刀在上面刻了「**等着**」两个字。

「好了，」她说，「把井盖打开。」

「这可是要拿性命去换的。」领头的说。

「才不是呢。」露西说，「往井里扔菠萝又不犯法。你们自己知道不犯法。这又不是打水。你们要不开，我就放我的小狗咬你们。它凶得很。」

布伦达听了这般抬举，龇出牙，呜呜地低吼。

===Original===
“Oh, very well,” said the stranger; “anything to avoid fuss.”

When the well lid was padlocked down again, Lucy said:

“What country is this?” though she was almost sure, because of the pineapples, that it was Somnolentia. And when they had said that word she said:

“Now I’ll tell you something. The Deliverer is coming up that well next time you draw water. He is coming to deliver you from the bondage of the Great Sloth.”

“It is true,” said the red round-headed leader, “that we are in bondage. And the Great Sloth wearies us with the singing of choric songs when we long to be asleep. But none can deliver us. There is no hope. There is nothing good but sleep. And of that we have never enough.”

===Chinese===
「哦，行行好。」陌生人说，「怎么省事怎么来吧。」

等井盖重新上了挂锁，露西说：

「这是什么地方？」话虽这么问，看这些菠萝，她十有八九笃定是索姆诺伦蒂亚。等他们说出那个词，她便说：

「现在我告诉你们一件事。下次你们打水的时候，解救者就要从那口井里上来。他是来把你们从大树懒的奴役里解救出来的。」

「我们确实受着奴役。」红衣圆脑袋的领头人说，「我们困得只想睡，大树懒却拿合唱颂歌来磨我们。可谁也解救不了我们。没有指望。天下除了睡觉没有好事。而觉我们从来睡不够。」

===Original===
“Oh, dear,” said Lucy despairingly, “aren’t there any women here? They always have more sense than men.”

“What you say is rude as well as untrue,” said the red leader; “but to avoid fuss we will lead you and your fierce dog to the huts of the women. And then perhaps you will allow us to go to sleep.”

The huts were poor and mean, little fenced-in corners in the ruins of what had once been a great and beautiful city, with gardens and streams; but now the streams were dry and nothing grew in the gardens but weeds and pineapples.

But the women﻿—who all wore green tunics of the same stiff shape as the men’s﻿—were not quite so sleepy as their husbands. They brought Lucy fresh pineapples to eat, and were dreamily interested in the cut of her clothes and the begging accomplishments of Brenda. And from the women she learned several things about the Somnolentians. They all wore the same shaped tunics, only the colours differed. The women’s were green, the drawers of water wore red, the attendants of the Great Sloth wore black, and the pineapple gatherers wore yellow.

And as Lucy sat at the door of the hut and watched the people in these four colours going lazily about among the ruins she suddenly knew what they were, and she exclaimed:

===Chinese===
「哦，天哪。」露西绝望地说，「这儿就没有女人吗？女人总比男人明白事理。」

「你这话说得又失礼又不实。」红衣领头人说，「不过图个省事，我们带你和你那只凶狗去女人的棚屋吧。然后，兴许你就肯让我们去睡觉了。」

那些棚屋又破又寒碜，是在一座曾经宏大美丽的城市的废墟里圈出来的小角落——这里原有过花园和流水；可如今溪流干涸，园子里除了荒草和菠萝，什么都不长。

不过女人们——她们穿的绿短褂和男人们的一样古板——倒不象她们的丈夫那么困。她们给露西端来新鲜菠萝吃，朦朦胧胧地对她衣服的剪裁和布伦达作揖讨食的本领大感兴趣。从女人们那里，她打听出好几件关于索姆诺伦蒂亚人的事。大家都穿一个式样的短褂，只是颜色不同：女人穿绿的，打水人穿红的，伺候大树懒的穿黑的，采菠萝的穿黄的。

露西坐在棚屋门口，望着这四种颜色的人懒洋洋地在废墟间来来去去，忽然明白他们是什么人了，不由得喊出声来：

===Original===
“I know what you are; you’re Halma men.”

Instantly every man within earshot made haste to get away, and the women whispered, “Hush! It is death to breathe that name.”

“But why?” Lucy asked.

“Halma was the great captain of our race,” said the woman, “and the Great Sloth fears that if we hear his name it will rouse us and we shall break from bondage and become once more a free people.”

Lucy determined that they should hear that name pretty often; but before she could speak it again the woman sighed, and remarking “The Great Sloth sleeps,” fell asleep then and there over the pineapple she was peeling. A vast silence settled on the city, and next moment Lucy also slept. She slept for hours.

===Chinese===
「我知道你们是什么了；你们是哈尔玛人。」

霎时间，凡是听得见的男人都赶紧躲开了，女人们悄声说：「嘘！说出那个名字就得死。」

「可为什么呀？」露西问。

「哈尔玛是我们种族的伟大统帅。」那女人说，「大树懒害怕我们听见他的名字就会振作起来，挣脱奴役，重新变成自由的民族。」

露西打定了主意，要让他们把这个名字听得勤一些；可她还没来得及再说一遍，那女人已经叹了口气，说了句「大树懒睡了」，就在她正削着的菠萝上当场睡着了。一片浩大的寂静罩住了全城，下一刻露西也睡着了。她一睡就是几个钟头。

===Original===
It took her some time to find the keeper of the padlock key, and when she had found him he refused to use it. Nothing would move him, not even the threat of the fierceness of Brenda.

At last, almost in despair, Lucy suddenly remembered a word of power.

“I command you to open the well and let down the bucket,” she said. “I command you by the great name of Halma.”

“It is death to speak that name,” said the keeper of the key, looking over his shoulder anxiously.

“It is life to speak that name,” said Lucy. “Halma! Halma! Halma! If you don’t open that well I’ll carve the name on a pineapple and send it in on the golden tray with the Great Sloth’s dinner.”

===Chinese===
她费了不少工夫才找到管挂锁钥匙的人，找到了，那人却死活不肯用。什么都动摇不了他，连布伦达有多凶的威胁也不管用。

最后，几乎要绝望了，露西忽然想起了一个有法力的词。

「我命令你打开井，放下吊桶。」她说，「我以哈尔玛的伟大名字命令你。」

「说出那个名字就得死。」管钥匙的人说，不安地回头张望。

「说出那个名字才有活路。」露西说，「哈尔玛！哈尔玛！哈尔玛！你不打开井，我就把这名字刻在菠萝上，随着大树懒的晚饭一起用金托盘送进去。」

===Original===
“It would have the lives of hundreds for that,” said the keeper in horror.

“Open the well then,” said Lucy.

They all held a council as soon as Philip and Max had been safely drawn up in the bucket, and Lucy told them all she knew.

“I think whatever we do we ought to be quick,” said Lucy; “that Great Sloth is dangerous. I’m sure it is. It’s sent already to say I am to be brought to its presence to sing songs to it while it goes to sleep. It doesn’t mind me because it knows I’m not the Deliverer. And if you’ll let me, I believe I can work everything all right. But if it knows you’re here, it’ll be much harder.”

The degraded Halma men were watching them from a distance, in whispering groups.

===Chinese===
「那可要拿几百条命去抵的。」管钥匙的人惊恐地说。

「那就开井吧。」露西说。

等菲利普和麦克斯顺顺当当坐吊桶上来之后，大家立刻开了个会，露西把她知道的一股脑儿讲了。

「我看不管干什么，都得快。」露西说，「那大树懒危险。我敢肯定。它已经派人来传话，说要把我带到它跟前，在它入睡前唱歌给它听。它不怕我，因为它知道我不是解救者。要是你们肯让我去，我相信我能把一切都办妥。可它要是知道你们在这儿，事情就难办多了。」

那些堕落的哈尔玛人远远地望着他们，三五成群，交头接耳。

===Original===
“I shall go and sing to the Great Sloth,” she said, “and you must go about and say the name of power to everyone you meet, and tell them you’re the Deliverer. Then if my idea doesn’t come off, we must overpower the Great Sloth by numbers and.﻿ ﻿… You just go about saying ‘Halma!’﻿—see?”

“While you do the dangerous part? Likely!” said Philip.

“It’s not dangerous. It never hurts the people who sing﻿—never,” said Lucy. “Now I’m going.”

And she went before Philip could stop her.

“Let her go,” said the parrot; “she is a wise child.”

===Chinese===
「我去给大树懒唱歌。」她说，「你们呢，得到处走动，见着谁就对谁念那个有法力的名字，告诉他们你就是解救者。这样，万一我的主意不成，咱们还能靠人多制服大树懒，还有……你们就到处去说『哈尔玛！』——明白吗？」

「让你去干危险的那份？想得美！」菲利普说。

「不危险。它从来不伤害唱歌的人——从来不。」露西说，「我这就去了。」

菲利普还没来得及拦，她已经走了。

「让她去吧。」鹦鹉说，「她是个有主见的孩子。」

===Original===
The temple of the Great Sloth was built of solid gold. It had beautiful pillars and doorways and windows and courts, one inside the other, each paved with gold flagstones. And in the very middle of everything was a large room which was entirely featherbed. There the Great Sloth passed its useless life in eating, sleeping and listening to music.

Outside the moorish arch that led to this inner room Lucy stopped and began to sing. She had a clear little voice and she sang “Jockey to the Fair,” and “Early one morning,” and then she stopped.

And a great sleepy slobbery voice came out from the room and said:

“Your songs are in very bad taste. Do you know no sleepy songs?”

“Your people sing you sleepy songs,” said Lucy. “What a pity they can’t sing to you all the time.”

===Chinese===
大树懒的庙是纯金造的。它有美丽的柱子、门廊、窗户和庭院，一层套一层，每一层都铺着金板。而正中央是一间大屋子，整间屋子全是羽绒床。大树懒就在那儿吃着、睡着、听着音乐，把它那百无一用的一生打发过去。

在通向这间内室的摩尔式拱门外，露西停下脚步，唱起歌来。她有一副清亮的小嗓子，先唱了《骑师赶集》（Jockey to the Fair），又唱了《一天清晨》（Early One Morning），然后就停住了。

屋里传出一个又困倦又黏糊的大嗓门，说：

「你这些歌品位很差。你就不会唱点催眠的歌吗？」

「你的子民唱给你的就是催眠的歌呀。」露西说，「可惜他们不能一天到晚给你唱。」

===Original===
“You have a sympathetic nature,” said the Great Sloth, and it came out and leaned on the pillar of its door and looked at her with sleepy interest. It was enormous, as big as a young elephant, and it walked on its hind legs like a gorilla. It was very black indeed.

“It *is* a pity,” it said; “but they say they cannot live without drinking, so they waste their time in drawing water from the wells.”

“Wouldn’t it be nice,” said Lucy, “if you had a machine for drawing water. Then they could sing to you all day﻿—if they chose.”

“If *I* chose,” said the Great Sloth, yawning like a hippopotamus. “I am sleepy. Go!”

“No,” said Lucy, and it was so long since the Great Sloth had heard that word that the shock of the sound almost killed its sleepiness.

===Chinese===
「你倒是个善解人意的性子。」大树懒说着走了出来，倚在门柱上，带着困倦的兴味打量她。它大极了，有一头小象那么大，像大猩猩一样用后腿走路。它还真是黑得很。

「*确实*可惜。」它说，「可他们说，不喝水活不成，所以只好把工夫浪费在井里打水上。」

「要是有一台抽水的机器，岂不美哉？」露西说，「那他们就能整天给你唱歌了——只要他们乐意。」

「只要*我*乐意。」大树懒说，打着河马般的大哈欠，「我困了。去吧！」

「不。」露西说。大树懒太久太久没听过这个字了，这一声的冲击差点儿要了它困意的命。

===Original===
“*What* did you say?” it asked, as if it could not believe its large ears.

“I said ‘No,’ ” said Lucy. “I mean that you are so great and grand you have only to wish for anything and you get it.”

“Is that so?” said the Great Sloth dreamily and like an American.

“Yes,” said Lucy with firmness. “You just say, ‘I wish I had a machine to draw up water for eight hours a day.’ That’s the proper length for a working day. Father says so.”

“Say it all again, and slower,” said the creature. “I didn’t quite catch what you said.”

===Chinese===
「你说的*什么*？」它问，好像没法相信自己那对大耳朵。

「我说『不』。」露西说，「我的意思是，您这么伟大、这么尊贵，想要什么，只要许个愿就到手了。」

「是这么回事吗？」大树懒梦悠悠地说，一副美国腔。

「是的。」露西斩钉截铁地说，「您只要说：『我希望有一台机器，一天替我抽八个钟头的水。』一个工作日就该这么长。爸爸说的。」

「再全说一遍，说慢点。」那家伙说，「我没太听清你说什么。」

===Original===
Lucy repeated the words.

“If that’s all.﻿ ﻿…” said the Great Sloth; “now say it again, very slowly indeed.”

Lucy did so and the Great Sloth repeated after her:

“I wish I had a machine to draw up water for eight hours a day.”

“Don’t,” it said angrily, looking back over its shoulder into the featherbedded room, “don’t, I say. Where are you shoving to? Who are you? What are you doing in my room? Come out of it.”

===Chinese===
露西把那句话又说了一遍。

「要是就这么点事……」大树懒说，「那现在再说一遍，要非常非常慢。」

露西照办了，大树懒跟着她复述：

「我希望有一台机器，一天替我抽八个钟头的水。」

「别挤。」它怒气冲冲地说，回头望进那间羽绒床铺成的屋子，「我说，别挤。你往哪儿蹭？你是谁？在我屋里干什么？出去。」

===Original===
Something did come out of the room, pushing the Great Sloth away from the door. And what came out was the vast featherbed in enormous rolls and swellings and bulges. It was being pushed out by something so big and strong that it was stronger that the Great Sloth itself, and pushed that mountain of lazy sloth-flesh half across its own inner courtyard. Lucy retreated before its advancing bulk and its extreme rage.

“Push me out of my own featherbedroom, would it?” said the Sloth, now hardly sleepy at all. “You wait till I get hold of it, whatever it is.”

The whole of the featherbed was out in the courtyard now, and the Great Sloth climbed slowly back over it into its room to find out who had dared to outrage its Slothful Majesty.

Lucy waited, breathless with hope and fear, as the Great Sloth blundered back into the inner room of its temple. It did not come out again. There was a silence, and then a creaking sound and the voice of the Great Sloth saying:

“No, no, no, I won’t. Let go, I tell you.” Then more sounds of creaking and the sound of metal on metal.

===Chinese===
果然有东西从屋里出来了，把大树懒从门口挤开了。出来的是那张巨大的羽绒床，鼓成一团团、一坨坨的大包。推它出来的是个又大又壮的东西，比大树懒自己还壮，硬是把那座懒肉堆成的大山，推得横穿过了它自家的内院。露西在它逼近的庞大身躯和冲天怒气面前节节后退。

「把我从我自己个儿的羽绒床屋里挤出去，它想？」大树懒说，这会儿困意几乎全没了，「等我逮住它，管它是什么东西。」

现在整张羽绒床都到了院子里，大树懒慢慢悠悠爬过床，回到屋里，去看看是谁胆敢冒犯它这尊懒大王。

大树懒跌跌撞撞回了庙里的内室。露西在外头等着，又叫希望憋着气，又叫恐惧憋着气。它没有再出来。先是一阵寂静，接着传来吱吱嘎嘎的响动，还有大树懒的声音在说：

「不，不，不，我不干。放开我，听见没有。」接着又是更多的吱嘎声，还有金属磕金属的声响。

===Original===
She crept to the arch and peeped round it.

The room that had been full of featherbed was now full of wheels and cogs and bands and screws and bars. It was full, in fact, of a large and complicated machine. And the handle of that machine was being turned by the Great Sloth itself.

“Let me go,” said the Great Sloth, gnashing its great teeth. “I won’t work!”

“You must,” said a purring voice from the heart of the machinery. “You wished for me, and now you have to work me eight hours a day. It is the law”; it was the machine itself which spoke.

“I’ll break you,” said the Sloth.

===Chinese===
她蹑手蹑脚挪到拱门边，探头张望。

那间原本堆满羽绒床的屋子，如今挤满了轮子、齿轮、皮带、螺丝和铁条。实际上，它被一台巨大而复杂的机器塞满了。而那台机器的摇柄，正由大树懒亲自动手摇着。

「放开我。」大树懒咬着它的大牙说，「我不干活！」

「您必须干。」一个咕噜咕噜的声音从机器心窝里传出来，「是您许愿要我来的，现在您就得一天摇我八个钟头。这是规矩。」说话的正是机器本身。

「我要砸碎你。」大树懒说。

===Original===
“I am unbreakable,” said the machine with gentle pride.

“This is your doing,” said the Sloth, turning its furious eyes on Lucy in the doorway. “You wait till I catch you!” And all the while it had to go on turning that handle.

“Thank you,” said Lucy politely; “I think I will not wait. And I shall have eight hours’ start,” she added.

Even as she spoke a stream of clear water began to run from the pumping machine. It slid down the gold steps and across the golden court. Lucy ran out into the ruined square of the city shouting:

“Halma! Halma! Halma! To me, Halma’s men!”

===Chinese===
「我是砸不碎的。」机器带着温和的骄傲说。

「这是你干的好事。」大树懒说着，把狂怒的眼睛转向门口的露西，「等我逮住你的！」可它还得一直一直摇那根摇柄。

「谢谢您。」露西彬彬有礼地说，「我想我就不等了。而且我能领先您八个钟头。」她补了一句。

就在她说话的当口，一股清水从抽水机里流了出来。水淌下金台阶，漫过金院子。露西跑出去，站在城里的废墟广场上高喊：

「哈尔玛！哈尔玛！哈尔玛！哈尔玛的子民，到我这边来！」

===Original===
And the men, already excited by Philip, who had gone about saying that name of power without a moment’s pause all the time Lucy had been in the golden temple, gathered round her in a crowd.

“Quick!” she said; “the Great Sloth is pumping water up for you. He will pump for eight hours a day. Quick! dig a channel for the water to run in. The Deliverer,” she pointed to Philip, “has given you back your river.”

Some ran to look out old rusty half-forgotten spades and picks. But others hesitated and said:

“The Great Sloth will work for eight hours, and then it will be free to work vengeance on us.”

“I will go back,” said Lucy, “and explain to it that if it does not behave nicely you will all wish for machine guns, and it knows now that if people wish for machinery they have to use it. It will be awake now for eight hours and if you all work for eight hours a day you’ll soon have your city as fine as ever. And there’s one new law. Every time the clock strikes you must all say ‘Halma!’ aloud, every one of you, to remind yourselves of your great destiny, and that you are no longer slaves of the Great Sloth.”

===Chinese===
而那些男人——露西待在金庙里的整个工夫，菲利普一直在四处奔走，一刻不停地念那个有法力的名字，早把他们煽动起来了——此刻成群围到了她身边。

「快！」她说，「大树懒正在给你们抽水。它一天要抽八个钟头。快！挖一条水道让水流。解救者，」她指着菲利普，「把你们的河还给你们了。」

有的人跑去翻找那些生了锈、半被遗忘的旧铁锹和镐头。可也有人犹犹豫豫，说：

「大树懒干八个钟头的活，回过头来就腾出工夫找咱们报仇了。」

「我回去。」露西说，「跟它讲清楚，它要是不好好干，你们就一起许愿要机关枪。它现在已经知道了，人们许愿要机器，就非用不可。它现在要醒八个钟头，你们要是也都一天干八个钟头，你们的城很快就会跟从前一样体面。还有一条新法令：时钟每敲一下，你们所有人都要大声说一声『哈尔玛！』，一个不落，好提醒自己记住你们伟大的天命，记住你们再也不是大树懒的奴隶。」

===Original===
She went back and explained machine guns very carefully to the now hardworking Sloth. When she came back all the men were at work digging a channel for the new river.

The women and children crowded round Lucy and Philip.

“Ah!” said the oldest woman of all, “now we shall be able to wash in water. I’ve heard my grandmother say water was very pleasant to wash in. I never thought I should live to wash in water myself.”

“Why?” Lucy asked. “What do you wash in?”

===Chinese===
她回去，把机关枪的事仔仔细细跟那位如今勤勤恳恳的大树懒讲了一遍。等她回来，所有的男人都在挖新河的水道了。

女人和孩子们把露西和菲利普围了个严实。

「啊呀！」年纪最老的老太太说，「这下我们能用水洗东西了。听我奶奶说过，用水洗东西可舒服了。我从没想到自己能活着用上水洗。」

「为什么？」露西问，「你们平时用什么洗？」

===Original===
“Pineapple juice,” said a dozen voices, “when we *do* wash!”

“But that must be very sticky,” said Lucy.

“It is,” said the oldest woman of all; “very!”

===Chinese===
「菠萝汁。」十几个声音齐声说，「在我们*当真*洗的时候！」

「那不是黏糊糊的吗？」露西说。

「是啊。」年纪最老的老太太说，「黏得很！」

## XI The Night Attack / 夜袭

===Original===
The Halma men were not naturally lazy. They were, in the days before the coming of the Great Sloth, a most energetic and industrious people. Now that the Sloth was obliged to work eight hours a day, the weight of its constant and catching sleepiness was taken away, and the people set to work in good earnest. (I did explain, didn’t I, that the Great Sloth’s sleepiness really was catching, like measles?)

So now the Halma men were as busy as ants. Some dug the channel for the new stream, some set to work to restore the buildings, while others weeded the overgrown gardens and ploughed the deserted fields. The head Halma man painted in large letters on a column in the marketplace these words:

“This city is now called by its ancient name of Briskford. Any citizen found calling it Somnolentia will not be allowed to wash in water for a week.”

The headman was full of schemes, the least of which was the lighting of the town by electricity, the power to be supplied by the Great Sloth.

“He can’t go on pumping eight hours a day,” said the headman; “I can easily adjust the machine to all sorts of other uses.”

In the evening a banquet was (of course) given to the Deliverers. The banquet was all pineapple and water, because there had been no time to make or get anything else. But the speeches were very flattering; and Philip and Lucy were very pleased, more so than Brenda, who did not like pineapple and made but little effort to conceal her disappointment. Max accepted bits of pineapple, out of politeness, and hid them among the feet of the guests so that nobody’s feelings should be hurt.

===Chinese===
哈尔玛人天生并不懒。在大树懒来到之前的日子，他们本是个最精力充沛、最勤勤恳恳的民族。如今大树懒不得不一天干八个钟头的活，它那无时不在、又有传染性的瞌睡便卸除了，人们认认真真地干起活来。（我解释过大树懒的瞌睡真的会传染，就像麻疹一样，对吧？）

于是现在哈尔玛人忙得像蚂蚁。有的挖新溪的水道，有的动手修缮房屋，还有的给荒芜的花园除草、耕种撂荒的田地。哈尔玛人头领在集市的一根柱子上用大字写下了这些话：

「本城自即日起恢复古名布里斯克福德。凡发现有市民仍称其为索姆诺伦蒂亚者，一星期内不得用水洗漱。」

头领满脑子都是宏图大计，其中最小的一项，是用电力给全城照明，动力由大树懒提供。

「他总不能一天到晚只抽八个钟头的水，」头领说，「这台机器要改作别的用场，我轻而易举。」

晚上，（不用说）为解救者们举办了一场宴会。宴会全是菠萝和白水，因为来不及置办别的。但致辞句句动听；菲利普和露西都非常高兴——布伦达可高兴不起来，她不爱吃菠萝，也懒得掩饰失望。麦克斯出于礼貌接过一块块菠萝，悄悄藏在客人们的脚边，免得伤任何人的感情。

===Original===
“I don’t know how we’re to get back to the island,” said Philip next day, “now we’ve lost the *Lightning Loose*.”
